===Original===
To 

 Leslie Faber 

 (“The Ringer”)

===Chinese===
献给

莱斯利·法伯

（“敲诈者”）

## Foreword / 序言

===Original===
Rightly speaking, it is improper, not to say illegal, for those sadly privileged few who go in and out of Broadmoor Criminal Lunatic Asylum, to have pointed out to them any particular character, however notorious he may have been or to what heights of public interest his infamy had carried him, before the testifying doctors and a merciful jury consigned him to this place without hope. But often had John Flack been pointed out as he shuffled about the grounds, his hands behind him, his chin on his breast, a tall, lean old man in an ill-fitting suit of drab clothing, who spoke to nobody and was spoken to by few.

“That is Flack﻿—**The** Flack﻿—the cleverest crook in the world.﻿ ﻿… Crazy John Flack﻿ ﻿… nine murders﻿ ﻿…”

In their queer, sane moments, men who were in Broadmoor for isolated homicides were rather proud of Old John. The officers who locked him up at night and watched him as he slept had little to say against him, because he gave no trouble, and through all the six years of his incarceration had never once been seized of those frenzies which so often end in the hospital for some poor innocent devil, and a rubber-padded cell for the frantic author of misfortune.

===Chinese===
严格说来，那些可悲地享有特权、得以进出布罗德莫尔刑事疯人院的少数人，是不该有人把任何特定人物指给他们看的——无论此人在作证的医生和仁慈的陪审团将他交付这个绝无希望之地以前如何声名狼藉，也无论他的恶名曾把公众的兴趣推到何种高度；这种指认不合规矩，甚至可以说违法。然而约翰·弗拉克却屡屡被人指认：他拖着脚步在院子里走动，双手背在身后，下巴抵着胸口，是个瘦高的老头，穿一身不合身的土褐色衣裳，不跟任何人说话，也难得有人同他讲话。

“那就是弗拉克——**那个**弗拉克——天底下最精明的歹徒。……疯子约翰·弗拉克……九桩命案……”

那些因单起凶杀案而关在布罗德莫尔的人，在神志清醒的古怪时刻，倒颇为老约翰感到自豪。夜里把他锁进牢房、看着他入睡的看守们几乎说不出他的坏话，因为他从不惹是生非；在被关押的整整六年里，他一次也不曾发作那种狂乱——那种狂乱往往以某个可怜的无辜家伙进医院、以闯祸的狂人进软垫牢房收场。

===Original===
He spent most of his time writing and reading, for he was something of a genius with his pen, and wrote with extraordinary rapidity. He filled hundreds of little exercise books with his great treatise on crime. The governor humoured him; allowed him to retain the books, expecting in due course to add them to his already interesting museum.

Once, as a great concession, Old Jack gave him a book to read, and the governor read and gasped. It was entitled *Method of Robbing a Bank Vault When Only Two Guards Are Employed*. The governor, who had been a soldier, read and read, stopping now and then to rub his head; for this document, written in the neat, legible hand of John Flack, was curiously reminiscent of a divisional order for attack. No detail was too small to be noted; every contingency was provided for. Not only were the constituents of the drug to be employed to “settle the outer watchman” given, but there was an explanatory note which may be quoted:

> “If this drug is not procurable, I advise that the operator should call upon a suburban doctor and describe the following symptoms.﻿ ﻿… The doctor will then prescribe the drug in a minute quantity. Six bottles of this medicine should be procured and the following method adopted to extract the drug.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
他把大部分时间用来写作和阅读，因为他动笔颇有几分天才，写起来快得惊人。他用几百本小练习簿写满了他的犯罪学鸿篇巨制。典狱长迁就他，允许他把这些簿子留在手边，只等着日后把它们添进他那已经颇有趣味的博物馆去。

有一回，老杰克格外开恩，给了他一册读，典狱长读了，倒抽一口冷气。那册子的题名是《只雇两名看守时如何抢劫银行金库》。当过兵的典狱长读了又读，不时停下来揉揉脑袋；因为这份以约翰·弗拉克工整易认的笔迹写成的文书，出奇地叫人想起一份师部进攻命令。再小的细节也无不载明；各种意外情况全都预先料到了。不仅写明了用来“打发外围看守”的药物的成分，还附了一条说明，不妨照录如下：

> “如果弄不到这种药，我建议动手的人去拜访一位郊区医生，描述下列症状。……医生随后便会开出微量的此种药物。应购得此药六瓶，并采用下述方法提取药物。……”

===Original===
“Have you written much like this, Flack?” asked the wondering officer.

“This?” John Flack shrugged his lean shoulders. “I am doing this for amusement, just to test my memory. I have already written sixty-three books on the subject, and those works are beyond improvement. During the six years I have been here, I have not been able to think of a single improvement on my old system.”

Was he jesting? Was this a flight of a disordered mind? The governor, used as he was to his patients and their peculiar ways, was not certain.

===Chinese===
“你写过许多这种东西吗，弗拉克？”满脸诧异的典狱长问道。

“这个？”约翰·弗拉克耸了耸瘦削的肩膀。“我写这个不过为了消遣，只想考考自己的记性。关于这个题目我已经写了六十三册，那些著作已臻完善，无可改进。在我待在此地的六年里，我想不出对我的老办法能有丝毫改进。”

他是在说笑吗？这是一个错乱头脑的狂想吗？典狱长尽管对他的病人及其种种怪癖早已见惯，却也拿不准。

===Original===
“You mean you have written an encyclopaedia of crime?” he asked incredulously. “Where is it to be found?”

Old Flack’s thin lips curled in a disdainful smile, but he made no answer.

Sixty-three handwritten volumes represented the life work of John Flack. It was the one achievement upon which he prided himself.

On another occasion, when the governor referred to his extraordinary literary labours, he said: “I have put a huge fortune in the hands of any clever man﻿—providing, of course,” he mused, “that he is a man of resolution and the books fall into his hands at a very early date. In these days of scientific discovery, what is a novelty today is a commonplace tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
“你是说你写了一部犯罪百科全书？”他满腹狐疑地问。“它在哪里能找到？”

老弗拉克薄薄的嘴唇撇出一丝轻蔑的笑意，却不作答。

六十三册手写卷帙是约翰·弗拉克的毕生事业。那是他唯一引以为傲的成就。

还有一次，典狱长提到他那异乎寻常的笔墨辛劳，他说：“我已经把一笔巨额财富放进任何一个聪明人的手心——当然，”他沉吟道，“条件是他是个有决断的人，而且这些册子要很早便落到他手里。在如今这个科学发现的时代，今天的新鲜事物，明天就成了家常便饭。”

===Original===
The governor had his doubts as to the existence of these deplorable volumes, but very soon after the conversation took place he had to revise his judgment. Scotland Yard, which seldom if ever chases chimeras, sent down one Chief Inspector Simpson, who was a man entirely without imagination and had been promoted for it.

His interview with Crazy John Flack was a brief one. “About these books of yours, Jack,” he said. “It would be terrible if they fell into wrong hands. Ravini says you’ve got a hundred volumes hidden somewhere.”

“Ravini?” Old John Flack showed his teeth. “Listen, Simpson! You don’t think you’re going to keep me in this awful place all my life, do you? If you do, you’ve got another guess coming. I’ll skip one of these odd nights﻿—you can tell the governor if you like﻿—and then Ravini and I are going to have a little talk.”

His voice grew high and shrill. The old mad glitter that Simpson had seen before came back to his eyes.

===Chinese===
典狱长对这些可叹的卷册是否当真存在心存疑虑，可是那次谈话之后不久，他便不得不修正自己的判断。苏格兰场向来几乎从不追捕子虚乌有之物，这回却派下来一位辛普森总督察——此人全无想象力，而且正因如此才得了提升。

他同疯子约翰·弗拉克的会面十分简短。“谈谈你那些册子的事，杰克，”他说，“它们若是落到坏人手里，那可就不得了。拉维尼说你把一百册藏在什么地方了。”

“拉维尼？”老约翰·弗拉克龇出了牙齿。“听着，辛普森！你总不至于以为，你们要把我关在这个可怕的地方一辈子吧？你要真这么想，那就大错特错了。这些日子里随便哪个晚上我都会溜走——你高兴的话尽管告诉典狱长——到那时，我和拉维尼就要小小地谈一谈了。”

他的声音变得又高又尖。辛普森从前见过的那种疯狂的凶光，又回到了他眼里。

===Original===
“Do you ever have daydreams, Simpson? I have three! I’ve got a new method of getting away with a million: that’s one, but it’s not important. Another one is Reeder: you can tell J. G. what I say. It’s a dream of meeting him alone one nice, dark, foggy night, when the police can’t tell which way the screams are coming. And the third is Ravini: George Ravini’s got one chance, and that is for him to die before I get out!”

“You’re mad,” said Simpson.

“That’s what I’m here for,” said John Flack truthfully.

This conversation with Simpson and that with the governor were two of the longest he ever had, all the six years he was in Broadmoor. Mostly when he wasn’t writing he strolled about the grounds, his chin on his chest, his hands clasped behind him. Occasionally, he reached a certain place near the high wall, and it is said that he threw letters over, though this is very unlikely. What is more possible is that he found a messenger who carried his many and cryptic letters to the outer world and brought in exchange monosyllabic replies. He was very friendly with the officer in charge of his ward, and one early morning this man was discovered with his throat cut. The ward door was open, and John Flack had gone out into the world to realize his daydreams.

===Chinese===
“你做白日梦吗，辛普森？我有三个！我有一个弄到一百万的新法子：那是一个，不过不要紧。另一个是里德：你可以把我的话转告 J. G.。那是在一个又黑又浓的雾夜里同他单独见面的梦，那种夜里，警察分不清惨叫声是从哪个方向传来的。第三个是拉维尼：乔治·拉维尼只有一个机会，那就是赶在我出去之前死掉！”

“你疯了。”辛普森说。

“我就是为这个才进来的。”约翰·弗拉克说得实实在在。

这次同辛普森的谈话和先前同典狱长的谈话，是他在布罗德莫尔整整六年里时间最长的两次交谈。不写作的时候，他多半在院子里踱步，下巴抵着胸口，双手交叠在背后。偶尔他会走到高墙近旁的某个去处，据说他曾把信扔出墙外，不过这很不可信。更可能的是，他找到了一个信使，替他把许多晦涩难解的信送到外面的世界，又带回一两个字的回音。他同负责他病区的看守交情很好，一天清晨，这人被人发现割断了喉咙。病区的门敞开着，约翰·弗拉克已经走进外面的世界，去实现他的白日梦了。

## I / 一



===Original===
There were two subjects which irritated the mind of Margaret Belman as the Southern Express carried her toward Selford Junction and the branch line train which crawled from the junction to Siltbury. The first of these was, not unnaturally, the drastic changes she now contemplated, the second the effect they already had had upon Mr. J. G. Reeder, that mild and middle-aged man.
When she had announced that she was seeking a post in the country, he might at least have shown some evidence of regret; a certain glumness would have been appropriate, at any rate. Instead, he had brightened visibly at the prospect.
“I am afraid I shan’t be able to come to London very often,” she had said.
“That is good news,” said Mr. Reeder, and added some banality about the value of periodical changes of air and the beauty of getting near to nature.
In fact, he had been more cheerful than he had been for a week﻿—which was rather exasperating.

===Chinese===
南方快车（Southern Express）载着玛格丽特·贝尔曼（Margaret Belman）驶向塞尔福德枢纽站（Selford Junction），从那里，一班支线列车再慢吞吞地爬向西尔特伯里（Siltbury）。旅途之中，有两桩心事搅得她心烦意乱。第一桩，说来也不奇怪，是她眼下正盘算的这番剧变；第二桩，则是这番剧变已经给里德先生（Mr. J. G. Reeder）——那位性情温和的中年男子——带来的效应。
当初她宣布自己打算去乡下谋个职位时，他至少也该显出几分惋惜才是；退一步说，摆出一副郁郁寡欢的样子，总归是合宜的。谁知一听说这事，他反倒明显地喜形于色。
“恐怕我不能常常来伦敦了。”她当时说。
“那是好消息。”里德先生说，接着又添了几句老生常谈，说定时换换空气何其有益，贴近大自然又是何等美妙。
事实上，他那一阵的高兴劲儿胜过了一星期以来的任何时候——这实在叫人气恼。

===Original===
Margaret Belman’s pretty face puckered as she recalled her disappointment and chagrin. All thoughts of dropping this application of hers disappeared. Not that she imagined for one moment that a six-hundred-a-year secretaryship was going to drop into her lap for the mere asking. She was wholly unsuited to the job; she had had no experience in hotel work; and the chances of her being accepted were remote.
As to the Italian who had made so many attempts to make her acquaintance﻿—he was one of the unpleasant commonplaces so familiar to a girl who worked for her living that in ordinary circumstances she would not have given him a second thought.
But that morning he had followed her to the station, and she was certain that he had heard her tell the girl who came with her that she was returning by the 6:15. A policeman would deal effectively with him﻿—if she cared to risk the publicity. But a girl, however annoyed, shrinks from such an ordeal; she must deal with him in her own way.
That was not a happy prospect, and the two matters in combination were sufficient to spoil what otherwise might have been a very happy or interesting afternoon.

===Chinese===
一想起自己的失望与懊恼，玛格丽特·贝尔曼那张俏丽的脸便皱了起来。她原本动过撤回这份申请的念头，如今这念头消失得无影无踪。倒不是说她一刻妄想这份年薪六百的秘书差事会开口一讨便落到自己怀里。她全然不适合这份工作；旅馆行当的事她毫无经验；获聘的希望更是微乎其微。
至于那个三番五次想结识她的意大利人——自食其力的姑娘对这类讨厌的寻常纠缠早已司空见惯，若在平常光景，她根本不会多想他一下。
可是那天早晨，他一路跟到了车站，而且她敢肯定，他听见她对同来的女友说，她要乘 6 点 15 分的车回来。警察自能整治得了他——只要她甘冒闹得沸沸扬扬的风险。然而一个姑娘家，无论多气恼，也不愿去受那种折腾；她得用自己的法子来对付他。
这前景可不美妙，两桩心事凑在一处，足以把一个原本可能非常快活、非常有味的下午糟蹋殆尽。

===Original===
As to Mr. Reeder﻿—
Margaret Belman frowned. She was twenty-three, an age when youngish men are rather tiresome. On the other hand, men in the region of fifty are not especially attractive. She loathed Mr. Reeder’s side whiskers; they made him look rather like a Scottish butler. Of course, he was a dear.﻿ ﻿…
Here the train reached the junction and she found herself at the surprisingly small station of Siltbury before she had quite made up her mind whether she was in love with Mr. Reeder or merely annoyed with him.

===Chinese===
至于里德先生——
玛格丽特·贝尔曼皱起了眉头。她二十三岁，正是在这个年纪，年轻男人显得相当烦人；可话又说回来，五十上下的男人也说不上特别动人。她厌恶里德先生的连鬓胡子；那让他瞧着颇像个苏格兰管家。当然啦，他是个可亲的人。……
待火车抵达枢纽站，她还没完全拿定主意——自己究竟是爱上了里德先生，还是仅仅在生他的气——人已站在西尔特伯里那座小得出奇的车站上了。

===Original===
The driver of the station cab stopped his unhappy-looking horse before the small gateway and pointed with his whip.
“This is the best way in for you, miss,” he said. “Mr. Daver’s office is at the end of the path.”
He was a shrewd old man, who had driven many applicants for the post of secretary at Larmes Keep, and he guessed that this one, the prettiest of all, did not come as a guest. In the first place, she brought no baggage, and then, too, the ticket collector had come running after her to hand back the return half of her railway ticket, which she had absentmindedly surrendered.
“I’d better wait for you, miss?”

===Chinese===
车站马车的车夫在那扇小门前勒住他那匹一脸苦相的马，用鞭子一指。
“小姐，打这儿进去最便当。”他说，“达弗先生（Mr. Daver）的办公室就在小路尽头。”
他是个精明的老头儿，来拉尔姆宅（Larmes Keep）应征秘书职位的姑娘他载过许许多多，一猜便知眼前这一位——她们中间最漂亮的一个——并不是来做客的。一来她没带行李；二来嘛，收票员还一路追出来，把她心不在焉间一并交出的回程半票还给了她。
“我在这儿等您回来，小姐，可好？”

===Original===
“Oh, yes, please,” said Margaret Belman hastily as she got down from the dilapidated victoria.
“You got an appointment?”
The cabman was a local character, and local characters assume privileges.
“I ast you,” he explained carefully, “because lots of young wimmin have come up to Larmes without appointments and Mr. Daver wouldn’t see ’em. They just cut out the advertisement and come along, but the ad says write. I suppose I’ve made a dozen journeys with young wimmin who ain’t got appointments. I’m telling you for your own good.”

===Chinese===
“哦，好的，劳驾了。”玛格丽特·贝尔曼一面从那辆破旧的四轮马车上下来，一面急忙应道。
“您有预约吗？”
这车夫是本地一位名人，而本地的名人向来要享几分特权的。
“我问您这个，”他细细地解释道，“是因为有好些年轻女人没约好就跑到拉尔姆来，达弗先生根本不见她们。她们光知道把广告剪下来就登门，可广告上写的是要先写信。我估摸着，拉着这些没预约的年轻女人，我白跑的路加起来总有十来趟了。我这么说可是为您好。”

===Original===
The girl smiled.
“You might have warned them before they left the station,” she said with good-humour, “and saved them the cab fare. Yes, I have an appointment.”
From where she stood by the gate, she had a clear view of Larmes Keep. It bore no resemblance to a hotel and less to the superior boarding house that she knew it to be. That part of the house which had been the original Keep was easily distinguished, though the gray, straight walls were masked with ivy that covered also part of the buildings which had been added in the course of the years.
She looked across a smooth green lawn, on which were set a few wicker chairs and tables, to a rose garden which, even in autumn, was a blaze of colour. Behind this was a belt of pine trees that seemed to run to the cliff’s edge. She had a glimpse of a gray-blue sea and a blur of dim smoke from a steamer invisible below the straight horizon. A gentle wind carried the fragrance of the pines to her, and she sniffed ecstatically.

===Chinese===
姑娘笑了。
“您本可以在她们出站之前提醒一句，”她好脾气地说，“也好省下她们的车钱。嗯，我是有预约的。”
她站在大门口，拉尔姆宅的景致一览无余。这宅子与旅馆毫无相似之处，与她明知实情的身份——一家高级寄宿公寓——就更不像了。宅子中原先是主堡的那部分一眼便可认出，只是灰色笔直的墙面已被常春藤遮掩，藤蔓还漫上了历年添建的部分楼宇。
她的目光越过一片平整的绿草坪——草坪上摆着几张藤椅藤桌——落在一座玫瑰园上，虽已入秋，园中依旧一片绚烂。玫瑰园后面是一带松林，仿佛一直延伸到悬崖边上。她望见灰蓝色的海，还望见一缕淡淡的烟影，来自平直海平线下看不见的一艘轮船。一阵轻风将松林的芬芳送到她面前，她如痴如醉地深深吸了一口气。

===Original===
“Isn’t it gorgeous!” she breathed.
The cabman said it “wasn’t bad” and pointed with his whip again.
“It’s that little square place﻿—only built a few years ago. Mr. Daver is more of a writing gentleman than a boardinghouse gentleman.”
She unlatched the oaken gate and walked up the stone path toward the sanctum of the writing gentleman. On either side of the crazy pavement was a deep border of flowers﻿—she might have been passing through a cottage garden.

===Chinese===
“这儿多美啊！”她轻声赞叹。
车夫说这儿“还不赖”，又用鞭子一指。
“就是那座方方正正的小宅子——才盖了没几年。达弗先生与其说是开公寓的先生，不如说是个动笔杆子的先生。”
她拉开橡木门上的门闩，沿着石径朝这位动笔杆子的先生的圣所走去。碎石小径两侧是深深的花畦——她恍若正穿行在一座村舍花园中。

===Original===
There was a long window and a small green door to the annex. Evidently she had been seen, for, as her hand went up to the brass bell-push, the door opened.
It was obviously Mr. Daver himself. A tall, thin man of fifty, with a yellow, elflike face and a smile that brought all her sense of humour into play. Very badly she wanted to laugh. The long upper lip overhung the lower, and except that the face was thin and lined, he had the appearance of some grotesque and foolish mascot. The staring, round brown eyes, the puckered forehead, and a twist of hair that stood upright on the crown of his head made him more brownie-like than ever.
“Miss Belman?” he asked, with a certain eagerness.
He lisped slightly and had a trick of clasping his hands as if he were in an agony of apprehension lest his manner should displease.
“Come into my den,” he said, and gave such emphasis to the last word that she nearly laughed again.

===Chinese===
附楼有一扇长窗和一扇绿色小门。显然早有人望见了她，因为她的手刚要伸向黄铜门铃按钮，门便开了。
这显然就是达弗先生本人。五十岁年纪，又高又瘦，一张蜡黄的、精灵似的脸，那副笑容一下子把她全部的幽默感都勾了起来。她极想放声大笑。长长的上唇耷拉在下唇外面，若不是这张脸又瘦又多皱，他活脱脱是个怪诞而蠢笨的吉祥物。那双圆睁的褐色眼睛、打褶的前额，再加上头顶正中直竖着的一撮头发，让他比以往任何时候都更像布朗尼小精怪（brownie）了。
“是贝尔曼小姐吗？”他问道，带着几分急切。
他说话微微有些咬舌，还有个把双手扣在一起的习惯，仿佛正为唯恐举止失礼而惶恐不安。
“到我的书斋里来。”他说，把最后一个词咬得极重，害得她差点又笑出声来。

===Original===
The “den” was a very comfortably furnished study, one wall of which was covered with books. Closing the door behind her, he pushed up a chair with a little nervous laugh.
“I’m so very glad you came. Did you have a comfortable journey? I’m sure you did. And is London hot and stuffy? I’m afraid it is. Would you like a cup of tea? Of course you would.”
He fired question and answer so rapidly that she had no chance of replying, and he had taken up a telephone and ordered the tea before she could express a wish on the subject.
“You are young, very young.” He shook his head sadly, “Twenty-four﻿—no? Do you use the typewriter? What a ridiculous question to ask!”

===Chinese===
这间“书斋”是间陈设极为舒适的书房，一面墙从上到下排满了书。他随手带上身后的门，轻笑一声——笑声里透着几分紧张——推过来一把椅子。
“您来了，我真是说不出的高兴。旅途还舒适吗？我敢说一定舒适。伦敦又热又闷吧？恐怕是的。来杯茶好不好？您当然乐意的。”
他自问自答，连珠炮一般，她根本无从插嘴；不等她对此表明意愿，他已抓起电话吩咐上茶了。
“您年轻，非常年轻。”他凄然摇头，“二十四岁——不对吗？您会用打字机吧？这问题问得多可笑！”

===Original===
“It is very kind of you to see me, Mr. Daver,” she said, “and I don’t suppose for one moment that I shall suit you, I have had no experience in hotel management, and I realize, from the salary you offer﻿—”
“Quiet,” said Mr. Daver, shaking his head solemnly: “that is what I require. There is very little work, but I wished to be relieved even of that little. My own labours”﻿—he waved his hand to a pedestal desk littered with paper﻿—“are colossal. I need a lady to keep accounts﻿—to watch my interests. Somebody I can trust. I believe in faces, do you? I see that you do. And in character shown in handwriting? You believe in that also. I have advertised for three months and have interviewed thirty-five applicants. Impossible! Their voices﻿—terrible! I judge people by their voices. So do you. On Monday, when you telephoned, I said to myself, ‘The Voice!’ ”
He was clasping his hands together so tightly that his knuckles showed whitely, and this time her laughter was almost beyond arrest.
“Although, Mr. Daver, I know nothing of hotel management, I think I could learn, and I want the position, naturally. The salary is terribly generous.”

===Chinese===
“达弗先生，多谢您肯见我。”她说，“我丝毫不觉得自己能合您的意。旅馆管理的事我毫无经验，而且从您开的薪水来看——”
“清静。”达弗先生说着，郑重其事地摇头，“我要的正是这个。活计很少，可我连那一星半点儿也想卸掉。我自己的劳作”——他朝一张摊满纸张的台柱书桌挥了挥手——“浩大无边。我需要一位女士管账——照看我的利益。一个我能信得过的人。我相信面相，您信吗？我看得出您也信。还有笔迹里显出的品性？这个您也信。我登了三个月广告，面见过三十五位应征者。全都不行！她们的嗓音——可怕极了！我凭嗓音识人。您也一样。星期一您打电话来的时候，我对自己说：‘就是这个嗓子！’”
他把双手扣得死紧，指节都泛了白，这一回她的笑意几乎再也按捺不住。
“达弗先生，虽然我对旅馆管理一窍不通，我想我学得会，而且我自然想要这个职位。这份薪水实在慷慨得吓人。”

===Original===
“ ‘Terribly generous,’ ” repeated the man, in a murmur. “How curious those words sound in juxtaposition!”
The door opened and a woman bearing a silver tray came in. She was dressed very neatly in black. The faded eyes scarcely looked at Margaret as she stood meekly waiting while Mr. Daver spoke.
“My housekeeper. How kind of you to bring the tea, Mrs. Burton!﻿—Mrs. Burton, this is the new secretary to the company. She must have the best room in the Keep﻿—the Blue Room. But﻿—ah!”﻿—he pinched his lip anxiously﻿—“blue may not be your colour?”
Again Margaret laughed.

===Chinese===
“‘慷慨得吓人。’”这人喃喃地重复了一遍，“这两个词并排放在一起，听着多么古怪！”
门开了，一个端着银托盘的女人走了进来。她一身黑衣，穿戴得极为齐整。达弗先生说话时，她温顺地立在一旁候着，那双黯淡的眼睛几乎不朝玛格丽特看一眼。
“我的管家。伯顿太太（Mrs. Burton），多谢您把茶送来！——伯顿太太，这位是公司新聘的秘书。她必须住主堡里最好的房间——蓝房间。可是——啊！”——他不安地捏了捏嘴唇——“蓝色未必配您的肤色吧？”
玛格丽特又笑了。

===Original===
“Any colour is my colour,” she said. “But I haven’t decided﻿—”
“Go with Mrs. Burton; see the house﻿—your office﻿—your room.”
He pointed to the door, and before the girl knew what she was doing she had followed the housekeeper through the door. A narrow passage connected the private office of Mr. Daver with the house, and Margaret was ushered into a large and lofty room which covered the superficial area of the Keep.
“The banquittin’ ’all,” said Mrs. Burton in a thin cockney voice remarkable for its monotony. “It’s used as a lounge. We’ve only got three boarders. Mr. Daver’s very partic’lar. We get a lot in for the winter.”

===Chinese===
“什么颜色都配我。”她说，“不过我还没拿定主意——”
“跟伯顿太太去吧；看看这宅子——您的办公室——您的房间。”
他朝门一指，姑娘还没弄明白自己在做什么，就已经跟着管家穿门而过。一条窄窄的走廊把达弗先生的私人办公室与宅子连通起来，玛格丽特被引进一间宽大高敞的屋子，它恰好占满主堡的整个底面积。
“宴会厅。”伯顿太太说，那口伦敦土腔细声细气，单调得出奇。“如今当客厅使。我们眼下只有三位房客。达弗先生挑剔得很。一到冬天，住进来的人就多了。”

===Original===
“Three boarders isn’t a very paying proposition,” said the girl.
Mrs. Burton sniffed.
“Mr. Daver don’t want it to pay. It’s the company he likes. He only turned it into a boardin’ ’ouse because he likes to see people come and go without having to talk to ’em. It’s a nobby.”
“A what?” asked the puzzled girl. “Oh, you mean a hobby?”
“I said a nobby,” said Mrs. Burton, in her listless, uncomplaining way.

===Chinese===
“三位房客，这可算不上一桩赚钱的买卖。”姑娘说。
伯顿太太吸了吸鼻子。
“达弗先生压根儿不指望它赚钱。他喜欢的是有人做伴。他把宅子改成寄宿公寓，只因乐得看人来人往，又不必同他们搭话。这是他的一桩‘皮好’。”
“一桩什么？”困惑的姑娘问道，“哦，您是说‘癖好’？”
“我说的是‘皮好’。”伯顿太太说，还是那副无精打采、不抱怨的腔调。

===Original===
Beyond the hall was a small and cosy sitting room with French windows opening on to the lawn. Outside the windows, three people sat at tea. One was an elderly clergyman with a strong, hard face. He was eating toast and reading a church paper, oblivious of his companion. The second member of the party was a pale-faced girl about Margaret’s own age. In spite of her pallor she was extraordinarily beautiful. A pair of big, dark eyes surveyed the visitor for a moment and then returned to her companion, a military-looking man of forty.
Mrs. Burton waited until they were ascending the broad stairway to the upper floor before she “introduced” them. “The clergyman’s a Reverend Dean from South Africa, the young lady’s Miss Olga Crewe, the other gent is Colonel Hothling﻿—they’re boarders.﻿—This is your room, miss.”
It was indeed a gem of an apartment; the sort of room that Margaret Belman had dreamed about. It was exquisitely furnished, and, like all the other rooms at Larmes Keep (as she discovered later), was provided with its private bathroom. The walls were panelled to half their height; the ceilings heavily beamed. She guessed that beneath the parquet was the original stone-flagged floor.
Margaret looked and sighed. It was going to be very hard to refuse this post. Why she should think of refusing it at all she could not for the life of her understand.

===Chinese===
大厅那头是一间小而舒适的起居室，落地长窗开向草坪。窗外有三个人正在用茶。一位是年长的牧师，面容刚硬有力。他一边吃着吐司，一边读一份教会报纸，全然不旁顾同伴。这一伙人的第二位，是个面色苍白的姑娘，年纪与玛格丽特相仿。尽管脸色苍白，她却出奇地美丽。一双又大又黑的眼睛将来客打量了片刻，随即又转回到她的同伴——一个四十岁上下、军人模样的男人——身上。
伯顿太太一直等到两人登上通往楼上的宽大楼梯，才把他们“介绍”了一番。“牧师是南非来的迪安牧师（Reverend Dean），年轻小姐是奥尔加·克鲁小姐（Olga Crewe），另一位先生是霍斯林上校（Colonel Hothling）——都是房客。——这就是您的房间，小姐。”
这确是一间精品似的屋子；正是玛格丽特·贝尔曼梦寐以求的那种房间。它陈设精美绝伦，而且同拉尔姆宅所有的房间一样（这是她后来才发现的），配有独用的浴室。墙壁半高镶着木饰板；天花板架着粗重的横梁。她猜在拼花地板下面，便是原先的石板地面。
玛格丽特看了看，叹了口气。要回绝这个职位实在难上加难。至于自己究竟为什么竟会想到回绝，她是无论如何也想不明白的。

===Original===
“It’s a beautiful room,” she said.
Mrs. Burton cast an apathetic eye round the apartment.
“It’s old,” she said. “I don’t like old houses. I used to live in Brixton﻿—”
She stopped abruptly, sniffed in a deprecating way, and jingled the keys that she carried in her hand.

===Chinese===
“这房间真美。”她说。
伯顿太太漠然地朝屋子四下里扫了一眼。
“它老了。”她说，“我不喜欢老房子。我从前住在布里克斯顿（Brixton）——”
她猛地打住，不以为然地吸了吸鼻子，把攥在手里的一串钥匙晃得叮当作响。

===Original===
“You’re suited, I suppose?”
“Suited? You mean, am I taking the appointment? I don’t know yet.”
Mrs. Burton looked round vaguely. The girl had the impression that she was trying to say something in praise of the place﻿—something that would prejudice her in favour of accepting the appointment. Then she spoke.
“The food’s good,” she said, and Margaret smiled.

===Chinese===
“您合意了吧，我猜？”
“合意？您是问我接不接这份差事？我还不知道呢。”
伯顿太太茫然地环顾四周。姑娘有个感觉：她正努力想说几句这地方的好话——几句能使她倾向接受这份差事的话。随后她开了口。
“伙食不赖。”她说。玛格丽特笑了。

===Original===
When she came back through the hall she saw the three people she had seen at tea. The Colonel was walking by himself; the clergyman and the pale-faced girl were strolling across the lawn talking to one another.
Mr. Daver was sitting at his desk, his high forehead resting on his palm, and he was biting the end of a pen as Mrs. Burton closed the door on them.
“You like the room: naturally. You will start﻿—when? Next Monday week, I think. What a relief! You have seen Mrs. Burton.” He wagged a finger at her roguishly. “Ah! Now you know! It is impossible! Can I leave her to meet the duchess and speed the duke? Can I trust her to adjust the little quarrels that naturally arise between guests? You are right﻿—I can’t. I must have a lady here﻿—I must! I must!”

===Chinese===
她穿过大厅回来时，又见到了方才用茶的那三个人。上校独自踱着步；牧师和那个面色苍白的姑娘正在草坪上边走边谈。
达弗先生正坐在书桌前，高高的额头抵着掌心，嘴里咬着一支笔的笔端——这时伯顿太太随手带门，把她们俩关在了门外。
“您喜欢那房间：这是自然的。您要上任了——什么时候来？我想，是下下星期一。真是如释重负！您已经见过伯顿太太了。”他狡黠地朝她晃了晃一根手指。“啊！这下您明白了！不行，绝对不行！我能放手让她去迎候公爵夫人、给公爵送行吗？宾客之间自然会生出些小小口角，我能托她去调停吗？您说得对——不能。这里必须有一位女士——必须！必须！”

===Original===
He nodded emphatically, his impish brown eyes fixed on hers, the bulging upper lip grotesquely curved in a delighted grin.
“My work suffers, as you see; constantly to be brought from my studies to settle such matters as the fixing of a tennis net﻿—intolerable!”
“You write a great deal?” she managed to ask.
She felt she must postpone her decision to the last possible moment.
“A great deal. On crime. Ah, you are interested? I am preparing an encyclopaedia of crime!”

===Chinese===
他重重地点头，那双精灵似的褐色眼睛紧盯着她的双眼，凸出的上唇咧成一道欢欣而怪诞的笑弧。
“您瞧，我的工作都跟着遭殃；老是被人从书斋里请出来，去料理诸如张罗网球网之类的杂事——叫人无法容忍！”
“您写得很多吗？”她好容易才插上一句。
她觉得自己非得把决定拖到最后一刻不可。
“多得吓人。写犯罪。啊，您来了兴致？我正在编纂一部犯罪百科全书！”

===Original===
He said this impressively, dramatically.
“On crime?”
He nodded.
“It is one of my hobbies. I am a rich man and can afford hobbies. This place is a hobby. I lose four thousand a year, but I am satisfied. I pick and choose my own guests. If one bores me I tell him to go﻿—that his room has been taken. Could I do that if they were my friends? No! They interest me; they fill the house; they give me company and amusement. When will you come?”

===Chinese===
他说得郑重其事，戏剧味十足。
“写犯罪？”
他点了点头。
“这是我的癖好之一。我是个有钱人，养得起癖好。这地方就是一桩癖好。我一年赔进去四千，可我心满意足。房客由我亲自拣选。哪一位叫我厌烦了，我就打发他走——就说他的房间已租给了别人。他们若是我朋友，我能这样吗？不能！他们叫我感兴趣；他们把宅子住满；他们给我做伴、供我消遣。您什么时候来？”

===Original===
She hesitated.
“I think﻿—”
“Monday week? Excellent!” He shook her hand vigorously.
“You need not be lonely. If my guests bore you, invite your own friends. Let them come as the guests of the house. Until Monday!”

===Chinese===
她犹豫了。
“我想——”
“下下星期一？好极了！”他用力握住她的手。
“您不必寂寞。我的房客若叫您生厌，就把您自己的朋友请来。让他们以本宅宾客的身份入住。星期一见！”

===Original===
She was walking down the garden path to the waiting cabman, a little dazed, more than a little undecided.
“Did you get the place, miss?” asked the friendly cabman.
“I suppose I did,” replied Margaret.
She looked back toward Larmes Keep. The lawns were empty, but near at hand she had a glimpse of a woman. Only for a second, and then she disappeared in a belt of laurel that ran parallel with the boundary wall of the property. Evidently there was a rough path through the bushes, and Mrs. Burton had sought this hiding place. Her hands covered her face as she staggered forward blindly, and the faint sound of her sobs came back to the astonished girl.
“That’s the housekeeper﻿—she’s a bit mad,” said the cabman calmly.

===Chinese===
她沿着花园小径朝等候着的车夫走去，脑子有点发懵，主意更是迟迟拿不定。
“职位到手了吗，小姐？”和气的车夫问道。
“大概是到手了。”玛格丽特答道。
她回头朝拉尔姆宅望去。草坪上空空荡荡，可就在近处，她瞥见了一个女人。只一秒钟，那身影便消失在一片与宅子界墙平行的月桂树丛里。灌木丛中显然有一条荒僻小径，伯顿太太找到的正是这处藏身之地。她双手掩面，跌跌撞撞地盲目前行，那微弱的啜泣声，一路传回到惊愕不已的姑娘耳中。
“那是管家——她有点儿疯。”车夫平静地说。

## II / 二

===Original===
George Ravini was not an unpleasant looking man. From his own point of view, which was naturally prejudiced, he was extremely attractive, with his crisp brown hair, his handsome Neapolitan features, his height and his poise. And when to his natural advantages were added the best suit that Savile Row could create, the most spotless of gray hats, and the malacca sword-stick on which one kid-gloved hand rested as upon the hilt of a foil, the shiniest of enamelled shoes, and the finest of gray silk socks, the picture was well framed and embellished.
Greatest embellishment of all were George Ravini’s luck rings. He was a superstitious man and addicted to charms. On the little finger of his right hand were three gold rings, and in each ring three large diamonds. The luck stones of Ravini were one of the traditions of Saffron Hill.
Most of the time he had the half-amused, half-bored smile of a man for whom life held no mysteries and could offer in experience little that was new. And the smile was justified, for George knew most of the things that were happening in London or likely to happen.
He had worked outward from a one-room house in Saffron Hill, where he first saw the light; had enlarged the narrow horizons which surrounded his childhood so that now, in place of the poverty-stricken child who had shared a bed with his father’s performing monkey, he was not only the possessor of a classy flat in Half Moon Street, but the owner of the block in which it was situated. His balance at the Continental Bank was a generous one; he had securities which brought him an income beyond his needs, and a larger revenue from the two night clubs and gambling houses which he controlled, to say nothing of the perquisites which came his way from a score of other sources.

===Chinese===
乔治·拉维尼（George Ravini）这人，长相并不讨嫌。照他自己那副难免偏心的眼光看来，他简直魅力十足：一头蓬松的褐发，一张英俊的那不勒斯人面孔，身量高大，举止从容。待这天生的优势再添上萨维尔街（Savile Row）所能裁制的最好的一套西装、一顶最一尘不染的灰帽、一根马六甲白藤手杖剑——一只戴小山羊皮手套的手搭在杖上，宛如搭着花剑的护手——再加一双最锃亮的漆皮鞋和最上等的灰色丝袜，这幅画像便算装裱精当，更添光彩。
而所有点缀之中最出彩的，当数乔治·拉维尼的幸运戒指。他是个迷信的人，笃好各种吉祥物件。他右手的小指上戴着三枚金戒指，每枚戒指上镶着三颗大钻石。拉维尼的幸运宝石，是萨弗伦山（Saffron Hill）的一桩传统。
他大部分时间都挂着一副半觉有趣、半觉无聊的笑意，那是一个觉得人生再无奥秘、阅历中难得有新事之人的笑。这笑也确有底气，因为伦敦眼下正在发生的事，以及多半要发生的事，乔治大多了如指掌。
他从萨弗伦山一间仅有一室的小屋向外打拼——他最初就是在那儿睁眼见世的；他把围裹童年的狭窄天地一寸寸拓宽，于是如今，这个当年与父亲的耍猴同榻而眠的穷孩子，不仅成了半月街（Half Moon Street）一套高档公寓的主人，还是公寓所在那栋楼的业主。他在大陆银行（Continental Bank）的存款颇为丰厚；他持有的证券带来超出所需的收益，而他掌管的两家夜总会和赌场进项更巨，更不必说从其他二十来处门路源源到手的外快。

===Original===
The word of Ravini was law from Leyton to Clerkenwell; his fiats were obeyed within a mile radius of Fitzroy Square; and no other gang leader in London might raise his head without George’s permission save at the risk of waking in the casualty ward of the Middlesex Hospital entirely surrounded by bandages.
He waited patiently on the broad space of Waterloo Station, occasionally consulting his gold wrist watch, and surveyed with a benevolent and proprietorial eye the stream of life that flowed from the barriers.
The station clock showed a quarter after six. He glanced at his watch and scanned the crowd that was debouching from No. 7 platform. After a few minutes’ scrutiny, he saw the girl, and with a pat to his cravat and a touch to the brim of his hat which set it tilting, he strolled to meet her.
Margaret Belman was too intent with her own thoughts to be thinking about the debonair and youngish man who had so often sought an introduction by the conventional method of pretending they had met before. Indeed, in the excitement of her visit to Larmes Keep, she had forgotten that this pestiferous gallant existed or was likely to be waiting for her on her return from the country.

===Chinese===
从莱顿（Leyton）到克勒肯维尔（Clerkenwell），拉维尼的话就是王法；在菲茨罗伊广场（Fitzroy Square）方圆一英里之内，他的号令无人不从；伦敦城里任何别的匪帮首领，未经乔治许可而胆敢冒头，就唯有等着在米德尔塞克斯医院（Middlesex Hospital）的事故病房里醒来——浑身上下缠满绷带。
他在滑铁卢车站（Waterloo Station）宽敞的空场上耐心等候，不时看一看金质腕表，以一副既慈祥又如业主巡视般的眼光，打量着从检票口涌出的人生洪流。
车站大钟指着六点一刻。他瞥了一眼腕表，扫视着从 7 号站台涌出的人群。端详了几分钟之后，他看见了那位姑娘，于是拍一拍领结，碰一碰帽檐——帽子随之微微斜侧——便踱步迎上前去。
玛格丽特·贝尔曼（Margaret Belman）满脑子都是自己的心事，根本无暇去想那个风度翩翩、年纪不大的男人——此人屡屡用假装两人早先见过面的老套路，设法与她结识。说实在的，这趟拉尔姆宅（Larmes Keep，法语意为“眼泪”）之行让她心绪激荡，她压根儿忘了这位缠人的殷勤公子还存在于世，更想不到他会候在她从乡下返回的路上。

===Original===
George Ravini stopped and waited for her approach, smiling his approval. He liked slim girls of her colouring: girls who dressed rather severely and wore rather nice stockings and plain little hats. He raised his hat; the luck stones glittered beautifully.
“Oh!” said Margaret Belman, and stopped, too.
“Good evening, Miss Belman,” said George, flashing his white teeth. “Quite a coincidence, meeting you again.”
As she attempted to walk past him, he fell in by her side.

===Chinese===
乔治·拉维尼站住脚，含笑等她走近，笑容里满是赞许。他喜欢的正是她这种身段肤色的苗条姑娘：衣着穿得颇为素净，袜子穿得相当不错，头上是简朴的小帽。他举了举帽；幸运宝石熠熠生辉，煞是好看。
“哦！”玛格丽特·贝尔曼说了一声，也停了下来。
“晚上好，贝尔曼小姐。”乔治说着，一口白牙闪闪发亮，“又遇见您，真是巧合。”
她想从他身旁走过去，他却顺势凑到她身边同行。

===Original===
“I wish I had my car here, I might have driven you home,” he said conversationally. “I’ve got a new Twenty Rolls﻿—rather a neat little machine. I don’t use it a great deal﻿—I like to walk from Half Moon Street.”
“Are you walking to Half Moon Street now?” she asked quietly.
But George was a man of experience.
“Your way is my way,” he said.
She stopped.

===Chinese===
“真希望我的车在这儿，我就可以开车送您回家了。”他像闲谈似的说，“我新到手一辆‘二十’型劳斯莱斯——相当精巧的一辆小车。我不大用它——我喜欢从半月街步行。”
“您现在是要步行去半月街吗？”她平静地问。
可乔治是个老于世故的人。
“您走的路就是我走的路。”他说。
她停下脚步。

===Original===
“What is your name?” she asked.
“Smith﻿—Anderton Smith,” he answered readily. “Why do you want to know?”
“I want to tell the next policeman we meet,” she said, and Mr. Ravini, not unaccustomed to such threats, was amused.
“Don’t be a silly little girl,” he said. “I’m doing no harm and you don’t want to get your name in the newspapers. Besides, I should merely say that you asked me to walk with you and that we were old friends.”

===Chinese===
“您叫什么名字？”她问。
“史密斯——安德顿·史密斯。”他应声便答，“您打听这个做什么？”
“我想告诉咱们接下来碰到的那位警察。”她说。拉维尼先生对这类威胁早已见惯不惊，反倒觉得有趣。
“别犯傻了，小姑娘。”他说，“我又没干什么坏事，您总不想让您的大名见报吧。再说了，我只需说，是您请我陪您一路走的，我们是老朋友。”

===Original===
She looked at him steadily.
“I may meet a friend very soon who will need a lot of convincing,” she said. “Will you please go away?”
George was pleased to stay, as he explained.
“What a foolish young lady you are!” he began. “I’m merely offering you the common courtesies﻿—”
A hand gripped his arm and slowly pulled him round﻿—and this in broad daylight on Waterloo Station, under the eyes of at least two of his own tribe. Mr. Ravini’s dark eyes snapped dangerously.

===Chinese===
她目不转睛地看着他。
“我很快就会遇见一位朋友，他可是要费许多口舌才肯相信的。”她说，“请您走开，行不行？”
乔治倒乐意留下，他还如此这般地解说了一通。
“您这位年轻小姐可真是傻气！”他开口道，“我不过是向您献上寻常的礼数——”
一只手攥住他的胳膊，慢慢地把他拽得转过身来——而这竟是在光天化日之下的滑铁卢车站，当着至少两个他自家同伙的眼睛。拉维尼先生那双黑眼睛里凶光迸射。

===Original===
And yet seemingly his assailant was a most inoffensive man. He was tall and rather melancholy-looking. He wore a frock coat buttoned tightly across his breast and a high, flat-crowned, hard felt hat. On his biggish nose a pair of steel-rimmed pince-nez were set at an awkward angle. A slither of sandy side whiskers decorated his cheek, and hooked to his arm was a lightly furled umbrella. Not that George examined these details with any care: they were rather familiar to him. He knew Mr. J. G. Reeder, detective to the Public Prosecutor’s office, and the fight went out of his eyes.
“Why, Mr. Reeder!” he said, with a geniality that almost sounded sincere. “This is a pleasant surprise. Meet my young lady friend Miss Belman﻿—I was just taking her along﻿—”
“Not to the Flotsam Club for a cup of tea?” murmured Mr. Reeder in a tone of pain. “Not to Harraby’s Restaurant? Don’t tell me that, Giorgio! Dear me! How interesting either experience would be!”
He beamed upon the scowling Italian.
“At the Flotsam,” he went on, “you would have been able to show the young lady where your friends caught young Lord Fallen for three thousand pounds only the night before last﻿—so they tell me. At Harraby’s you might have shown her that interesting little room where the police come in by the back way whenever you consider it expedient to betray one of your friends. She has missed a treat!”

===Chinese===
然而，动手的人在表面上偏偏是个再无害不过的人。他个子高高，模样颇为愁苦。他穿一件胸前紧扣的长礼服，戴一顶高筒平顶的硬毡帽。在那只略大的鼻子上，一副钢框夹鼻眼镜别扭地斜架着。两撇稀稀拉拉的浅褐色连鬓胡子装点着他的面颊，臂弯里还挂着一把微微收拢的雨伞。倒不是说乔治仔细审视了这些细节：这些他早就看熟了。他认得检察总长办公室的这位侦探——里德先生（Mr. J. G. Reeder），眼里的斗意顿时敛尽。
“哎呀，里德先生！”他说道，那份亲热劲儿听上去几乎像是出于真心，“这真是意外之喜。来认识一下我这位年轻的女伴贝尔曼小姐——我正要带她去——”
“该不是去弗洛特瑟姆俱乐部（Flotsam Club）喝杯茶吧？”里德先生用一种痛心的声气低声说，“也不去哈拉比餐馆（Harraby's）？乔治奥，可别这么跟我说！天哪！随便哪一样经历该多有意思啊！”
他笑容满面地望着这个满面怒容的意大利人。
“在弗洛特瑟姆俱乐部，”他接着说，“您本可以指给这位小姐看，前天夜里您的朋友们在哪儿设局，把年轻的法伦勋爵（Lord Fallen）钓去了三千英镑——人家是这么告诉我的。在哈拉比餐馆，您本可以领她看看那间有趣的小房间——每逢您觉得出卖哪位朋友划算的时候，警察就从后门进来。她可是错过了一大乐事！”

===Original===
George Ravini’s smile did not harmonize with his sudden pallor.
“Now, listen, Mr. Reeder﻿—”
“I’m sorry I can’t, Giorgio.” Mr. Reeder shook his head mournfully. “My time is precious. Yet, I will spare you one minute to tell you that Miss Belman is a very particular friend of mine. If her experience of today is repeated, who knows what might happen, for I am, as you probably know, a malicious man.” He eyed the Italian thoughtfully. “Is it malice, I wonder, which inhibits a most interesting revelation which I have on the tip of my tongue? I wonder. The human mind, Mr. Ravini, is a curious and complex thing. Well, well, I must be getting along. Give my regards to your criminal associates, and if you find yourself shadowed by a gentleman from Scotland Yard, bear him no resentment. He is doing his duty. And do not lose sight of my﻿—um﻿—warning about this lady.”
“I have said nothing to this young lady that a gentleman shouldn’t.”

===Chinese===
乔治·拉维尼脸上的笑意，与他陡然泛白的面色全然不衬。
“喂，听着，里德先生——”
“很遗憾，我可听不了，乔治奥。”里德先生凄然摇头，“我的时间宝贵。不过，我愿意为您匀出一分钟，告诉您贝尔曼小姐是我一位非常特别的朋友。倘若她今天的遭遇再来一回，谁知会发生什么事呢，因为我，您大概也知道，是个心地歹毒的人。”他若有所思地端详着这个意大利人。“我倒纳闷，是不是这份歹毒，一时摁住了我舌尖上那桩极为有趣的隐情？真是纳闷。人的头脑啊，拉维尼先生，是样又古怪又复杂的东西。好啦，好啦，我得走了。请代我向您的罪犯同伙们问好。还有，倘若您发觉自己被苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）的某位先生盯梢，请不要对他心怀怨恨。他是在履行职责。另外，切莫把我对这位小姐的——呃——警告抛在脑后。”
“我对这位小姐说的话，没有一句是正人君子不该说的。”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder peered at Ravini.
“If you have,” he said, “you may expect to see me some time this evening﻿—and I shall not come alone. In fact”﻿—this in a most confidential tone﻿—“I shall bring sufficient strong men with me to take from you the keys of your box in the Fetter Lane Safe Deposit.”
That was all he said, and Ravini reeled under the threat.
Before he had quite recovered, Mr. J. G. Reeder and his charge had disappeared into the throng.

===Chinese===
里德先生眯起眼睛端详拉维尼。
“您要是说了，”他说，“今晚什么时候就等着见我吧——而且我不会一个人来。事实上”——这时他换上一种极尽推心置腹的语气——“我会带上足够多的壮汉，从您手里取走菲特巷（Fetter Lane）保管库里您那只寄存箱的钥匙。”
他就说了这么几句，而拉维尼被这番威胁震得晕头转向。
还不等他完全回过神来，里德先生已带着他照护的人消失在人流之中。

## III / 三

===Original===
“An interesting man,” said Mr. Reeder, as the cab crossed Westminster Bridge. “He is, in fact, the most interesting man I know at this particular moment. It was fate that I should walk into him as I did. But I wish he wouldn’t wear diamond rings!”

He stole a sidelong glance at his companion.

“Well, did you﻿—um﻿—like the place?”

===Chinese===
“一个有意思的人，”出租汽车驶过威斯敏斯特桥（Westminster Bridge）时，里德先生说，“说实在的，此时此刻，他是我所知道的最有意思的人。我那样一头撞见他，全是命运的安排。只是我但愿他别戴钻石戒指！”

他侧眼偷觑了一下身边的同伴。

“那么，您觉得——呃——喜欢那地方吗？”

===Original===
“It is very beautiful,” she said; without enthusiasm, “but it is rather far away from London.”

His face fell.

“Have you declined the post?” he asked anxiously.

She half turned in the seat and looked at him.

===Chinese===
“很美，”她说，不带什么热情，“就是离伦敦太远了些。”

他的脸沉了下来。

“您回绝那份差事了吗？”他焦急地问。

她在座位上侧过身来，望着他。

===Original===
“Mr. Reeder, I honestly believe you wish to see the back of me!”

To her surprise, Mr. Reeder went very red.

“Why﻿—um﻿—of course I do﻿—I don’t, I mean. But it seems a very good position, even as a temporary position.” He blinked at her. “I shall miss you, I really shall miss you, Miss﻿—um﻿—Margaret. We have become such”﻿—here he swallowed something﻿—“good friends, but the﻿—a certain business is on my mind﻿—I mean, I am rather perturbed.”

He looked from one window to the other as though he suspected an eavesdropper riding on the step of the cab, and then, lowering his voice:

===Chinese===
“里德先生，我老实相信，您是巴不得早点摆脱我！”

出她意料，里德先生的脸涨得通红。

“为什么——呃——我当然是巴不得——我是说，我不是。不过那看来是份很好的差事，就算是临时的，也很难得。”他朝她眨眨眼。“我会想您的，我真的会想您的，——呃——玛格丽特小姐。我们已经成了这么好的”——说到这里他咽下了什么——“好朋友，可是那——有桩事情压在我心上——我是说，我心里相当不安。”

他从这扇车窗望到那扇车窗，仿佛疑心有个窃听的扒在车门的踏板上，随后压低了嗓音：

===Original===
“I have never discussed with you, my dear Miss﻿—um﻿—Margaret, the rather unpleasant details of my trade; but there is, or was, a gentleman named Flack﻿—*F*-*l*-*a*-*c*-*k*,” he spelt it. “You remember?” he asked anxiously, and when she shook her head: “I hoped that you would. One reads about these things in the public press. But five years ago you would have been a child﻿—”

“You’re very flattering,” she smiled. “I was, in fact, a grown-up young lady of eighteen.”

“Were you really?” asked Mr. Reeder in a hushed voice. “You surprise me! Well, Mr. Flack was the kind of person one so frequently reads about in the pages of the sensational novelist﻿—who has not too keen a regard for the probabilities and facts of life. A master criminal, the organizer of﻿—um﻿—a confederation, or, as vulgar people would call it, gang.”

He sighed and closed his eyes, and she thought for one moment he was praying for the iniquitous criminal.

===Chinese===
“我亲爱的——呃——玛格丽特小姐，我还从未同您谈起过我这一行里那些不太愉快的细节；可是有这么一位，或者说曾经有这么一位弗拉克先生——*F*-*l*-*a*-*c*-*k*，”他把名字一个字母一个字母地拼了出来。“您记得吗？”他急切地问；见她摇头，又说：“我本来指望您记得。这种事情，报纸上是常读得到的。不过五年前，您还是个孩子——”

“您真会捧人，”她笑道，“其实呢，我当时已是十八岁的大姑娘了。”

“真的吗？”里德先生压着嗓子问，“您真让我吃惊！是这样，弗拉克先生是那类人物——人们屡屡在耸人听闻的小说家书页里读到的，而这类小说家对人生的常理与事实又不太讲究。一个大罪犯，一个——呃——联合组织的组织者，用粗人的说法，就是一个匪帮。”

他叹了口气，闭上眼睛；有一刹那，她还以为他在为那个恶贯满盈的罪犯祷告。

===Original===
“A brilliant criminal﻿—it is a terrible thing to confess, but I have had a reluctant admiration for him. You see, as I have so often explained to you, I am cursed with a criminal mind. But he was mad.”

“All criminals are mad: you have explained that so often,” she said, a little tartly, for she was not anxious that the conversation should drift from her immediate affairs.

“But he was really mad,” said Mr. Reeder with great earnestness, and tapped his forehead deliberately. “His very madness was his salvation. He did daring things, but with the cunning of a madman. He shot down two policemen in cold blood﻿—he did this at midday in a crowded City street and got away. We caught him at last, of course. People like that are always caught in this country. I﻿—um﻿—assisted. In fact, I﻿—well, I assisted! That is why I am thinking of our friend Giorgio; for it was Mr. Ravini who betrayed him to us for two thousand pounds. I negotiated the deal, Mr. Ravini being a criminal﻿—”

She stared at him open-mouthed.

===Chinese===
“一个出色的罪犯——承认这一点很可怕，可我一直不由得佩服他。您知道，正如我常向您解释的，我天生一副罪犯的头脑，这是我的不幸。可他是疯子。”

“所有的罪犯都是疯子：这话您解释过不知多少回了。”她略带尖酸地说，因为她不愿话题从她眼下的正事上漂走。

“可他是真的疯，”里德先生极为恳切地说，还刻意敲了敲自己的脑门。“正是那份疯狂救了他。他干大胆的事，却带着疯子的狡诈。他冷血地打死了两名警察——是在正午、在伦敦城熙熙攘攘的大街上干的，居然叫他逃掉了。当然，我们最后还是抓到了他。这种人在这国家总是会被抓到的。我——呃——从旁协助了。事实上，我——咳，我协助了！这正是我想起咱们那位朋友乔治奥的缘故；因为把他出卖给我们的，正是拉维尼先生，得了两千镑。那笔交易是我谈的，因为拉维尼先生本人是个罪犯——”

她目瞪口呆地盯着他。

===Original===
“That Italian? You don’t mean that?”

Mr. Reeder nodded.

“Mr. Ravini had dealings with the Flack gang, and by chance learned of Old John’s whereabouts. We took old John Flack in his sleep.” Mr. Reeder sighed again. “He said some very bitter things about me. People, when they are arrested, frequently exaggerate the shortcomings of their﻿—er﻿—captors.”

“Was he tried?” she asked.

===Chinese===
“那个意大利人？您不是当真的吧？”

里德先生点了点头。

“拉维尼先生同弗拉克匪帮有过来往，碰巧得知了老约翰的下落。我们趁老约翰·弗拉克熟睡时抓了他。”里德先生又叹了口气。“他说过我许多恶毒的话。人被逮捕的时候，往往会夸大他们的——呃——逮捕者的短处。”

“他受审了吗？”她问。

===Original===
“He was tried,” said Mr. Reeder, “on a charge of murder. But of course he was mad. ‘Guilty but insane’ was the verdict, and he was sent to Broadmoor Criminal Lunatic Asylum.”

He searched feebly in his pockets, produced a very limp packet of cigarettes, extracted one, and asked permission to smoke. She watched the damp squib of a thing drooping pathetically from his lower lip. His eyes were staring sombrely through the window at the green of the park through which they were passing, and he seemed entirely absorbed in his contemplation of nature.

“But what has that to do with my going into the country?”

Mr. Reeder brought his eyes round to survey her.

===Chinese===
“受审了，”里德先生说，“罪名是杀人。不过他当然是疯子。‘有罪，但精神失常’——判决是这么说的，随后他就被送进了布罗德莫尔刑事疯人院。”

他有气无力地在口袋里摸索，掏出一包软塌塌的香烟，抽出一支，请求允许他吸烟。她看着那受潮的玩意儿可怜巴巴地耷拉在他的下唇上。他的眼睛阴郁地望着窗外，望着他们正在穿行的那片园林的绿意，仿佛全然沉醉在对大自然的观照之中。

“可这些同我下乡去有什么相干？”

里德先生把目光收回来，打量着她。

===Original===
“Mr. Flack was a very vindictive man,” he said, “a very brilliant man﻿—I hate confessing this. And he has﻿—um﻿—a particular grudge against me, and being what he is, it would not be long before he discovered that I﻿—er﻿—I﻿—am rather attached to you, Miss﻿—Margaret.”

A light dawned on her, and her whole attitude toward him changed as she gripped his arm.

“You mean, you want me out of London in case something happens? But what could happen? He’s in Broadmoor, isn’t he?”

Mr. Reeder scratched his chin and looked up at the roof of the cab.

“He escaped a week ago﻿—hum! He is, I think, in London at this moment.”

===Chinese===
“弗拉克先生是个非常记仇的人，”他说，“一个非常出色的人——我讨厌承认这一点。而他对我——呃——怀着一份特别的怨毒；以他那样的人，要不了多久就会发现，我——呃——我——对您相当有情意，——玛格丽特小姐。”

她恍然大悟，攥住他的胳膊，对他的整个态度都变了。

“您的意思是，您要我离开伦敦，以防出什么事？可又能出什么事呢？他在布罗德莫尔，不是吗？”

里德先生挠了挠下巴，抬眼望着车篷顶。

“他一星期前逃掉了——嗯！这会儿，我想他就在伦敦。”

===Original===
Margaret Belman gasped.

“Does this Italian﻿—this Ravini man﻿—know?”

“He does not know,” said Mr. Reeder carefully, “but I think he will learn﻿—yes, I think he will learn.”

A week later, after Margaret Belman had gone, with some misgivings, to take up her new appointment, all Mr. Reeder’s doubts as to the location of John Flack were dissipated.

---

===Chinese===
玛格丽特·贝尔曼倒抽一口冷气。

“那个意大利人——那个拉维尼——知道吗？”

“他不知道，”里德先生字斟句酌地说，“不过我想他会知道的——对，我想他会知道的。”

一星期后，玛格丽特·贝尔曼怀着几分忐忑走马上任去了；而里德先生关于约翰·弗拉克行踪的一切疑虑，也都烟消云散。

---

===Original===
There was some slight disagreement between Margaret Belman and Mr. Reeder, and it happened at lunch on the day she left London. It started in fun﻿—not that Mr. Reeder was ever kittenish﻿—by a certain suggestion she made. Mr. Reeder demurred. How she ever summoned the courage to tell him he was old-fashioned, Margaret never knew﻿—but she did.

“Of course you could shave them off,” she said scornfully. “It would make you look ten years younger.”

“I don’t think, my dear﻿—Miss﻿—um﻿—Margaret, that I wish to look ten years younger,” said Mr. Reeder.

A certain tenseness followed, and she went down to Siltbury feeling a little uncomfortable. Yet her heart warmed to him as she realized that his anxiety to get her out of London was dictated by a desire for her own safety. It was not until she was nearing her destination that she realized that he himself was in no ordinary danger. She must write and tell him she was sorry. She wondered who the Flacks were; the name was familiar to her, though in the days of their activity she gave little or no attention to people of their kind.

===Chinese===
玛格丽特·贝尔曼和里德先生之间起了一点小小的不愉快，就发生在她离开伦敦那天的午餐桌上。事情由玩笑开头——倒不是说里德先生什么时候有过猫儿似的轻佻——她提了个建议，里德先生表示反对。她到底从哪里来的胆量，说他老派，玛格丽特自己也永远不明白——可她还是说了。

“您当然可以把它们剃掉，”她轻蔑地说，“那会使您看上去年轻十岁。”

“我可不觉得，亲爱的——小姐——呃——玛格丽特，我想要年轻十岁。”里德先生说。

随后便有了几分尴尬；她南下西尔特伯里时，心里颇有些不自在。然而，一明白他急着要她离开伦敦全是为她的安全着想，她心里便对他热乎起来。直到快到目的地时，她才意识到，他自己正处在非同小可的危险之中。她一定得写信告诉他，她很抱歉。她纳闷弗拉克一家人究竟是什么来头；这名字她耳熟，只是当年他们闹得正凶的时候，她对这类人物极少留意，或者根本不留意的。

===Original===
Mr. Daver, looking more impish than ever, gave her a brief interview on her arrival. It was he who took her to her office and very briefly explained her duties. They were neither heavy nor complicated, and she was relieved to discover that she had practically nothing whatever to do with the management of Larmes Keep. That was in the efficient hands of Mrs. Burton.

The staff of the hotel were housed in two cottages about a quarter of a mile from the Keep, only Mrs. Burton living on the premises.

“This keeps us more select,” said Mr. Daver. “Servants are an abominable nuisance. You agree with me? I thought you would. If they are needed in the night, both cottages have telephones, and Grainger, the porter, has a passkey to the outer door. That is an excellent arrangement﻿—of which you approve? I am sure you do.”

Conversation with Mr. Daver was a little superfluous. He supplied his own answers to all questions.

He was leaving the office when she remembered his great study.

===Chinese===
达弗先生的样子比以往更像个小精灵，在她抵达时同她简短地谈了一次。正是他领她去了办公室，把她的职责三言两语讲了一遍。职责既不繁重也不复杂；叫她宽慰的是，她发现拉尔姆宅的经营管理，她几乎完全用不着插手。那一切都有效地握在伯顿太太手里。

旅馆的员工住在大约四分之一英里外的两座小屋里，只有伯顿太太住在宅子里。

“这样我们便更清高些，”达弗先生说，“仆人是可恶的麻烦。您同意我的看法吧？我就知道您会的。夜里若用得着他们，两座小屋都有电话，门房格雷杰（Grainger）手里有大门的万能钥匙。这个安排好极了——您赞成吧？我肯定您赞成。”

同达弗先生交谈是多余的。所有问题他都自问自答。

他正要离开办公室，她想起了他那间大书房。

===Original===
“Mr. Daver, do you know anything about the Flacks?”

He frowned.

“Flax? Let me see, what is flax?”

She spelled the name.

===Chinese===
“达弗先生，您知道弗拉克一家吗？”

他皱起眉头。

“亚麻？让我想想，亚麻是个什么东西？”

她把那个名字的字母拼了一遍。

===Original===
“A friend of mine told me about them the other day,” she said. “I thought you would know the name. They are a gang of criminals.”

“Flack! To be sure﻿—to be sure! Dear me, how very interesting! Are you also a criminologist? John Flack, George Flack, Augustus Flack”﻿—he spoke rapidly, ticking them off on his long, tobacco-stained fingers. “John Flack is in a criminal lunatic asylum; his two brothers﻿ ﻿… Terrible fellows, terrible, terrible fellows! What a marvellous institution is our police force! How wonderful is Scotland Yard! You agree with me? I was sure you would. Flack!” He frowned and shook his head. “I thought of dealing with the Flacks in a short monograph, but my data is not complete. Do you know them?”

She shook her head smilingly.

“No, I haven’t that advantage.”

===Chinese===
“前几天我有位朋友跟我谈起过他们，”她说，“我以为您会知道这个姓氏。他们是一伙罪犯。”

“弗拉克！没错——没错！天哪，多有意思！您也是位犯罪学家吗？约翰·弗拉克、乔治·弗拉克、奥古斯塔斯·弗拉克”——他语速飞快，用那几根被烟草熏黄的长手指逐一数着。“约翰·弗拉克在一家刑事疯人院里；他的两个兄弟……可怕的家伙，可怕，太可怕的家伙了！我们的警察队伍是多么了不起的机构！苏格兰场是多么神奇！您同意我的看法吧？我就知道您会的。弗拉克！”他皱眉摇头。“我本想在一篇短篇专论里写写弗拉克一家，可惜我的资料不全。您认识他们吗？”

她含笑摇了摇头。

“不，我还没有这份荣幸。”

===Original===
“Terrible creatures,” said Mr. Daver. “Amazing creatures. Who is your friend, Miss Belman? I should like to meet him. Perhaps he could tell me something more about them.”

Margaret received the suggestion with dismay.

“Oh, no, you’re not likely to meet him,” she said hurriedly, “and I don’t think he would talk even if you met him﻿—perhaps it was indiscreet of me to mention him at all.”

The conversation must have weighed on Mr. Daver’s mind, for just as she was leaving her office that night for her room, a very tired girl, he knocked at the door, opened it at her invitation, and stood in the doorway.

===Chinese===
“可怕的生灵，”达弗先生说，“惊人的生灵。您那位朋友是谁，贝尔曼小姐？我倒想见见他。说不定他能再告诉我一些他们的事。”

这个提议让玛格丽特着实惊慌。

“哦，不，您不大可能见到他，”她急忙说，“就算见到了，我想他也不会说的——也许我根本就不该提起他，太欠考虑了。”

这番谈话一定压在了达弗先生心上，因为那天夜里，她正要离开办公室回房去——一个疲惫不堪的姑娘——他敲了敲门，应她一声请进便推开门，站在门口。

===Original===
“I have been going into the records of the Flacks,” he said, “and it is surprising how little information there is. I have a newspaper cutting which says that John Flack is dead. He was the man who went into Broadmoor. Is he dead?”

Margaret shook her head.

“I couldn’t tell you,” she replied untruthfully. “I only heard a casual reference to him.”

Mr. Daver scratched his round chin.

===Chinese===
“我一直在查阅弗拉克一家的案卷，”他说，“资料少得惊人。我有一份剪报，说约翰·弗拉克死了。他就是进布罗德莫尔的那个人。他死了吗？”

玛格丽特摇了摇头。

“这我说不上来，”她答道，说的不是实话，“我只是偶然听人提起过他。”

达弗先生挠了挠他圆圆的下巴。

===Original===
“I thought possibly somebody might have told you a few facts which you, so to speak, a laywoman”﻿—he giggled﻿—“might have regarded as unimportant, but which I﻿—”

He hesitated expectantly.

“That is all I know, Mr. Daver,” said Margaret.

She slept soundly that night; the distant hush-hush of the waves as they rolled up the long beach of Siltbury Bay lulling her to dreamless slumber.

===Chinese===
“我原想，说不定有谁跟您讲过一些事实，而您，可以说，作为一个门外汉”——他咯咯笑了——“也许当作无关紧要，可是我——”

他满怀期待地顿住了。

“我知道的就这些，达弗先生。”玛格丽特说。

那一夜她睡得很沉；波浪卷上西尔特伯里湾长长的海滩，那遥远的沙沙声哄她进入了无梦的睡眠。

===Original===
Her duties did not begin till after breakfast, which she had in her office, and the largest part was the checking of the accounts. Apparently, Mrs. Burton attended to that side of the management, and it was only at the month’s end, when checks were to be drawn, that her work was likely to be heavy. In the main, her day was taken up with correspondence. There were some one hundred and forty applicants for her post, who had to be answered; there were, in addition, a number of letters from persons who desired accommodation at Larmes Keep. All these had to be taken to Mr. Daver, and it was remarkable how fastidious he was. For example:

“The Reverend John Quinton? No, no; we have one parson in the house, that is enough. Tell him we are very sorry but we are full up. Mrs. Bagley wishes to bring her daughter? Certainly not! I cannot have children distracting me with their noise. You agree? I see you do. Who is this woman﻿—‘coming for a rest cure’? That means she’s ill. I cannot have Larmes Keep turned into a sanitarium. You may tell them all that there will be no accommodations until after Christmas. After Christmas they can all come﻿—I am going abroad.”

The evenings were her own. She could, if she desired, go into Siltbury, which boasted two cinemas and a pierrot party, and Mr. Daver put the hotel car at her disposal for the purpose.

She preferred, however, to wander through the grounds. The estate was much larger than she had supposed. Behind, to the south of the house, it extended for half a mile, the boundary to the east being represented by the cliffs, along which a breast-high rubble wall had been built, and with excellent reason, for here the cliff fell sheer two hundred feet to the rocks below. At one place there had been a little landslide; the wall had been carried away and the gap had been temporarily filled by a wooden fence. Some attempt had been made to create a nine-hole golf course, she saw, as she wandered round, but evidently Mr. Daver had grown tired of this enterprise, for the greens were knee-high in waving grasses.

===Chinese===
她的职责到早餐之后才开始——早餐是在办公室里用的——而大部分活计是核对账目。显然，经营上的这一面由伯顿太太打理，只有到月底开支票的时候，她的活计才可能繁重起来。大体而言，她的一天都耗在通信上。求职的申请大约有一百四十份，都得一一回复；此外还有若干来信，是想在拉尔姆宅落脚的人写的。这些统统得送到达弗先生那里去，而他挑剔得出奇。比如：

“约翰·昆顿牧师（Reverend John Quinton）？不行，不行；家里已经有一位牧师，足够了。告诉他我们非常抱歉，客满了。巴格利太太（Mrs. Bagley）想带女儿来？万万不可！我不能让孩子们的吵闹分我的心。您同意吧？我看得出您同意。这个女人是谁——‘来做静养疗法的’？那就是说她有病。我不能让拉尔姆宅变成一所疗养院。您可以告诉他们大家，圣诞节以前不会有空房。圣诞节以后他们只管都来——我要出国。”

晚上归她自己支配。她要是愿意，可以进西尔特伯里去，那镇子以两家电影院和一家丑角戏班自夸，为此达弗先生还把旅馆的汽车交给她随意使用。

不过她更喜欢在园子里漫步。庄园比她原先设想的大得多。宅子背后朝南的那一片，纵深有半英里，东面的边界便是悬崖，沿崖筑了一道齐胸高的石垒墙，而且理由十足，因为这里的悬崖笔直下落两百英尺，直抵底下的礁石。有一处发生过小小的滑坡，墙体塌掉了，豁口临时用木栅栏堵着。她一路闲逛，看出有人试过开辟一座九洞高尔夫球场，可达弗先生显然对这桩事业厌了倦了，因为球洞区的草已长到膝盖高，随风摆动。

===Original===
At the southwest corner of the house, and distant about a hundred yards, was a big clump of rhododendrons, and this she explored, following a twisting path that led to the heart of the bushes. Quite unexpectedly she came upon an old well. The brickwork about it was in ruins; the well itself was boarded in. On the weather-beaten roof-piece above the windlass was a small wooden notice board, evidently fixed for the enlightenment of visitors:

> “This well was used from 935 to 1794. It was filled in by the present owners of the property in May, 1914, 135 cartloads of rock and gravel being used for the purpose.”

It was a pleasant occupation, standing by that ancient well and picturing the collar serfs and barefooted peasants who through the ages had stood where she was standing. As she came out of the bushes she saw the pale-faced Olga Crewe.

Margaret had not spoken either to the Colonel or to the clergyman; either she had avoided them, or they her. Olga Crewe she had not seen, and now she would have turned away, but the girl moved across to intercept her.

===Chinese===
在宅子的西南角，距宅子约一百码，有一大丛杜鹃花，她循着一条蜿蜒的小径，一路探进花丛深处。全然出乎意料，她撞见了一口古井。井周的砖工已经倾颓，井口本身封了木板。绞盘上方那块饱经风雨的顶板上，钉着一块小小的木告示牌，显然是为启迪游客而设：

> “此井使用年代自 935 年至 1794 年。本产业现业主于 1914 年 5 月将其填没，为此共用去石块砾石 135 车。”

站在这口古井旁，想象着历代那些戴颈圈的农奴和赤脚的农夫——他们就站在她此刻站立的地方——是一桩愉快的消遣。她从花丛里出来时，看见了脸色苍白的奥尔加·克鲁。

上校和牧师，玛格丽特都还没有说过话；不是她避着他们，就是他们避着她。奥尔加·克鲁她也没见过，而这会儿她本想转身走开，姑娘却横着走过来拦住了她。

===Original===
“You are the new secretary, aren’t you?”

Her voice was musical, rather alluring. “Custardy” was Margaret’s mental classification.

“Yes, I’m Miss Belman.”

The girl nodded.

===Chinese===
“您就是新来的秘书吧？”

她的嗓音悦耳，相当撩人。“蛋奶冻”——这是玛格丽特心里的归类。

“是的，我是贝尔曼小姐。”

姑娘点了点头。

===Original===
“My name you know, I suppose? Are you going to be terribly bored here?”

“I don’t think so,” smiled Margaret. “It is a beautiful spot.”

The eyes of Olga Crewe surveyed the scene critically.

“I suppose it is﻿—very beautiful, yes, but one gets very tired of beauty after a few years.”

===Chinese===
“我的名字您想必知道了？您在这儿会不会闷得慌？”

“我想不会，”玛格丽特微笑道，“这地方很美。”

奥尔加·克鲁的眼睛挑剔地打量着四下的景色。

“我想是吧——很美，是的，可是过上几年，人对美就腻透了。”

===Original===
Margaret listened in astonishment.

“Have you been here so long?”

“I’ve practically lived here since I was a child. I thought Joe would have told you that: he’s an inveterate old gossip.”

“Joe?” She was puzzled.

“The cab driver, news-gatherer and distributor.”

===Chinese===
玛格丽特愕然听着。

“您在这儿住了这么久？”

“我从小到大，简直就一直住在这儿。我还以为乔（Joe）会把这事告诉您呢：他是个改不了嚼舌根的老家伙。”

“乔？”她给弄糊涂了。

“马车夫呀，新闻的收集商兼分发商。”

===Original===
She looked at Larmes Keep and frowned.

“Do you know what they used to call this place, Miss Belman? The House of Tears﻿—the Château des Larmes.”

“Why ever?” asked Margaret.

Olga Crewe shrugged her pretty shoulders.

===Chinese===
她望着拉尔姆宅，皱起了眉。

“您可知道这地方从前叫什么，贝尔曼小姐？眼泪之宅——法语‘泪之堡’（Château des Larmes）。”

“到底是为什么呀？”玛格丽特问。

奥尔加·克鲁耸了耸她那漂亮的肩膀。

===Original===
“Some sort of tradition, I suppose, that goes back to the days of Baron Augernvert, who built it. The locals have corrupted the name to Larmes Keep. You ought to see the dungeons.”

“Are there dungeons?” asked Margaret in surprise, and Olga nodded. For the first time she seemed amused.

“If you saw them and the chains and the rings in the walls and the stone floors worn thin by bare feet, you might guess how its name arose.”

Margaret stared back toward the Keep. The sun was setting behind it, and silhouetted as it was against the red light there was something ominous and sinister in that dark, squat pile.

===Chinese===
“我想总归是某种传统吧，可以追溯到建宅的奥根弗特男爵（Baron Augernvert）那个年代。当地人把名字叫走了样，成了拉尔姆宅。您真该去看看那些地牢。”

“还有地牢？”玛格丽特惊讶地问。奥尔加点了点头。她头一回像是有了兴致。

“您要是见着那些地牢，见着铁链、墙上的铁环，还有被赤脚磨薄的石地，您也许就猜得出这名字是怎么来的。”

玛格丽特回头望向主堡。太阳正在它背后沉落；它衬着那片红光显出的黑影，使这座黝黑敦实的建筑带上某种不祥与阴森。

===Original===
“How very unpleasant!” she said, and shivered.

Olga Crewe laughed.

“Have you seen the cliffs?” she said, and led the way back to the long wall, and for a quarter of an hour they stood, their arms resting on the parapet, looking down into the gloom.

“You ought to get someone to row you round the face of the cliff. It’s simply honeycombed with caves,” she said. “There’s one at the water’s edge that tunnels right under the Keep. When the tides are unusually high they are flooded. I wonder Daver doesn’t write a book about it.”

===Chinese===
“多叫人难受！”她说着，打了个寒噤。

奥尔加·克鲁笑了。

“您看过那些悬崖了吗？”她说着，领路回到那道长墙边；有一刻钟工夫，她们胳膊搭在矮墙沿上，站着俯望底下的幽暗。

“您该找个人划船带您绕崖壁一圈。那简直让洞窟掏成了蜂窝，”她说，“水边上就有一个，隧道一直通到主堡底下。潮水特别高的时候，洞就让淹了。真奇怪达弗不拿这个写本书。”

===Original===
There was just the faintest hint of a sneer in her tone, but it did not escape Margaret’s attention.

“That must be the entrance,” she said, pointing down to a swirl of water that seemed to run right up to the face of the cliff.

Olga nodded.

“At high tide you wouldn’t notice that,” she said, and then, turning abruptly, she asked the girl if she had seen the bathing pool.

===Chinese===
她的话音里只有一丝极淡的讥诮，却没能逃过玛格丽特的注意。

“那准是入口。”她说，手往下一指，指着一股似乎一直卷到崖壁跟前的洄水。

奥尔加点了点头。

“涨潮的时候您就注意不到它了。”她说；随后她猛地转过身，问姑娘看没看过那座浴池。

===Original===
This was an oblong bath, sheltered by high box hedges and lined throughout with blue tiles; a delightfully inviting plunge.

“Nobody uses it but myself. Daver would die at the thought of jumping in.”

Whenever she referred to Mr. Daver it was in a scarcely veiled tone of contempt. She was not more charitable when she referred to the other guests. As they were nearing the house, Olga said, apropos of nothing:

“I shouldn’t talk too much to Daver if I were you. Let him do the talking: he likes it.”

===Chinese===
那是一座长方形的浴池，有高高的黄杨树篱遮护，通体镶着蓝瓷砖；诱人一跃而入，妙不可言。

“除了我没人用它。一想到跳进去，达弗就会死。”

每逢提起达弗先生，她的口气总是近乎毫不掩饰的轻蔑。提起其他房客时，她也不更厚道些。她们快走到宅子时，奥尔加没头没脑地说：

“我要是您，就不跟达弗多说话。让他说去：他喜欢那个。”

===Original===
“What do you mean?” asked Margaret quietly, but at that moment Olga left her side without any word of farewell and went toward the Colonel, who was standing, a cigar between his teeth, watching their approach.

The House of Tears!

Margaret remembered the title as she was undressing that night, and, despite her self-possession, shivered a little.

===Chinese===
“您这话什么意思？”玛格丽特轻声问；可就在这时，奥尔加丢下她走开了，连句道别的话也没有，朝上校那边去了——上校正站在那里，嘴里叼着雪茄，望着她们走近。

眼泪之宅！

那天夜里更衣时，玛格丽特想起了这个名目；尽管她素来自持，也不禁微微打了个寒噤。

## IV / 四

===Original===
The policeman who stood on the corner where Bennett Street meets Hyde Lane had the world to himself. It was nearing three o’clock on a chilly morning of early fall. The good and bad of Mayfair slept﻿—all, apparently, except Mr. J. G. Reeder, Friend of the Law and Terror of Criminals. Police Officer Dyer saw the yellow light behind the casemented window and smiled benevolently.

The night was so still that when he heard a key turn in a lock, he looked over his shoulder, thinking the noise was from the house immediately behind him. But the door did not move. Instead he saw a woman appear on the top doorstep five houses away.

===Chinese===
站在贝内特街与海德巷交口值岗的警察，独自拥有着整个世界。时近凌晨三点，初秋一个寒气袭人的清晨。梅费尔的好人坏人都睡了——看样子，除了 J. G. 里德先生，法律之友、罪犯之灾，谁都没睡。警员戴尔望见那扇平开窗后面的黄色灯光，和善地微微一笑。

夜是这样静，以致他听见一把钥匙在锁眼里转动时，还当声音出自身后紧挨着的那所宅子，回头望了一眼。可那扇门并没有动。他看见的，是一个女人出现在相隔五户之遥的门前台阶顶上。

===Original===
“Officer!”

The voice was low, cultured, very urgent. He moved more quickly toward her than policemen usually move.

“Anything wrong, miss?”

Her face, he noticed in his worldly way, was “made up”; the cheeks heavily rouged, the lips a startling red for one who was afraid. He supposed her to be pretty in normal circumstances, but was doubtful as to her age. She wore a long black dressing gown, fastened up to her chin. Also he saw that the hand that gripped the railing which flanked the steps glittered in the lights of the street lamps.

===Chinese===
“警官！”

嗓音低沉、文雅，透着十万火急。他朝她走去，步子比警察平素的脚步快得多。

“出什么事了吗，小姐？”

他以老于世故的眼光注意到，她的脸是“化过妆”的：两颊敷着厚厚的胭脂，嘴唇红得吓人，对一个心惊胆战的人来说未免太红。他估摸着她在平常时候该是个漂亮女人，只是拿不准年纪。她穿一件黑色长晨衣，一直扣到下巴。他还看见，她攥着台阶旁栏杆的那只手，在路灯底下闪闪发光。

===Original===
“I don’t know﻿—quite. I am﻿—alone in the﻿—house and I﻿—thought I heard﻿—something.”

Three words to a breath. Obviously she was terrified.

“Haven’t you any servants in the house?”

The constable was surprised, a little shocked.

===Chinese===
“我不知道——敢说。我一个人——待在——宅子里，我——仿佛听见——什么动静。”

一口气三个字。她显然吓坏了。

“家里没有仆人吗？”

警员又惊讶，又有点儿错愕。

===Original===
“No. I only came back from Paris at midnight﻿—we took the house furnished﻿—I think the servants I engaged mistook the date of my return. I am Mrs. Granville Fornese.”

In a dim way he remembered the name. It had that value of familiarity which makes even the most assured hesitate to deny acquaintance. It sounded grand, too﻿—the name of a Somebody. And Bennett Street was a place where Somebodies live.

The officer peered into the dark hall.

“If you would put the light on, madam, I will look round.”

She shook her head; he almost felt the shiver of her.

===Chinese===
“没有。我半夜才从巴黎回来——宅子是连带家具租下的——我想我雇的仆人们把日子弄错了。我是格兰维尔·福尔内塞太太。”

他隐隐约约记得这个名字。它自带一种熟稔的分量，叫哪怕最有把握的人也不好意思一口咬定不认识。它听着也气派——是一位大人物的名字。而贝内特街，正是个大人物们住的地方。

警员朝黑黢黢的门厅里张望。

“夫人，劳您把灯打开，我进去四下看看。”

她摇了摇头；他几乎感觉得到她身上的战栗。

===Original===
“The lights aren’t working. That is what frightened me. They were quite all right when I went to bed at one o’clock. Something woke me﻿—I don’t know what﻿—and I switched on the lamp by the side of my bed, but there was no light. I keep a little portable battery lamp in my bag. I found this and turned it on.”

She stopped, set her teeth in a mirthless smile. Police Officer Dyer saw the dark eyes were staringly wide.

“I saw﻿—I don’t know what it was﻿—just a patch of black, like somebody crouching by the wall. Then it disappeared. And the door of my room was wide open. I closed and locked it when I went to bed.”

===Chinese===
“灯全不亮了。吓着我的正是这个。我一点钟上床时还好好的。什么东西把我弄醒了——不知道是什么——我去开床边的灯，灯却不亮。我包里带着一盏小小的电池便携灯。我摸出它来，拧亮了。”

她顿了顿，咬着牙，绽出一个毫无笑意的笑。警员戴尔看见那双黑眼睛瞪得滚圆。

“我看见——不知道那是什么——就是一团黑影，像有人蹲在墙边。随后它就不见了。而我房间的门敞得大开。我上床时明明关好、上了锁的。”

===Original===
The officer pushed open the door wider, sent a white beam of light along the passage. There was a small hall table against the wall, where a telephone instrument stood. Striding into the hall, he took up the instrument and lifted the hook: the phone was dead.

“Does this﻿—”

So far he got with the question, and then stopped. From somewhere above him he heard a faint but sustained creak﻿—the sound of a foot resting on a faulty floor board. Mrs. Fornese was still standing in the open doorway, and he went back to her.

===Chinese===
警员把门推得更开些，一道白色光柱沿走廊扫去。墙边有一张小小的门厅桌，上面摆着电话机。他大步走进门厅，拿起话机掀起了挂钩：电话毫无声息。

“这是不是——”

问题只问到这里，他便停住了。他听见头顶上某个地方传来一阵轻微却持续的吱呀声——是一只脚踩上松动地板的声音。福尔内塞太太仍站在敞开的门口，他便退回到她那里去。

===Original===
“Have you a key to this door?” he asked, and she shook her head.

He felt along the inner surface of the lock and found a stop-catch, pushed it up.

“I’ll have to phone from somewhere. You’d better﻿—”

What had she best do? He was a plain police constable and was confronted with a delicate situation.

“Is there anywhere you could go﻿—friends?”

===Chinese===
“这扇门的钥匙您有吗？”他问。她摇了摇头。

他顺着锁的内侧摸了一遍，摸到一处止动卡舌，把它推了上去。

“我只好到别处去打电话了。您最好——”

她最好做什么呢？他不过是个普通警员，眼下却遇上了难办的局面。

“您有没有什么地方可去——朋友家？”

===Original===
“No.” There was no indecision in that word. And then: “Doesn’t Mr. Reeder live opposite? Somebody told me﻿—”

In the house opposite a light showed. Mr. Dyer surveyed the lighted window dubiously. It stood for the elegant apartment of one who held a post superior to chief constables. No. 7 Bennett Street had been at a recent period converted into flats, and into one of these Mr. Reeder had moved from his suburban home. Why he should take a flat in that exclusive and interesting neighbourhood, nobody knew. He was credited by criminals with being fabulously rich; he was undoubtedly a snug man.

===Chinese===
“没有。”这一个字里没有半点迟疑。接着又说：“里德先生不是就住在对面吗？有人跟我说过——”

对面那所宅子里亮着一扇窗。戴尔先生满腹狐疑地打量着那扇亮灯的窗。它代表的是一位职位比郡警察局长还高的人的雅致公寓。贝内特街 7 号是不久前才改建成公寓楼的，里德先生从郊外的住宅搬进了其中一套。他为什么偏要到那个非富贵莫入、耐人寻味的街区来住公寓，谁也说不上来。罪犯们传言他家财万贯，富得离奇；而他无疑是个安逸妥帖的人。

===Original===
The constable hesitated, searched his pocket for the smallest coin of the realm, and, leaving the lady on the doorstep, crossed the road and tossed a ha’penny to the window. A second later the casement window was pushed open.

“Excuse me, Mr. Reeder, could I see you for a second?”

The head and shoulders disappeared, and in a very short time Mr. Reeder appeared in the doorway. He was so fully dressed that he might have been expecting the summons. The frock coat was tightly buttoned, on the back of his head his flat-topped felt hat, on his nose the pince-nez through which he never looked were askew.

===Chinese===
警员犹豫了一下，在口袋里摸索王国通行的最小面额硬币，把那位太太留在台阶上，穿过马路，将一枚半便士抛向那扇窗。一秒钟后，窗扉推开了。

“劳驾，里德先生，能同您说句话吗？”

那颗脑袋和肩膀缩了回去，不多时，里德先生便出现在门口。他衣裳穿得齐齐整整，倒像一直在等着这声召唤似的。常礼服的扣子一颗颗扣得严实，扁顶毡帽扣在后脑勺上，鼻梁上架着他从不透过它看东西的那副夹鼻眼镜，还歪着。

===Original===
“Anything wrong, constable?” he asked gently.

“Could I use your phone? There is a lady over there﻿—Mrs. Fornese﻿—alone﻿—heard somebody in the house. I heard it, too﻿—”

He heard a short scream﻿—a crash﻿—and jumped round. The door of No. 4 was closed. Mrs. Fornese had disappeared.

===Chinese===
“出什么事了吗，警员？”他温和地问。

“可以用一下您的电话吗？那边有位太太——福尔内塞太太——独自一人——听见宅子里有人。我也听见了——”

他听见一声短促的尖叫——一声碰撞——霍地转过身去。 4 号的门已经关上。福尔内塞太太不见了。

===Original===
In six strides Mr. Reeder had crossed the road and was at the door. Stooping, he pressed in the flap of the letter box and listened. No noise but the ticking of a clock﻿—a faint sighing sound.

“Hum!” said Mr. Reeder, scratching his long nose thoughtfully. “Hum﻿—would you be so kind as to tell me all about this﻿—um﻿—happening?”

The police constable repeated the story, more coherently.

“You fastened the spring lock so that it would not move? A wise precaution.”

===Chinese===
里德先生六大步跨过马路，到了那扇门前。他俯下身，按开信箱口的盖板，侧耳倾听。除了时钟的滴答——还有一声轻微的、叹息似的声响——什么动静也没有。

“哼！”里德先生若有所思地搔着长鼻子说。“哼——可否劳驾把这件——呃——变故的始末跟我说一说？”

警员把经过又讲了一遍，这回讲得更有条理。

“您把弹簧锁掣住，叫它动弹不得？稳妥的防备。”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder frowned. Without another word he crossed the street and disappeared into his flat. There was a small drawer at the back of his writing desk. This he unlocked and, taking out a leather holdall, unrolled this and selecting three curious steel instruments that were not unlike small hooks, fitted one into a wooden handle and returned to the constable.

“This, I fear, is﻿—I will not say ‘unlawful,’ for a gentleman of my position is incapable of an unlawful act﻿—shall I say ‘unusual’?”

All the time he talked in his soft, apologetic way, he was working at the lock, turning the instrument first one way and then the other. Presently, with a click, the lock turned and Mr. Reeder pushed open the door.

===Chinese===
里德先生皱起眉头。他一言不发地穿过街去，消失在自己的公寓里。他的写字台后头有个小抽屉。他开了锁，取出一只皮质工具袋，摊开来，从三件模样古怪、颇像小钩子的钢制家伙里挑出一枚，装上一根木柄，回到警员跟前。

“这东西，恐怕是——我不愿说它‘不合法’，因为像我这样身份的绅士做不出不合法的事来——要不要说它‘不寻常’呢？”

他一面用那软绵绵、赔着小心的口气说着话，一面摆弄着锁，把那家什一会儿向这边转，一会儿向那边转。不多时，咔哒一声，锁开了，里德先生推开了门。

===Original===
“I think I had better borrow your lamp﻿—thank you.”

He took the electric lamp from the constable’s hand and flung a white circle of light into the hall. There was no sign of life. He cast the beam up the stairs, and, stooping his head, listened. There came to his ear no sound, and noiselessly he stepped farther into the hall.

The passage continued beyond the foot of the stairs, and at the end was a door which apparently gave to the domestic quarters of the house. To the policeman’s surprise, it was the door which Mr. Reeder examined. He turned the handle, but the door did not move, and, stooping, he squinted at the keyhole.

===Chinese===
“我想我最好借您的灯用一用——谢谢您。”

他从警员手里接过电筒，一环白光投进门厅。毫无人的踪迹。他把光柱扫上楼梯，低下头听了听。耳边没有任何声息，他便悄然无声地再往门厅深处走去。

走廊过了楼梯脚还往前去，尽头有一扇门，看样子通向宅子的仆役用房。叫警察吃惊的是，里德先生细细察看的正是这扇门。他拧了拧把手，门纹丝不动，他便俯下身，眯眼往锁眼里瞧。

===Original===
“There was somebody﻿—upstairs,” began the policeman with respectful hesitation.

“There was somebody upstairs,” repeated Mr. Reeder absently. “You heard a creaky board, I think.”

He came slowly back to the foot of the stairs and looked up. Then he cast his lamp along the floor of the hall.

“No sawdust,” he said, speaking to himself, “so it can’t be *that*.”

===Chinese===
“方才楼上——有人。”警察恭恭敬敬又吞吞吐吐地开口。

“方才楼上有人。”里德先生心不在焉地重复道。“你听见的是一块吱呀作响的地板，我想。”

他慢慢踱回楼梯脚下，抬头望了望。然后他把手电沿着门厅的地板扫了一遍。

“没有锯末，”他自言自语道，“所以不会是*那个*。”

===Original===
“Shall I go up, sir?” said the policeman, and his foot was on the lower tread when Mr. Reeder, displaying unexpected strength in so weary-looking a man, pushed him back.

“I think not, constable,” he said firmly. “If the lady is upstairs she will have heard our voices. But the lady is not upstairs.”

“Do you think she’s in the kitchen, sir?” asked the puzzled policeman.

Mr. Reeder shook his head sadly.

===Chinese===
“我上去看看吗，长官？”警察说。他一只脚刚踏上最下一级台阶，里德先生——这模样疲惫的人竟使出叫人意想不到的力气——一把将他推了回去。

“我看不必了，警员。”他语气坚决地说。“太太若在楼上，她早听见我们的说话声了。可这位太太不在楼上。”

“您是说她躲在厨房里，长官？”大惑不解的警察问。

里德先生难过地摇了摇头。

===Original===
“Alas! how few modern women spend their time in a kitchen!” he said, and made an impatient clucking noise, but whether this was a protest against the falling off of woman’s domestic qualities, or whether he “tchk’d” for some other reason, it was difficult to say, for he was a very preoccupied man.

He swung the lamp back to the door.

“I thought so,” he said, with a note of relief in his voice. “There are two walking sticks in the hall stand. Will you get one of them, constable?”

Wondering, the officer obeyed, and came back, handing a long cherrywood stick with a crooked handle to Mr. Reeder, who examined it in the light of his lamp.

===Chinese===
“唉！如今肯把工夫花在厨房里的女人，是多么少啊！”他说着，发出一声不耐烦的啧啧。可这一声究竟是抗议女性持家本领的每况愈下，还是另有缘故才“啧”的，就难说了，因为他是个心事重重的人。

他把手电转回那扇门。

“果然不出我所料，”他说，声音里透出一丝宽慰。“门厅的立架上插着两根手杖。劳驾取一根来，警员。”

警员满腹狐疑地照办了，回来时把一根柄子弯曲的樱桃木长杖递给里德先生。里德先生就着自己手电的光端详它。

===Original===
“Dust-covered and left by the previous owner. The spike in place of the ferrule shows that it was purchased in Switzerland. Probably you are not interested in detective stories and have never read of the gentleman whose method I am plagiarizing?”

“No, sir,” said the mystified officer.

Mr. Reeder examined the stick again.

“It is a thousand pities that it is not a fishing rod,” he said. “Will you stay here, and don’t move.”

===Chinese===
“蒙着灰，是先前的房主落下的。杖尾装的不是铁箍而是尖头，可见是在瑞士买的。多半你对侦探小说不感兴趣，没读到过那位先生吧——我此刻剽窃的正是他的手法？”

“没有，长官。”一头雾水的警员说。

里德先生又把那根手杖端详了一遍。

“万分可惜，它不是一根钓鱼竿。”他说。“你站在这里，别动。”

===Original===
And then he began to crawl up the stairs on his knees, waving his stick in front of him in the most eccentric manner. He held it up, lifting the full length of his arm, and as he crawled upward he struck at imaginary obstacles. Higher and higher he went, silhouetted against the reflected light of the lamp he carried, and Police Constable Dyer watched him open-mouthed.

“Don’t you think I’d better﻿—”

He got as far as this when the thing happened. There was an explosion that deafened him; the air was suddenly filled with flying clouds of smoke and dust; he heard the crackle of wood and the pungent scent of something burning. Dazed and stupefied, he stood stock still, gaping up at Mr. Reeder, who was sitting on a stair, picking little splinters of wood from his coat.

===Chinese===
接着他便跪在楼梯上往上爬，手里的杖以最古怪的方式在身前挥舞。他把杖高高举起，整条手臂伸得笔直，一边往上爬，一边朝假想中的障碍物劈打。他越爬越高，身影衬在他自己提灯的反光里，警员戴尔看得目瞪口呆。

“您看我是不是最好——”

话说到这儿，事情就发生了。一声爆炸震得他耳朵发聋；空气里霎时腾起乱飞的烟尘；他听见木头的爆裂声，闻到什么东西烧焦的刺鼻气味。他晕头转向、呆若木鸡地钉在原地，张着嘴仰望里德先生——后者正坐在一级台阶上，从外衣上摘下一根根小小的木刺。

===Original===
“I think you may come up in perfect safety,” said Mr. Reeder, with great calmness.

“What﻿—what was it?” asked the officer.

The enemy of criminals was dusting his hat tenderly, though this the officer could not see.

“You may come up.”

===Chinese===
“我想你现在上来，包管安全无虞。”里德先生说，镇定极了。

“那——那是什么？”警员问。

罪犯的克星正温存地掸着他的帽子，不过这一点警员看不见。

“你可以上来了。”

===Original===
Police Constable Dyer ran up the stairs and followed the other along the broad landing till he stopped and focused in the light of his lamp a queer-looking and obviously homemade spring gun, the muzzle of which was trained through the banisters so that it covered the stairs up which he had ascended.

“There was,” said Mr. Reeder carefully, “a piece of black thread stretched across the stairs, so that any person who bulged or broke that thread was certain to fire the gun.”

“But﻿—but the lady?”

Mr. Reeder coughed.

===Chinese===
警员戴尔奔上楼梯，跟着对方沿宽阔的楼梯平台走去，最后停在一处，用手电照定了一支样子古怪、显然是自制的弹簧枪：枪口穿过栏杆的空隙瞄着，正覆盖着他刚才上来的那段楼梯。

“原先，”里德先生字斟句酌地说，“有一根黑线横在楼梯上，谁只要碰凸了线、碰断了线，就必定打响这杆枪。”

“可是——可是那位太太呢？”

里德先生咳了一声。

===Original===
“I do not think she is in the house,” he said, ever so gently. “I rather imagine that she went through the back. There is a back entrance to the mews, is there not? And that by this time she is a long way from the house. I sympathize with her﻿—this little incident has occurred too late for the morning newspapers and she will have to wait for the sporting editions before she learns that I am still alive.”

The police officer drew a long breath.

“I think I’d better report this, sir.”

“I think you had,” sighed Mr. Reeder. “And will you ring up Inspector Simpson and tell him that, if he comes this way, I should like to see him?”

===Chinese===
“我看她不在这宅子里了。”他说得极其温柔。“我倒愿意设想，她是从后头走的。通马厩巷有一条后门，对不对？此刻她离这宅子怕已经很远了。我真是同情她——这件小小的意外来得太晚，赶不上早报了，她要等到下午的体育版，才能得知我还活着。”

警员长长吸了一口气。

“我看这事我还是上报的好，长官。”

“我看也是。”里德先生叹了口气。“还劳驾打电话给辛普森督察，告诉他，他要是打这条路过来，我想见见他。”

===Original===
Again the policeman hesitated.

“Don’t you think we’d better search the house? They may have done away with this woman.”

Mr. Reeder shook his head.

“They have not done away with any woman,” he said decisively. “The only thing they have done away with is one of Mr. Simpson’s pet theories.”

===Chinese===
警察又犹豫起来。

“我们是不是最好搜一搜这宅子？他们兴许已经把这位太太害了。”

里德先生摇了摇头。

“他们谁也没害。”他斩钉截铁地说。“唯一被害掉的，是辛普森先生心爱的一条理论。”

===Original===
“But, Mr. Reeder, why did this lady come to the door?”

Mr. Reeder patted him benignantly on the arm, as a mother might pat a child who asks a foolish question.

“The lady had been standing at the door for half an hour,” he said gently; “on and off for half an hour, constable, hoping against hope, one imagines, that she would attract my attention. But I was looking at her from a room that was not﻿—er﻿—illuminated. I did not show myself because I﻿—er﻿—have a very keen desire to live!”

On this baffling note Mr. Reeder went into his house.

===Chinese===
“可是，里德先生，这位太太干吗要跑到门口来呢？”

里德先生慈祥地拍了拍他的胳膊，像母亲拍一个问了傻话的孩子。

“那位太太在门口站了半个钟头了。”他温和地说。“断断续续站了半个钟头，警员。可以想见，她抱着万一的指望，盼着能引得我注意。可我是在一间没有——呃——点灯的房间里瞧着她。我不露面，是因为我——呃——求生的愿望非常强烈！”

丢下这句叫人摸不着头脑的话，里德先生进了自家的宅门。

## V / 五

===Original===
Mr. Reeder sat at his ease, wearing a pair of grotesquely painted velvet slippers, a cigarette hanging from his lips, and explained to the detective inspector who had called in the early hours of the morning his reason for adopting a certain conclusion.

“I do not imagine for one moment that it was my friend Ravini. He is less subtle, in addition to which he has little or no intelligence. You will find that this coup has been planned for months, though it has only been put into execution today. No. 4 Bennett Street is the property of an old gentleman who spends most of his time in Italy. He has been in the habit of letting the house furnished for years; in fact, it was only vacated a month ago.”

“You think, then,” said the puzzled Simpson, “That the people, whoever they were, rented the house﻿—”

===Chinese===
里德先生好整以暇地坐着，脚上趿一双绘满古怪花纹的天鹅绒拖鞋，唇间叼着一支烟，正向清晨时分登门造访的督察解释，自己何以得出某一结论。

“我压根儿不认为那会是我的朋友拉维尼。他远没有那份精细，何况他也没多少头脑可言。你会发现，这桩买卖筹划已有数月之久，只是到今天才付诸实行。贝内特街（Bennett Street）4 号的产业，属于一位大半年头待在意大利的老先生。多年来他惯于把宅子连家具一并出租；说起来，一个月前房客才刚刚退租。”

“那么您认为，”一脸困惑的辛普森说，“那些人，不管是谁，是把宅子租了下来——”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder shook his head.

“Even that I doubt,” he said. “They have probably an order in view, and in some way got rid of the caretaker. They knew I would be at home last night, because I am always at home﻿—um﻿—on most nights since﻿—” Mr. Reeder coughed in his embarrassment. “A young friend of mine has recently left London﻿—I do not like going out alone.”

And to Simpson’s horror, a pinkish flush suffused the sober countenance of Mr. Reeder.

===Chinese===
里德先生摇了摇头。

“连这个我也怀疑，”他说，“他们多半是瞅准了一桩活计，这才想法子撵走了看房人。他们知道我昨晚会待在家里，因为我总是待在家里——呃——近来大多数晚上都是——”里德先生窘得咳嗽起来。“我有位年轻的朋友近日离开了伦敦——我不喜欢独自出门。”

而令辛普森骇然的是，里德先生那副持重的脸上竟泛起一层淡淡的红晕。

===Original===
“A few weeks ago,” he went on, with a pitiable attempt at airiness, “I used to dine out, attend a concert or one of those exquisite melodramas which have such an appeal to me.”

“Whom do you suspect?” interrupted Simpson, who had not been called from his bed in the middle of the night to discuss the virtue of melodrama. “The Gregorys or the Donovans?” He named two groups that had excellent reason to be annoyed with Mr. Reeder and his methods.

J. G. Reeder shook his head.

===Chinese===
“几个星期以前，”他接着说，可怜巴巴地想装出一副轻快的口吻，“我还常常在外面吃饭，去听听音乐会，或者看看那些妙不可言的情节剧——它们于我有莫大的吸引力。”

“您疑心的是谁？”辛普森打断了他——他可不是深更半夜被人从床上叫起来讨论情节剧的优劣的。“是格雷戈里帮，还是多诺万帮？”他点出的是两个团伙，两者都有充足的理由怨恨里德先生和他的手段。

J. G. 里德摇了摇头。

===Original===
“Neither,” he said. “I think﻿—indeed I am sure﻿—that we must go back to ancient history for the cause.”

Simpson opened his eyes.

“Not Flack?” he asked incredulously. “He’s hiding﻿—he wouldn’t start anything so soon.”

===Chinese===
“都不是，”他说，“我想——的确，我敢肯定——要找原因，得回溯到古老的历史里去。”

辛普森睁大了眼睛。

“不会是弗拉克吧？”他不肯相信地问，“他正躲着呢——他不至于这么快就重新开张。”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder nodded.

“John Flack. Who else could have planned such a thing? The art of it!
