“I was thinking of Miss Belman,” interrupted J. G. Reeder, “who is a friend of mine and would, moreover, take the greatest possible care of that delicate mechanism.”
Daver was silent for a moment, turning this over in his mind.
“Would it not be better if a man﻿—and the last train down﻿—”

===Chinese===
“正是那个贼，”里德先生应道，“那是个叫人心惊的人物，我不打算让这事就这么撂下。算我走运，我在房门的格板上发现了一枚指纹。”
他看见达弗的脸色变了。
“我虽说发现了一枚指纹，其实是发现了一个样子像指纹的东西；只有借助指纹镜查验，我才能拿得准。不巧的是，我没想到会用到那样的器械，不知您能不能派个人去伦敦，替我把它带下来？”
“万分乐意，”达弗说，口吻里却少了热忱，“派个男仆——”
“我想到的是贝尔曼小姐，”J. G. 里德插嘴道，“她是我的一位朋友，而且，那样精巧的机件，她必会万分小心地照管。”
达弗沉吟了片刻，在心里掂量这件事。
“派个男人去岂不更妥——还有下行的末班车——”

===Original===
“She could come down by car; I can arrange that.”
Mr. Reeder fumbled his chin.
“Perhaps it would be better if I brought down a couple of men from the Yard.”
“No, no,” said Daver quickly. “You can send Miss Belman. I haven’t the slightest objection. I will tell her.”
Mr. Reeder looked at his watch.
“The next train is at eight thirty-five, and that is the last train, I think. The young lady will be able to get her dinner before she starts.”
It was he who brought the news to the astonished Margaret Belman.

===Chinese===
“她可以坐汽车回来；这个我能安排。”
里德先生摩挲着下巴。
“要不，我从苏格兰场带两个人下来，或许更好。”
“不必不必，”达弗连忙说，“就派贝尔曼小姐去。我毫无异议。我去同她说。”
里德先生看了看表。
“下一班火车八点三十五分，我想那也是末班车了。动身之前，这位小姐还来得及吃晚饭。”
把这消息带给满脸惊诧的玛格丽特·贝尔曼的，正是他。

===Original===
“Of course I’ll go up to town; but don’t you think somebody else could get this instrument for you, Mr. Reeder? Couldn’t you have it sent down﻿—”
She saw the look in his eyes and stopped.
“What is it?” she asked, in a lower voice.
“Will you do this for﻿—um﻿—me, Miss﻿—um﻿—Margaret?” said Mr. Reeder, almost humbly.
He went to the lounge and scribbled a note, while Margaret telephoned for the cab. It was growing dark when the closed landau drew up before the hotel and J. G. Reeder, who accompanied her, opened the door.
“There’s a man inside,” he said, dropping his voice to a whisper. “Please don’t scream: he’s an officer of police and he’s going with you to London.”
“But﻿—but﻿—” she stammered.
“And you’ll stay in London tonight,” said Mr. Reeder. “I will join you in the morning﻿—I hope.”

===Chinese===
“我当然可以进城去；可里德先生，您不觉得这器械换个别人也取得到吗？您不能叫人把它送下来——”
她看见他眼中的神色，便收住了话。
“怎么了？”她压低声音问。
“您肯为——嗯——我做这一件事吗，小姐——嗯——玛格丽特？”里德先生说，语气近乎卑微。
他去休息厅草草写了张便条，玛格丽特则打电话叫车。那辆带篷的四轮马车驶抵旅馆门前时，天色正暗下来；送她同行的 J. G. 里德，拉开了车门。
“车里有个男人，”他把嗓音压成耳语，“请别叫出声：他是个警官，随您一起去伦敦。”
“可是——可是——”她结结巴巴地说。
“而且今夜您就留在伦敦，”里德先生说，“明早我就来与您会合——但愿如此。”

## XII / 十二

===Original===
Mr. Reeder was in his room, laying out his moderate toilet requirements on the dressing table, and meditating upon the waste of time involved in conforming to fashion﻿—for he had dressed for dinner﻿—when there came a tap at the door. He paused, a well-worn hairbrush in his hand, and looked around.
“Come in,” he said, and added: “if you please.”
The little head of Mr. Daver appeared around the opening of the door, anxiety and apology in every line of his peculiar face.
“Am I interrupting you?” he asked. “I am terribly sorry to bother you at all, but Miss Belman being away, you quite understand? I’m sure you do.”
Mr. Reeder was courtesy itself.

===Chinese===
里德先生正在自己房里，把那套为数不多的梳洗用品一样样摆上梳妆台，一面寻思着讲究衣着时尚何其浪费时间——为了晚餐，他可是特意换上了礼服——这时响起了轻轻的叩门声。他停住手，手里握着一把用旧了的发刷，回头望去。
“请进，”他说，又添上一句：“劳驾啦。”
达弗先生那颗小小的脑袋从门缝边探了出来，那张古怪脸上的每一道纹路，都写着焦灼和歉意。
“我打搅您了吧？”他问，“真对不起，本不该来烦您的，可贝尔曼小姐又不在，您完全体谅的，对吧？我敢说您一定体谅。”
里德先生简直是礼貌的化身。

===Original===
“Come in, come in, sir,” he said. “I was merely preparing for the night. I am a very tired man, and the sea air﻿—”
He saw the face of the proprietor fall.
“Then, Mr. Reeder, I have come upon a useless errand. The truth is”﻿—he slipped inside the door, closed it carefully behind him as though he had an important statement to make which he did not wish to be overheard﻿—“my three guests are anxious to play bridge, and they deputed me to ask if you would care to join them?”
“With pleasure,” said Mr. Reeder graciously. “I am an indifferent player, but if they will bear with me, I shall be down in a few minutes.”
Mr. Daver withdrew, babbling his gratitude and apologies.

===Chinese===
“请进，请进，先生，”他说，“我不过是收拾收拾准备就寝。我是个累坏了的人，再说这海边的空气——”
他看见东家的脸沉了下来。
“那么，里德先生，我这趟是白跑了。实情是”——他侧身进门，又小心翼翼地掩上身后的门，仿佛有一句要紧话要说、生怕叫人听了去似的——“我那三位客人想打桥牌，他们委托我来问一问，您愿不愿意下来凑一桌？”
“不胜荣幸，”里德先生雍容地说，“我只是个平庸的牌手，不过只要他们多多担待，几分钟后我就下来。”
达弗先生退了出去，一叠连声地道着谢、赔着不是。

===Original===
The door was hardly closed upon him before Mr. Reeder crossed the room and locked it. Stooping, he opened one of the trunks, took out a long, flexible rope ladder, and dropped it through the open window into the darkness below, fastening one end to the leg of the four-poster. Leaning out of the window, he said something in a low voice, and braced himself against the bed to support the weight of the man who came nimbly up the ladder into the room. This done, he replaced the rope ladder in his trunk, locked it, and, walking to a corner of the room, pulled at one of the solid panels. It hinged open and revealed the deep cupboard which Mr. Daver had shown him.
“That is as good a place as any, Brill,” he said. “I’m sorry I must leave you for two hours, but I have an idea that nobody will disturb you there. I am leaving the lamp burning, which will give you enough light.”
“Very good, sir,” said the man from Scotland Yard, and took up his post.
Five minutes later, Mr. Reeder locked the door of his room and went downstairs to the waiting party.

===Chinese===
门在那位身后几乎还没合拢，里德先生便已穿过房间，把门锁上。他俯身打开一只衣箱，取出一条又长又软的绳梯，顺着敞开的窗子垂进底下的黑暗里，把一头拴在四柱大床的床腿上。他探身窗外，低声说了句什么，随即抵住床架，承住了那个顺着梯子敏捷爬进房来的男人的分量。事毕，他把绳梯收回箱中锁好，走到房间一角，拉了拉一块严丝合缝的格板。格板铰转开来，露出那个深壁橱——达弗先生先前指给他看过的。
“这地方再好不过了，布里尔（Brill），”他说，“抱歉，只得把你撂在这儿两个钟头，不过我料想没人会去搅扰你。灯我给它留着，亮堂堂的，够你使了。”
“是，先生。”这位苏格兰场来的人应道，随即就位守了起来。
五分钟后，里德先生锁好房门，下楼去赴那等着他的牌局了。

===Original===
They were in the big hall, a very silent and preoccupied trio, until his arrival galvanized them into something that might pass for light conversation. There was indeed a fourth present when he came in: a sallow-faced woman in black, who melted out of the hall at his approach, and he guessed her to be the melancholy Mrs. Burton. The two men rose at his approach, and after the usual self-deprecatory exchange which preceded the cutting for partners, Mr. Reeder found himself sitting opposite the military looking Colonel Hothling. On his left was the pale girl; on his right, the hard-faced Rev. Mr. Dean.

===Chinese===
三个人在大厅里坐着，沉默寡言、心事重重，直到他到来，才像通了电似的打起精神，凑出些勉强可以充作轻松闲谈的谈话来。他进门时屋里其实还有第四个人：一个穿黑衣裳、面色蜡黄的女人，见他走近，便悄然隐出了大厅——他猜，她就是那位郁郁寡欢的伯顿太太。两个男人见他过来都站起身，照例先来一番自谦互让——这是切牌定搭档前的老规矩——随后里德先生发现自己对面坐的是那位军人气派的霍斯林上校。左边是面色苍白的姑娘；右边，是板着一张硬脸的迪恩牧师。

===Original===
“What do we play for?” growled the Colonel, caressing his moustache, his steely blue eyes fixed on Mr. Reeder.
“A modest stake, I hope,” begged that gentleman. “I am such an indifferent player.”
“I suggest sixpence a hundred,” said the clergyman. “It is as much as a poor parson can afford.”
“Or a poor pensioner either,” grumbled the Colonel, and sixpence a hundred was agreed.

===Chinese===
“我们打什么彩头？”上校粗声粗气地问，一手捻着唇上的小胡子，一双钢蓝色的眼睛紧盯着里德先生。
“但愿是小注头，”这位先生恳求道，“我这牌打得实在太稀松。”
“我提议一百分六便士，”牧师说，“一个穷牧师，也就出得起这个数了。”
“要不，一个靠养老金糊口的穷人也一样，”上校咕哝道。一百分六便士，就此说定。

===Original===
They played two games in comparative silence. Reeder was sensitive of a strained atmosphere but did nothing to relieve it. His partner was surprisingly nervous for one who, as he remarked casually, had spent his life in military service.
“A wonderful life,” said Mr. Reeder in his affable way.
Once or twice he detected the girl’s hand, as she held the cards, tremble never so slightly. Only the clergyman remained still and unmoved, and incidentally played without error.

===Chinese===
他们相对无言地打了两局。里德先生对紧绷的气氛感觉敏锐，却不动声色，无意去缓和。他这位搭档曾漫不经意地提到，自己大半生都在军中效力，可他这份紧张劲儿，却出人意料。
“好一派精彩的生涯，”里德先生和颜悦色地说。
有一两回，他察觉姑娘持牌的手在微微发颤，轻得几乎看不出来。唯有那牧师自始至终纹丝不动，顺带说一句，牌也打得滴水不漏。

===Original===
It was after an atrocious revoke on the part of his partner, a revoke which gave his opponents the game and rubber, that Mr. Reeder pushed back his chair.
“What a strange world this is!” he remarked sententiously. “How like a game of cards!”
Those who were best acquainted with Mr. Reeder knew that he was most dangerous when he was most philosophical. The three people who sat about the table heard only a boring commonplace, in keeping with their conception of this somewhat dull-looking man.

===Chinese===
直到搭档犯下一次恶劣透顶的藏牌——这一藏，把本局连同这一盘都拱手送给了对手——里德先生才把椅子往后一推。
“这世道真是古怪！”他一本正经地发表着高论，“多像一局牌啊！”
最熟识里德先生为人的那些人都知道，他越是玄言玄语，便越是危险。围桌而坐的三个人听到的，却只是一句无聊的陈词滥调，倒也正合他们对这个相貌乏味的男人的观感。

===Original===
“There are some people,” mused Mr. Reeder, looking up at the lofty ceiling, “who are never happy unless they have all the aces. I, on the contrary, am most cheerful when I have in my hand all the knaves.”

===Chinese===
“世上有那么一种人，”里德先生仰望着高高的天花板出神地说，“手里不把尖儿（A）攥全了，就浑身不痛快。我呢，恰恰相反，手里攥着满把的‘无赖’（J）时，才最开心不过。”

===Original===
“You play a very good game, Mr. Reeder.”
It was the girl who spoke, and her voice was husky, her tone hesitant, as though she was forcing herself to speak.
“I play one or two games rather well,” said Mr. Reeder. “Partly, I think, because I have such an extraordinary memory﻿—I never forget knaves.”
There was a silence. This time the reference was too direct to be mistaken.

===Chinese===
“您这一手牌打得真漂亮，里德先生。”
说话的是那姑娘。她的嗓音发哑，语气迟疑，像是硬逼着自己开口。
“有一两样玩意儿我玩得还算不赖，”里德先生说，“一部分缘故嘛，我想，是我这记性出奇地好——无赖这号人，我从来忘不掉。”
一时沉默。这一回，话锋所指太直接，谁也错听不了。

===Original===
“There used to be in my younger days,” Mr. Reeder went on, addressing nobody in particular, “a knave of hearts, who eventually became a knave of clubs, and drifted down into heaven knows what other welters of knavery! In plain words, he started his professional﻿—um﻿—life as a bigamist, continued his interesting and romantic career as a tout for gambling hells, and was concerned in a bank robbery in Denver. I have not seen him for years, but he is colloquially known to his associates as ‘the Colonel’: a military looking gentleman with a pleasing appearance and a glib tongue.”
He was not looking at the Colonel as he spoke, so he did not see the man’s face go pale.

===Chinese===
“在我年轻那会儿，”里德先生接着往下说，也不是专对哪一个人讲，“有过一位红桃无赖，后来改换门庭做了梅花无赖，再往后又流落到天知道的什么别的无赖泥潭里去了！说白了，他的职业——嗯——生涯，头一桩是当重婚犯，接着又做赌窟里拉客的掮客，继续他那有趣而罗曼蒂克的事业，还在丹佛（Denver）掺和过一桩银行劫案。我多年没见过他了，不过他的同伙给他起了个俗号，唤作‘上校’：一位仪表堂堂、伶牙俐齿、颇有军人气派的绅士。”
说话时他并没有朝上校看，因此没有看见那人的脸刷地变白。

===Original===
“I have not met him since he grew a moustache, but I could recognize him anywhere by the peculiar colour of his eyes and by the fact that he has a scar at the back of his head, a souvenir of some unfortunate fracas in which he became engaged. They tell me that he became an expert user of knives﻿—I gather he sojourned a while in Latin America﻿—a knave of clubs and a knave of hearts﻿—hum!”
The Colonel sat rigid, not a muscle of his face moving.

===Chinese===
“打他蓄起小胡子，我就再没见过他。不过凭他那双颜色特别的眼睛，凭他后脑勺上那道疤——那是他卷进某场不幸殴斗留下的纪念——无论在哪儿我都能认出他来。听说他后来练成了使刀子的行家——我推想，他在拉丁美洲盘桓过一阵——一位梅花无赖，又兼一位红桃无赖——哼！”
上校僵坐不动，脸上一块肌肉也不动。

===Original===
“One supposes,” Mr. Reeder continued, looking at the girl thoughtfully, “that he has by this time acquired a competence which enables him to stay at the very best hotels without any fear of police supervision.”
Her dark eyes were fixed unwaveringly on his. The full lips were closed, the jaws set.
“How very interesting you are, Mr. Reeder!” she drawled at last. “Mr. Daver tells me you are associated with the police force?”
“Remotely, only remotely,” said Mr. Reeder.
“Are you acquainted with any other knaves, Mr. Reeder?”

===Chinese===
“照理推想，”里德先生接着说，一边若有所思地望着那姑娘，“他如今该已挣得一份家业，住得起顶讲究的旅馆，不必惧怕警察的监视了。”
她那双黑眼睛一瞬不瞬地盯住他的眼睛。丰满的嘴唇紧闭，牙关咬紧。
“您这个人可真有意思，里德先生！”她终于拖长了声调说，“达弗先生告诉我，您是跟警界沾边的？”
“沾那么一点儿边，仅仅一点儿边。”里德先生说。
“您还认得别的什么无赖吗，里德先生？”

===Original===
It was the cool voice of the clergyman, and Mr. Reeder beamed around at him.
“With the knave of diamonds,” he said softly. “What a singularly appropriate name for one who spent five years in the profitable pursuit of illicit diamond-buying in South Africa, and five unprofitable years on the breakwater in Capetown, becoming, as one might say, a knave of spades from the continual use of that necessary and agricultural implement, and a knave of pickaxes, too, one supposes. He was flogged, if I remember rightly, for an outrageous assault upon a warder, and on his release from prison was implicated in a robbery in Johannesburg. I am relying on my memory, and I cannot recall at the moment whether he reached Pretoria Central﻿—which is the colloquial name for the Transvaal prison﻿—or whether he escaped. I seem to remember that he was concerned in a banknote case which I once had in hand. Now, what was his name?”

===Chinese===
这是那位牧师冷冷的嗓音。里德先生笑吟吟地朝他扬起脸来。
“还认得一位方块无赖，”他轻声说，“这名字对这么一个人真是再贴切不过：他在南非干非法收购钻石的营生，获利五年；又在开普敦（Capetown）的防波堤上服了五年无利可图的苦役——可以说，因为常年抡着那件必不可少的农用家什，他成了黑桃无赖——黑桃者，铁锹也——而且想必还兼做铁镐无赖。如果我没记错，他因为丧心病狂地袭击一名看守挨过鞭子，刑满出狱之后，又在约翰内斯堡（Johannesburg）卷进了一桩劫案。我这全凭记性，一时想不起他到底进没进过比勒陀利亚中央监狱（Pretoria Central）——那是德兰士瓦（Transvaal）监狱的俗称——还是半路脱逃了。我恍惚记得，他跟我经手过的一宗钞票案有些牵连。对了，他叫什么名字来着？”

===Original===
He looked thoughtfully at the clergyman. “Gregory Dones! That is it﻿—Mr. Gregory Dones! It is beginning to come back to me now. He had an angel tattooed on his left forearm, a piece of decoration which one would have imagined sufficient to keep him to the narrow paths of virtue, and even to bring him eventually within the fold of the church.”
The Rev. Mr. Dean got up from the table, put his hand in his pocket and took out some money.
“You lost the rubber, but I think you win on points,” he said. “What do I owe you, Mr. Reeder?”
“What you can never pay me,” said Mr. Reeder, shaking his head. “Believe me, Gregory, your score and mine will never be wholly settled to your satisfaction!”
With a shrug of his shoulders and a smile, the hard-faced clergyman strolled away. Mr. Reeder watched him out of the corner of his eye and saw him disappear toward the vestibule.

===Chinese===
他若有所思地望着牧师。“格雷戈里·多恩斯（Gregory Dones）！就是这个名字——格雷戈里·多恩斯先生！现在我开始想起来了。他左前臂上刺着一个天使。这一件纹身装饰，按说该足以引着他走正道、守本分，甚至说不定到头来还能把他收进教会的羊圈呢。”
迪恩牧师从桌边站起身，伸手到口袋里掏出些钱来。
“这一盘你们是输了，不过论点数，我想是您赢了，”他说，“我该付您多少，里德先生？”
“那是您永远也付不清的，”里德先生摇着头说，“相信我，格雷戈里，你我这笔账，永远也算不到叫你满意的那一天！”
那板着脸的牧师耸了耸肩，面带微笑，踱了开去。里德先生用眼角余光送着他，望见他朝门厅那头消失了。

===Original===
“Are all your knaves masculine?” asked Olga Crewe.
Reeder nodded gravely.
“I hope so, Miss Crewe.”
Her challenging eyes met his.
“In other words, you don’t know me?” she said bluntly. And then, with sudden vehemence: “I wish to God you did! I wish you did!”
Turning abruptly, she almost ran from the hall.

===Chinese===
“您那些无赖，清一色都是男的？”奥尔加·克鲁问。
里德先生郑重地点了点头。
“但愿如此，克鲁小姐。”
她那双含着挑衅的眼睛迎上他的目光。
“换句话说，您并不认得我？”她单刀直入地说。紧接着，语气陡然激烈起来：“我巴不得老天爷叫您认得！巴不得您认得！”
她猛一转身，几乎是逃也似的奔出了大厅。

===Original===
Mr. Reeder stood where she had left him, his eyes roving left and right. In the shadowy entrance of the hall, made all the more obscure by the heavy dark curtains which covered it, he saw a dim figure standing. Only for a second, and then it disappeared. The woman Burton, he thought.
It was time to go to his room. He had taken only two steps from the table when all the lights in the hall went out. In such moments as these Mr. Reeder was a very nimble man. He spun round and made for the nearest wall, and stood waiting, his back to the panelling. And then he heard the plaintive voice of Mr. Daver.
“Who on earth has put the lights out? Where are you, Mr. Reeder?”
“Here!” said Mr. Reeder, in a loud voice, and dropped instantly to the ground. Only in time; he heard a whistle, a thud, and something struck the panel above his head.
Mr. Reeder emitted a deep groan and crawled rapidly and noiselessly across the floor.

===Chinese===
里德先生站在她撂下他的地方，两眼左右逡巡。在大厅幽暗的入口处——厚重的黑帘把它遮得愈发晦暗——他看见立着一条朦胧的人影。只一秒钟，人影便不见了。是伯顿太太吧，他想。
该回自己房间去了。他离桌才迈出两步，大厅里的灯霎时全灭了。每逢这种时刻，里德先生是个身手极其敏捷的人。他一个旋身，抢向最近的墙壁，背抵着墙板站定等候。跟着，他听见了达弗先生那哀婉的嗓音。
“这灯到底是谁弄灭的？您在哪儿呢，里德先生？”
“在这儿！”里德先生高声应道，随即霎时卧倒在地。就差那么一瞬；他听见嗖的一声、咚的一响，什么东西扎上了他头顶的墙板。
里德先生发出一声深沉的呻吟，随即飞快无声地在地板上爬行起来。

===Original===
Again came Daver’s voice.
“What on earth was that? Has anything happened, Mr. Reeder?”
The detective made no reply. Nearer and nearer he was crawling toward where Daver stood. And then, as unexpectedly as they had been extinguished, the lights went on. Daver was standing in front of the curtained doorway, and on the proprietor’s face was a look of blank dismay, as Mr. Reeder rose at his feet.
Daver shrank back, his big white teeth set in a fearful grin, his round eyes wide open. He tried to speak and his mouth opened and closed, but no sound issued. From Reeder his eyes strayed to the panelled wall﻿—but Reeder had already seen the knife buried in the wood.

===Chinese===
达弗的声音又响起来。
“那到底是什么？出什么事了吗，里德先生？”
侦探没有作答。他朝达弗站立之处越爬越近。就在这时，灯又亮了，同熄灭时一样毫无征兆。达弗正站在挂帘的门道前，里德先生从他脚边站起身来，只见东家脸上是一片呆然的惊惶。
达弗直往后缩，一口大白牙龇出一个骇人的笑，一双圆眼睛瞪得溜圆。他想说话，嘴张了又合、合了又张，却半点声音也发不出来。他的目光从里德身上溜向墙板——可那把扎进木头里的刀，里德早已看见了。

===Original===
“Let me think,” he said gently. “Was that the Colonel or the highly intelligent representative of the Church?”
He went across to the wall and with an effort pulled out the knife. It was long and broad.
“A murderous weapon,” said Mr. Reeder.
Daver found his voice.
“A murderous weapon,” he echoed hollowly. “Was it﻿—thrown at you, Mr. Reeder? How very terrible!”

===Chinese===
“让我想一想，”他温和地说，“方才那一下，是上校掷的，还是教会那位绝顶聪明的代表掷的？”
他走到墙边，费了一把劲，把刀拔了出来。刀身又长又阔。
“好一件凶器。”里德先生说。
达弗这才找回了自己的嗓音。
“好一件凶器，”他空洞洞地应和着，“这——这是朝您掷来的吗，里德先生？太可怕了！”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder was gazing at him sombrely.
“Your idea?” he asked, but by now Mr. Daver was incapable of replying.
Reeder left the shaken proprietor lying limply in one of the big armchairs and walked up the carpeted stairs to the corridor. And if against his black coat the automatic was not visible, it was nevertheless there.

===Chinese===
里德先生正阴沉沉地盯着他。
“是您出的主意？”他问。可这时的达弗先生，已经答不上话来了。
里德先生任由那位吓破了胆的东家瘫在一张大扶手椅里，自己踏着铺地毯的楼梯上楼进了走廊。那支自动手枪贴在黑呢外衣底下，纵然看不出来，它却实实在在是在那里的。

===Original===
He stopped before his door, unlocked it, and threw it wide open. The lamp by the side of the bed was still burning. Mr. Reeder switched on the wall light, peeped through the crack between the door and the wall before he ventured inside.
He shut the door, locked it, and walked over to the cupboard.
“You may come out, Brill,” he said. “I presume nobody has been here?”
There was no answer, and he pulled open the cupboard door quickly.
It was empty!

===Chinese===
他在自己房门前停步，开了锁，把门大大推开。床边的灯还亮着。里德先生拧亮壁灯，先从门和墙的缝隙往里窥了一眼，才敢走进去。
他关上门，上了锁，走到壁橱跟前。
“你可以出来了，布里尔，”他说，“我料想没人来过吧？”
没有应声。他一把拉开壁橱的门。
壁橱空空如也！

===Original===
“Well, well,” said Mr. Reeder, and that meant that matters were everything but well.
There was no sign of a struggle; nothing in the world to suggest that Detective Brill had not walked out of his own free will and made his exit by the window, which was still open.
Mr. Reeder tiptoed back to the light-switch and turned it; stretched across the bed and extinguished the lamp; and then he sidled cautiously to the window and peeped round the stone framing. It was a very dark night, and he could distinguish no object below.
Events were moving only a little faster than he had anticipated; for this, however, he was responsible. He had forced the hands of the Flack confederation, and they were extremely able hands.

===Chinese===
“唉，唉。”里德先生说。而这句话的意思是：事情糟得不能再糟了。
没有半点搏斗的痕迹；世上也没有任何迹象表明，布里尔侦探不是出于自愿走出去、从那扇还敞着的窗子里离开的。
里德先生蹑手蹑脚回到电灯开关旁，把壁灯拧灭；又隔着床面探过身去，熄了床边的灯；然后小心翼翼地侧身蹭到窗口，从石头窗框边上往外窥探。夜色浓黑，底下什么也分辨不出。
事态的进展只比他预料的略快一步；不过这得归咎于他自己。是他逼得弗拉克同盟摊了牌，而那一帮的手，又偏偏是顶能干的手。

===Original===
He was unlocking the trunk when he heard a faint sound of steel against steel. Somebody was fitting a key into the lock, and he waited, his automatic covering the door. Nothing further happened, and he went forward to investigate. His flashlamp showed him what had happened. Somebody outside had inserted a key, turned it and left it in the lock, so that it was impossible for the door to be unlocked from the inside.
“I am rather glad,” said Mr. Reeder, speaking his thoughts aloud, “that Miss﻿—um﻿—Margaret is on her way to London!”
He pursued his lips reflectively. Would he be glad if he also was at this moment en route for London? Mr. Reeder was not very certain about this.
On one point he was satisfied﻿—the Flacks were going to give him a very small margin of time, and that margin must be used to the best advantage.

===Chinese===
他正开着衣箱，忽听得钢铁轻碰钢铁的一声细响。有人正把钥匙插进锁孔。他持枪等着，枪口对住房门。此后再无动静，他便上前查看。手电筒照出了究竟：外头有人把钥匙插进锁孔，拧转之后留在了锁里，这样一来，这扇门就再也无法从屋里打开了。
“我倒是相当庆幸，”里德先生自言自语地说出了心声，“小姐——嗯——玛格丽特这会儿已经上了去伦敦的路！”
他若有所思地咂了咂嘴唇。倘若此刻正在赴伦敦途中的是他本人，他也会感到庆幸吗？这一点，里德先生不很拿得准。
有一点他是满意的——弗拉克一伙留给他的时间余地小得很，而这点滴余地，必须用到刀刃上。

===Original===
So far as he could tell, the trunks had not been opened. He pulled out the rope-ladder, groped down to the bottom, and presently withdrew his hand, holding a long white cardboard cylinder. Crawling under the window, he put up his hand and fixed an end of the cylinder in one of the china flowerpots that stood on the broad windowsill and which he had moved to allow the ingress of Brill. When this had been done to his satisfaction, he struck a match and, reaching up, set fire to a little touch-paper at the cylinder’s free end. He brought his hand down just in time; something whizzed into the room and struck the panelling of the opposite wall with an angry smack. There was no sound of explosion. Whoever fired was using an air pistol. Again and again, in rapid succession, came the pellets, but by now the cylinder was burning and spluttering, and in another instant the grounds were brilliantly illuminated as the flare burst into a dazzling red flame that, he knew, could be seen for miles.

===Chinese===
就他所能判断的，衣箱都不曾被人打开过。他抽出绳梯，探手摸到箱底，不多时手抽了回来，握着一个长长的白色硬纸筒。他爬到窗下，举臂把纸筒的一端插进宽窗台上摆着的几只瓷花盆中的一只里——那几只花盆，是他先前为放布里尔进来挪动过的。待一切办得妥妥帖帖，他划着一根火柴，探臂向上，点燃了纸筒空着那一端的一小截导火纸。他的手收得正是时候；什么东西嗖地一声飞进屋来，怒声啪的一响，打在对面墙的墙板上。没有爆炸声。放枪的人用的是气枪。弹丸一发接一发、疾如连珠地射来，可这时纸筒已经烧了起来，噼啪迸溅，转瞬之间，火光轰然爆成一团炫目的红焰，把整座园子照得雪亮——他知道，这光几英里外都望得见。

===Original===
He heard a scampering of feet below, but dared not look out. By the time the first tender load of detectives had come flying up the drive, the grounds were deserted.
With the exception of the servants, there were only two persons at Larmes Keep when the police began their search. Mr. Daver and the faded Mrs. Burton alone remained. “Colonel Hothling” and “the Rev. Mr. Dean” had disappeared as though they had been whisked from the face of the earth.

===Chinese===
他听见底下响起一阵奔逃的脚步声，却不敢探头外望。等第一批满载侦探的车子顺着车道疾驰赶到时，园子里已经空无一人。
警察着手搜查时，拉尔姆宅里除了仆役，只剩两个人。留下来的，唯有达弗先生和那位颜色黯淡的伯顿太太。“霍斯林上校”和“迪恩牧师”则消失得无影无踪，仿佛被人从地面上凭空摄走了一般。

===Original===
Big Bill Gordon interviewed the proprietor.
“This is Flack’s headquarters, and you know it. You’ll be well advised to spill everything and save your own skin.”
“But I don’t know the man; I’ve never seen him!” wailed Mr. Daver. “This is the most terrible thing that has happened to me in my life! Can you make me responsible for the character of my guests? You’re a reasonable man? I see you are! If these people are friends of Flack, I have never heard of them in that connection. You may search my house from cellar to garret, and if you find anything that in the least incriminates me, take me off to prison. I ask that as a favour. Is that the statement of an honest man? I see you are convinced!”

===Chinese===
大比尔·戈登审问起东家来。
“这儿就是弗拉克的总部，你自己心知肚明。放聪明点儿，竹筒倒豆子全招出来，也好保全你自己这张皮。”
“可我根本不认得这个人；我连面都没见过他！”达弗先生哭丧着喊道，“这是我这辈子碰上的最可怕的事！我客人的品行操守，难道也能叫我担干系？您是个讲道理的人吧？我看得出，您是的！这几个人就算是弗拉克的朋友，这一层干系我可从来不知道。您尽管从我家的地窖一路搜到阁楼，但凡搜出半点儿连累我的东西，就把我押进监狱去。我把这话当恩典求您。这可是一个老实人说的话吧？瞧，您信了！”

===Original===
Neither he nor Mrs. Burton nor any of the servants who were questioned in the early hours of the morning could afford the slightest clue to the identity of the visitors. Miss Crewe had been in the habit of coming every year and of staying four and sometimes five months. Hothling was a newcomer, as also was the parson. Inquiries made by telephone of the chief of the Siltbury police confirmed Mr. Daver’s statement that he had been the proprietor of Larmes Keep for twenty-five years, and that his past was blameless. He himself produced his title deeds. A search of his papers, made at his invitation, and of the three tin boxes in the safe, produced nothing but support for his protestations of innocence.

===Chinese===
无论他本人、伯顿太太，还是清晨受讯的那班仆役，谁也拿不出半点线索，供人查明那几位客人的来历。克鲁小姐素来年年都来，一住四个月，有时五个月。霍斯林是新客，那位牧师也是新来的。电话打到西尔特伯里警察局长那里查证的结果，证实了达弗先生的供述：他做拉尔姆宅的东家已有二十五年，履历清白无瑕。他还亲自呈出了地契。他主动邀人搜查了他的文件，连同保险柜里的三只铁皮箱，结果非但找不出任何连累他的东西，反倒桩桩件件都给他那些自称无辜的表白作了佐证。

===Original===
Big Bill interviewed Mr. Reeder in the hall over a cup of coffee at three o’clock in the morning.
“There’s no doubt at all that these people were members of the Flack crowd, probably engaged in advance against his escape, and how they got away the Lord knows! I have had six men on duty on the road since dark, and neither the woman nor the two men passed me.”
“Did you see Brill?” asked Mr. Reeder, suddenly remembering the absent detective.
“Brill?” said the other in astonishment. “He’s with you, isn’t he? You told me to have him under your window﻿—”

===Chinese===
凌晨三点钟，大比尔在大厅里就着一杯咖啡，向里德先生问话。
“这没跑儿，这几个人都是弗拉克一伙的，多半是预先布置下来、接应他越狱的。可他们是怎么跑掉的，只有天知道！天一擦黑我就派了六个人在路上值守，那女人和两个男人，谁也没从我眼皮子底下过去。”
“你见着布里尔了吗？”里德先生问，忽然想起那位失踪的侦探。
“布里尔？”对方愕然道，“他不是跟你在一块儿吗？是你吩咐我把他安置在你窗子底下的——”

===Original===
In a few words Mr. Reeder explained the situation, and together they went up to No. 7. There was nothing in the cupboard to afford the slightest clue to Brill’s whereabout. The panels were sounded, but there was no evidence of secret doors﻿—a romantic possibility which Mr. Reeder had not excluded, for this was the type of house where he might expect to find them.
Two men were sent to search the grounds for the missing detective, and Reeder and the police chief went back to finish their coffee.
“Your theory has turned out accurate so far, but there is nothing to connect Daver.”
“Daver’s in it,” said Mr. Reeder. “He was not the knife-thrower; his job was to locate me on behalf of the Colonel. But Daver brought Miss Belman down here in preparation for Flack’s escape.”

===Chinese===
里德先生三言两语讲明了情形，两人一同上了 7 号房。壁橱里没有任何可以指明布里尔下落的线索。格板逐一敲过，却找不出暗门的迹象——暗门这种富于传奇色彩的可能，里德先生本来也没有排除，因为恰恰是这样的宅子，最叫他指望寻见这种东西。
两名警员奉命去园中搜寻失踪的侦探，里德先生和大比尔回去把咖啡喝完。
“你的推断，到眼下为止桩桩应验，可就是牵不紧达弗。”
“达弗脱不了干系，”里德先生说，“掷刀的不是他；他的差事，是替上校探明我站的位置。至于达弗把贝尔曼小姐弄到这儿来，那是为弗拉克越狱做的准备。”

===Original===
Big Bill nodded.
“She was to be hostage for your good behaviour.” He scratched his head irritably. “That’s like one of Crazy John’s schemes. But why did he try to shoot you up? Why wasn’t he satisfied with her being at Larmes Keep?”
Mr. Reeder had no immediate explanation. He was dealing with a madman, a person of whims. Consistency was not to be expected from Mr. Flack.
He passed his fingers through his scanty hair.
“It is all rather puzzling and inexplicable,” he said. “I think I’ll go to bed.”
He was dreaming sleeplessly, under the watchful eye of a Scotland Yard detective, when Big Bill came bursting into the room.

===Chinese===
大比尔点了点头。
“她是扣下的人质，好叫你规规矩矩。”他烦躁地挠着脑袋，“这倒像疯子约翰的手笔。可他干吗要开枪崩你？她人都到了拉尔姆宅，他还有什么不满足的？”
里德先生一时给不出解释。他打交道的对象是个疯子，一个兴之所至、反复无常的人。指望弗拉克先生前后如一，本就是奢望。
他把手指插进稀疏的头发里拢了拢。
“这一切实在叫人纳闷，说不清道不明，”他说，“我想我还是睡觉去吧。”
他在一名苏格兰场侦探的看守之下睁眼做着无眠的梦，这时大比尔一头闯进了房间。

===Original===
“Get up, Reeder!” he said roughly.
Mr. Reeder sat up in bed, instantly awake.
“What is wrong?” he asked.
“Wrong! That gold lorry left the Bank of England this morning at five o’clock on its way to Tilbury and hasn’t been heard from since!”

===Chinese===
“起来，里德！”他粗声喝道。
里德先生从床上坐起，霎时全醒。
“出什么事了？”他问。
“什么事！那辆运金车今儿早上五点钟离开英格兰银行，开往蒂尔伯里，打那以后就音信全无了！”

## XIII / 十三

===Original===
At the last moment the bank authorities had changed their minds, and overnight had sent £53,000 worth of gold for conveyance to the ship. They had borrowed for the purpose an army lorry from Woolwich, a service which is sometimes claimed by the national banking institution.
The lorry had been accompanied by eight detectives, the military driver also being armed. Tilbury was reached at half-past eleven o’clock at night, and the lorry, a high-powered Lassavar, had returned to London at two o’clock in the morning. It had been again loaded in the bank courtyard under the eyes of the officer, sergeant, and two men of the guard that is on duty on the bank premises from sunset to sunrise. A new detachment of picked men from Scotland Yard, each carrying an automatic pistol, loaded the lorry for its second journey, the amount of gold this time being £73,000 worth. After the boxes had been put into the van, they had climbed up, and the lorry had driven away from the bank. Each of the eight men guarding this treasure was passed under review by a high officer of Scotland Yard who knew everyone personally. The lorry was seen in Commercial Road by a detective inspector of the division, and its progress was noted also by a police cyclist patrol who was on duty at the junction of Ripple and Barking roads.
The main Tilbury road runs within a few hundred yards of the village of Rainham, and it was at this point, only a few miles distant from Tilbury, that the lorry disappeared. Two motorcyclist policemen, who had gone out to meet the gold convoy and who had received a telephone message from the Ripple road to say that it had passed, grew uneasy and telephoned to Tilbury.

===Chinese===
到了最后关头，银行当局改变了主意，连夜调运了价值 53,000 英镑的黄金，送往船上。为此，他们从伍利奇（Woolwich）借来一辆军用卡车——向军队借车这项服务，那家国家银行机构有时也是要享用的。
随行的有八名侦探，开车的军用司机也带了枪。夜里十一点半抵达蒂尔伯里（Tilbury），凌晨两点，这辆大功率的拉萨瓦尔（Lassavar）卡车回到伦敦。卡车在银行院子里再度装货，自日落至日出在行内值守的卫队——一名军官、一名中士加两名士兵——全程监视。苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）新派出一队精选人员，人人携带自动手枪，为卡车的第二趟行程装货，这一次装载的黄金价值 73,000 英镑。钱箱装进车斗之后，众人爬上车，卡车驶离银行。守卫这批财宝的八个人，个个都由苏格兰场一位对他们人人识得的高级官员当面查验过。本区的一名探长在商业路（Commercial Road）上看见了这辆卡车，在里普尔路（Ripple Road）与巴金路（Barking Road）交叉口执勤的一名自行车巡警也记下了它的行踪。
蒂尔伯里干道从雷纳姆村（Rainham）几百码外经过，正是在这里——离蒂尔伯里不过几英里——卡车失踪了。两名骑摩托车的警察原是出迎黄金车队的，又接到里普尔路打来的电话，说车队已经过去，久候不至，心下不安，便打电话到蒂尔伯里询问。

===Original===
It was an airless morning, with occasional banks of mist lying in the hollows, and part of the road, especially near the river, was covered with patches of white fog, which dispersed about eight o’clock in the morning under a southeasterly wind. The mist had almost disappeared when the search party from Tilbury pursued their investigations and came upon evidence of the tragedy which the morning was to reveal. This was an old Ford motor car that had evidently run from the read, miraculously missed a telegraph pole, and ditched itself. The machine had not overturned; there were no visible marks of injury; yet the man who sat at the wheel was stone dead when he was found. An immediate medical examination failed to discover an injury of any kind to the man, who was a small farmer of Rainham, and on the face of it it looked as though he had died of a heart attack whilst on his way to town.
Just beyond the place where he was found, the road dips steeply between high banks. It is known as Coles Hollow, and at its deepest part the cutting is crossed by a single-track bridge, which connects two portions of the farm through which the road runs. The dead farmer and his car had been removed when Reeder and Inspector Simpson of Scotland Yard, who had been put in charge of the case, arrived on the spot. No news of any kind had been received of the lorry; but the local police, who had been following its tracks, had made two discoveries. Apparently, in going through the cutting, the lorry had run almost head-on into the wall of the bank on the right, for there was a deep scoop in the clayey soil which the impact had hollowed out.
“It almost appears,” said Simpson, “that the lorry swerved here to avoid the farmer’s car. There are his wheel tracks, and you notice they were wobbling from side to side. Probably the man was already dying.”

===Chinese===
那是个无风的早晨，洼地里时不时横着几团雾，部分路段——尤以近河处为甚——覆着一层层的白雾，到早晨八点上下，才被东南风吹散。蒂尔伯里来的搜索队循迹勘察时，雾已将近散尽，他们撞见了这个清晨即将揭开的惨剧的迹象。那是一辆老旧的福特汽车，显然是冲出了路面，奇迹般地躲过一根电线杆，栽进了沟里。车身没有翻覆，也看不见任何撞伤的痕迹，然而坐在方向盘后的男人被发现时已经死透了。随即进行的医学检查，在这名雷纳姆的小农身上找不出半点伤处，表面看来，他像是进城途中心脏病发作猝死的。
就在发现他的地点再往前一点，道路在高峻的路坡之间陡然下陷。此地名叫科尔斯洼（Coles Hollow），在最深处，一道路堑被一座单轨桥横跨，桥把道路所穿过的农场两部分连在一起。死者和他的车已被移走，这时里德先生和奉命主办此案的苏格兰场辛普森督察（Inspector Simpson）赶到了现场。卡车方面没有任何消息；但一路循着车辙跟踪的地方警察有了两项发现。看样子，卡车在穿过路堑时几乎迎头撞上了右边的坡壁，因为黏土里有一个被撞击掏空的深坑。
“看情形很像是，”辛普森说，“卡车在这里打了个急转，避开农夫的车。那儿有他的车辙，您也注意到了，辙印左歪右晃。多半这个人当时已经不行了。”

===Original===
“Have you traced the lorry tracks from here?” asked Reeder.
Simpson nodded and called a sergeant of the Essex Constabulary, who had charted the tracks.
“They seem to have turned up north toward Becontree,” he said. “As a matter of fact, a policeman at Becontree said he saw a large lorry come out of the mist and pass him, but that had a tilt on it and was going toward London. It was an army lorry, too, and was driven by a soldier.”
Mr. Reeder had lit a cigarette and was holding the flaming match in his hand, staring at it solemnly.

===Chinese===
“从这儿往后的卡车车辙，你们循迹查过了吗？”里德问。
辛普森点点头，唤来一名埃塞克斯郡警察队（Essex Constabulary）的中士，车辙就是他测绘的。
“车辙像是朝北折向贝孔特里（Becontree）去了。”他说，“实际上，贝孔特里的一名警察说，他看见一辆大卡车从雾里钻出来，从他身边驶过，不过那车带着篷，是往伦敦去的。也是辆军用卡车，开车的是个士兵。”
里德先生点了一支烟，火柴还燃在手里，他神情肃然地盯着它。

===Original===
“Dear me!” he said, and dropped the match and noticed that its flame was soon extinguished.
And then he began what seemed to be a foolish search of the ground, striking match after match.
“Isn’t there light enough for you, Mr. Reeder?” asked Simpson irritably.
The detective straightened his back and smiled. Only for a second was he amused, and then his long face went longer than ever.

===Chinese===
“哎呀！”他说着，丢下火柴，注意到火苗很快就灭了。
接着，他开始了一番看似愚蠢的地面搜索，一根接一根地划火柴。
“您是嫌光线还不够亮吗，里德先生？”辛普森不耐烦地问。
这位侦探直起腰来，笑了笑。那点兴味只维持了一秒钟，随即他的长脸拉得比先前更长了。

===Original===
“Poor fellow!” he said softly. “Poor fellow!”
“Whom are you talking about?” demanded Simpson, but Mr. Reeder did not reply. Instead, he pointed up to the bridge in the centre of which was an old and rusted water wagon, the type which certain English municipalities still use. He climbed up to the bank and examined the iron tank, opened the hatches and groped inside, lighting matches to aid his examination.
“Is it empty?” asked Simpson.
“I am afraid it is,” said Mr. Reeder, and inspected the worn hose leading from its iron spindles. He descended the cutting more melancholy than ever.

===Chinese===
“可怜的人！”他轻声说，“可怜的人！”
“您说的是哪一位？”辛普森追问，里德先生却不作答。他反而朝桥上指去——桥中央停着一辆锈迹斑斑的老式洒水车，正是英国某些市政当局至今还在用的那种。他爬上路坡，查看那只铁水箱，掀开舱口，把手伸进去摸索，又划着火柴辅助查验。
“是空的吗？”辛普森问。
“恐怕是空的。”里德先生说，又查看了从铁轴上引出的那条磨旧的水龙软管。他步下路堑，神色比先前愈发郁郁。

===Original===
“Have you thought how easy it is to disguise an ordinary army lorry?” he asked. “A tilt, I think the sergeant said, and on its way to London.”
“Do you think that was the gold van?”
Mr. Reeder nodded.
“I’m certain,” he said.

===Chinese===
“您想过没有，把一辆寻常军车改头换面，是何等容易的事？”他问，“带篷的，我想中士是这么说的，而且是开往伦敦的。”
“您认为那就是运金车？”
里德先生点了点头。
“我确信无疑。”他说。

===Original===
“Where was it attacked?”
Mr. Reeder pointed to the mark of the wheels on the side of the road.
“There,” he said simply, and Simpson growled impatiently.
“Stuff! Nobody heard a shot fired, and you don’t think our people would go down without a fight, do you? They could have held their own against five times their number, and no crowd has been seen on this road!”

===Chinese===
“它是在哪儿遇的袭？”
里德先生指了指路边的车轮印痕。
“就在那儿。”他只说了这一句，辛普森不耐烦地嘟囔起来。
“胡说！谁也没听见放过一枪，您总不至于以为我们的人会不战而倒吧？就是碰上五倍于己的敌人他们也顶得住，何况这条路上连一伙人影都没人见过！”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder nodded.
“Nevertheless, this is where the convoy was attacked and overcome,” he said. “I think you ought to look for the lorry with the tilt, and get on to your Becontree man and get a closer description of the machine he saw.”
In a quarter of an hour the police car brought them to the little Essex village, and the policeman who had seen the wagon was interviewed. It happened a few minutes before he went off duty, he said. There was a thick mist at the time, and he heard the rumble of the lorry wheels before it came into sight. He described it as a typical army wagon. So far as he could tell it was gray, and had a black tilt with “W.D.” and a broad arrow painted on the side, “W.D.” standing for War Department, the broad arrow being the sign of the Government. He saw one soldier driving and another sitting by his side. The back of the tilt was laced up and he could not see into the interior. The soldier, as he passed, had waved his hand in greeting, and the policeman had thought no more about the matter, until the robbery of the gold convoy was reported.
“Yes, sir,” he said, in answer to Reeder’s inquiry, “I think it was loaded. It went very heavily on the road. We often get these lorries coming up from Shoeburyness.”

===Chinese===
里德先生点了点头。
“然而，车队恰恰是在这里遇袭并被制服的。”他说，“我想您应当去找那辆带篷的卡车，并联系您在贝孔特里的那位警员，把 他见到的那台车子的形貌问得更仔细些。”
一刻钟后，警车把他们载到埃塞克斯郡那座小村，见过那辆卡车的警察接受了问询。他说，事情就发生在他下班前几分钟。当时雾很浓，卡车还没露面，他就先听到了车轮的隆隆声。他形容那是一辆典型的军用车。就他所能辨认的，车是灰色的，装着黑色车篷，侧面漆着“W.D.”字样和一支宽箭——“W.D.”是陆军部（War Department）的缩写，宽箭则是政府的标记。他看见一个士兵在开车，另一个坐在他旁边。车篷后襟系得紧紧的，里头看不进去。那士兵驶过时曾扬手致意，警察也没再多想，直到黄金车队遭劫的消息报了上来。
“是的，先生。”他回答里德的询问说，“我看那车装着货。在路上走得沉得很。这些军车常从休伯里内斯（Shoeburyness）那边开过来。”

===Original===
Simpson had put through a telephone inquiry to the Barking police, who had seen the military wagon. But army convoys were no unusual sight in the region of the docks. Either that or one similar was seen entering the Blackwall Tunnel, but the Greenwich police, on the south side of the river, had failed to identify it, and from there on all trace of the lorry was lost.
“We’re probably chasing a shadow anyway,” said Simpson. “If your theory is right, Reeder﻿—but it can’t be right! They couldn’t have caught these men of ours so unprepared that somebody didn’t shoot, and there’s no sign of shooting.”
“There was no shooting,” said Mr. Reeder, shaking his head.
“Then where are the men?” asked Simpson.

===Chinese===
辛普森已向巴金（Barking）警方挂电话查问，他们见过那辆军车。可是在码头一带，军队车队本就不是稀罕物。那辆车或者一辆相仿的车被人看见驶入布莱克沃尔隧道（Blackwall Tunnel），但河南岸的格林尼治（Greenwich）警方没能把它辨认出来，从那儿往后，卡车的踪迹便全部断了。
“反正我们多半是在追一个影子。”辛普森说，“倘若您的推断成立，里德——可它不可能成立！对方不可能打得我们这几个人如此措手不及，以至于没一个人来得及开枪；再说又没有半点开过枪的迹象。”
“是没有开枪。”里德先生说着，摇了摇头。
“那么，人呢？”辛普森问。

===Original===
“Dead,” said Mr. Reeder quietly.
It was Scotland Yard, in the presence of an incredulous and horrified commissioner, that Mr. J. G. Reeder reconstructed the crime.
“Flack is a chemist; I think I impressed it upon you. Did you notice, Simpson, on the bridge across the cutting an old water cart? I think you have since learned that it does not belong to the farmer who owns the land, and that he has never seen it before. It may be possible to discover where that was purchased. In all probability you will find that it was bought a few days ago at the sale of some municipal stores. I noticed in the *Times* there was an advertisement of such a sale. Do you realize how easy it would be not only to store under pressure, but to make, in that tank, large quantities of a deadly gas, one important element of which is carbon monoxide? Suppose this, or, as it may prove, a more deadly gas, has been so stored, do you realize how simple a matter it would be on a still, breathless morning to throw a big hose over the bridge and fill the hollow with the gas? That is, I am sure, what happened. Whatever else was used, there is still carbon monoxide in the cutting, for when I dropped a match it was immediately extinguished, and every match I struck near the ground went out. If the car had run right through and climbed the other slope of the cutting, the driver and the men inside the lorry might have escaped death. As it was, rendered momentarily unconscious, the driver turned his wheel and ran into the bank, stopping the lorry. They were probably dead before Flack and his associate, whoever it was, jumped down, wearing gas masks, lifted the driver back into the lorry and drove on.”

===Chinese===
“死了。”里德先生平静地说。
正是在苏格兰场，当着一位满腹狐疑、骇然失色的总监，里德先生把这桩罪案重新构建了一遍。
“弗拉克是个化学家；这一点我想我已向各位强调过了。辛普森，横跨路堑的那座桥上有一辆旧式洒水车，您注意到了吗？我想您后来已经查明，它不属于拥有这片土地的那个农夫，他此前也从未见过它。兴许查得出这车是在哪里买的。十之八九，您会发现它是几天前在某场市政物资拍卖上购得的。我注意到《泰晤士报》（Times）上登过这样一场拍卖的广告。您可曾意识到，在那只水箱里，不仅可以高压储存，而且可以就地制造大批量的致命毒气——其中一个重要成分便是一氧化碳？姑且假定这种气体——或者，日后查明，一种更毒的气体——就这般储存着，您可曾意识到，在一个无风无息的早晨，把一条粗大的软管从桥上垂下去，让毒气灌满洼地，是何等轻而易举的事？我确信，实情正是如此。且不论还用过别的什么，路堑里至今仍残留着一氧化碳，因为我丢下的那根火柴当即熄灭，我在近地面处划的每一根火柴也都灭了。倘若那辆车当时径直穿了过去，爬上路堑另一侧的坡，司机和卡车里的人或许还能免于一死。实际情况却是，司机一时昏了过去，转了方向盘，一头撞上路坡，卡车就此停住。待到弗拉克和他的同伙——不管那是何人——戴着防毒面具跳下来，把司机抬回卡车，继续往前开去时，他们多半已经死了。”

===Original===
“And the farmer﻿—” began the commissioner.
“His death probably occurred some time after the lorry had passed. He also descended into that death hollow, but the speed at which his car was going carried him up nearer the cutting, though he must have been dead by the time he got out.”
He rose and stretched himself wearily.

===Chinese===
“那么那个农夫——”总监开口道。
“他的死多半发生在卡车驶过之后一段时间。他也是驶进那个死亡洼地的人，只是他车速太疾，那股冲力把他带上了坡，直抵路堑近旁——不过等他出来的时候，想必已经死了。”
他站起身来，疲惫地伸展了一下身子。

===Original===
“Now I think I will go and interview Miss Belman and set her mind at rest,” he said. “Did you send her to the hotel, as I asked you, Mr. Simpson?”
Simpson stared at him in blank astonishment.
“Miss Belman?” he said. “I haven’t seen Miss Belman!”

===Chinese===
“现在我想我该去见一见贝尔曼小姐，让她安下心来。”他说，“辛普森先生，您照我的嘱托把她送到旅馆去了吗？”
辛普森目瞪口呆地望着他。
“贝尔曼小姐？”他说，“我没有见过贝尔曼小姐！”

## XIV / 十四



===Original===
Her head in a whirl, Margaret Belman had stepped into the cab that was waiting at the door of Larmes Keep. The door was immediately slammed behind her and the cab moved off. She saw her companion; he had shrunk into a corner of the landau, and greeted her with a little embarrassed grin. He did not speak until the cab was some distance from the house.
“My name’s Gray,” he said. “Mr. Reeder hadn’t a chance to introduce me. Sergeant Gray, C.I.D.”
“Mr. Gray, what does all this mean﻿—this instrument I am to get?”
Gray coughed. He knew nothing about the instrument, he explained, but his instructions were to put her into a car that would be waiting at the foot of the hill road.

===Chinese===
玛格丽特·贝尔曼（Margaret Belman）脑子里一团乱麻，登上了停在拉尔姆宅门前的那辆马车。车门随即在她身后砰地关上，马车启动了。她看清了同行之人；他缩在四轮马车的角落里，冲她龇出一丝局促的笑。直到马车驶出宅子老远，他才开口。
“我叫格雷。”他说，“里德先生没来得及介绍我。C.I.D. 的格雷警士（Sergeant Gray）。”
“格雷先生，这一切到底是怎么回事——要我去取的那件仪器又是怎么回事？”
格雷咳了一声。他解释说，那件仪器他一无所知；他奉到的指示，是把她送上山路脚下等候的一辆汽车。

===Original===
“Mr. Reeder wants you to go up by car. You didn’t see Brill anywhere, did you?”
“Brill?” she frowned. “Who is Brill?”
He explained that there had been two officers inside the grounds, himself and the man he had mentioned.
“But what is happening? Is there anything wrong at Larmes Keep?” she asked.

===Chinese===
“里德先生要您坐汽车回伦敦。您没在哪儿看见布里尔（Brill）吧？”
“布里尔？”她皱起眉头，“布里尔是谁？”
他解释说，宅子园内原本有两名警官，一个是他自己，另一个便是他方才提到的那位。
“可这到底是怎么回事？拉尔姆宅出了什么事吗？”她问。

===Original===
She had no need to ask the question. That look in J. G. Reeder’s eyes had told her that something indeed was very wrong.
“I don’t know, miss,” said Gray diplomatically. “All I know is that the Chief Inspector is down here with a dozen men and that looks like business. I suppose Mr. Reeder wanted to get you out of it.”
She didn’t “suppose,” she knew, and her heart beat a little quicker.
What was the mystery of Larmes Keep? Had all this to do with the disappearance of Ravini? She tried hard to think calmly and logically, but her thoughts were out of control.

===Chinese===
这问题原不必问。里德先生（Mr. J. G. Reeder）眼里那副神色早已告诉她，事情确乎大为不妙。
“我不知道，小姐。”格雷话说得圆滑，“我只知道总督察带着一打人马下来了，这架势看来是有正事。我揣摩，里德先生是想让您避开这场是非。”
她不是“揣摩”，她是知道，而且她的心跳得快了几分。
拉尔姆宅的谜团究竟是什么？这一切与拉维尼（Ravini）的失踪有关吗？她竭力想冷静地、有条理地思考，可思绪全然不听使唤。

===Original===
The landau stopped at the foot of the hill, and Gray jumped out. A little ahead of him she saw the tail light of a car drawn up by the side of the roadway.
“You’ve got the letter, miss? The car will take you straight to Scotland Yard, and Mr. Simpson will look after you.”
He followed her to the car and held open the door for her, and stood in the roadway watching till the tail light disappeared round a bend of the road.
It was a big, cosy landaulette, and Margaret made herself comfortable in the corner, pulled the rug over her knees, and settled down to the two hours’ journey. The air was a little close; she tried unsuccessfully to pull down one of the windows, then tried the other. Not only was there no glass to the windows, but the shutters were immovable. Something scratched her knuckle. She felt along the frame of the window﻿—screws, recently inserted. It was a splinter of the raw wood which had cut her.

===Chinese===
四轮马车在山脚下停住，格雷跳下车。她看见前方不远处，一辆汽车停靠在路旁，尾灯亮着。
“信您带好了吧，小姐？这辆车会送您直去苏格兰场（Scotland Yard），辛普森先生（Mr. Simpson）会照应您。”
他陪她走到车前，替她拉开车门，随后站在路上望着，直到尾灯消失在道路的拐弯处。
那是一辆宽大舒适的活动顶篷轿车。玛格丽特在角落里安顿妥当，把毯子拉过来盖在膝上，准备安度这两小时的旅程。车里空气有点闷；她想拉下一扇车窗，没成，又试另一扇。这两扇窗不但没有玻璃，遮板也纹丝不动。什么东西划了她的指节。她顺着窗框摸过去——螺丝，是新近拧上去的。划破她手的，是一小片新木茬。

===Original===
With growing uneasiness she felt for the inside handle of the door, but there was none. A search of the second door revealed a like state of affairs.
Her movements must have attracted the attention of the driver, for the glass panel was pushed back and a harsh voice greeted her.
“You can sit down and keep quiet! This isn’t Reeder’s car; I’ve sent it home.”

===Chinese===
她越来越不安，伸手去摸车门的内把手，却没有。再探另一扇门，情形一模一样。
她的动静想必惊动了司机，因为隔板玻璃被推了回来，一个粗厉的声音向她打招呼。
“您给我乖乖坐好！这不是里德的车；那辆车让我打发回去了。”

===Original===
The voice went into a chuckle that made her blood run cold.
“You’re coming with me﻿—to see life. Reeder’s going to weep tears of blood. You know me, eh? Reeder knows me. I wanted to get him tonight. But you’ll do, my dear.”
Suddenly the glass panel was shut to. He turned off the main road and was following a secondary, his object being, she guessed, to avoid the big towns and villages en route. She put out her hand and felt the wall of the car. It was an all-weather body with a leather back. If she had a knife she might cut﻿—
She gasped as a thought struck her, and, reaching up, felt the metal fastening that kept the leather hood attached. Exerting all her strength, she thrust back the flat hook and, bracing her feet against the front of the machine, pulled at the leather hood. A rush of cold air came in as the hood began slowly to collapse. The closed car was now an open car. She could afford to lose no time. The car was making thirty miles an hour, but she must take the risk of injury. Scrambling over the back of the hood, she gripped tight at the edge, and let herself drop into the roadway.

===Chinese===
那声音转为低笑，笑得她浑身的血都凉了。
“您这就跟我走——来见识见识世面。里德要痛哭血泪了。您认得我，嗯？里德认得我。我本想今夜就把他拿到手。不过您也顶用，亲爱的。”
隔板玻璃突然关上了。他驶离大路，转上一条岔道，她猜他的用意是避开沿途的大镇大村。她伸出手，摸了摸车厢的壁板。这是一部全天候封闭车身，后段蒙着皮革。她要是有把刀，兴许可以割开——
一个念头闪过，她倒抽一口冷气，伸手向上，摸到了扣住皮质顶篷的金属搭扣。她使出全身力气，顶开扁平的挂钩，双脚抵住车身前部，拽住皮顶篷猛拉。冷风灌了进来，顶篷慢慢塌落。密闭的车成了敞篷车。她一刻也耽搁不起。汽车正以时速三十英里飞驰，可她只能冒受伤之险了。她手脚并用地翻过顶篷后沿，紧紧抓住边沿，任由自己坠落到路面上。

===Original===
Although she turned a complete somersault, she escaped injury in some miraculous fashion, and, coming to her feet, cold with fear and trembling in every limb, she looked round for a way of escape. The hedge on her left was high and unpenetrable. On her right was a low wooden fence, and over this she climbed, as she heard the squeak of brakes and saw the car come to a standstill.
Even as she fled, she was puzzled to know what kind of land she was on. It was not cultivated; it was more like common land, for there was springy down beneath her feet, and clumps of gorse bushes sent out their spiny fingers to clutch at her dress as she flew past. She thought she heard the man hailing her, but fled on in the darkness.
Somewhere near at hand was the sea. She could smell the fragrance of it. Once when she stopped to take breath she could hear the distant thunder of the waves as they rolled up some unseen beach. She listened, almost deafened by the beating of her own heart.

===Chinese===
虽然整个人翻了一个跟斗，她却奇迹般地毫发无伤。她爬起身来，吓得浑身冰凉、四肢发抖，环顾四周寻找逃路。左边的树篱又高又密，无法穿越；右边是一道低矮的木栅，她翻了过去——这时正听见刹车吱的一响，看见汽车停了下来。
她一边奔逃，一边纳闷自己置身的是一片什么地界。这不是耕田；更像是一片公地，因为脚下踏着富有弹性的丘陵草地，一丛丛荆豆伸出带刺的指爪，在她飞奔而过时撕扯她的衣裙。她仿佛听见那男人在喊她，却只顾在黑暗里继续奔逃。
近处什么地方就是海。她闻得见海的气息。有一回她停下来喘气，听得见波涛滚上某片看不见的海滩时那遥远的轰鸣。她侧耳细听，几乎被自己的心跳震聋了。

===Original===
“Where are you? Come back, you fool﻿—”
The voice was near at hand. Not a dozen yards away she saw a black figure moving, and had all she could do to stifle the scream that rose in her throat. She crouched down behind a bush and waited, and then to her horror she saw a beam of light spring from the darkness. Her pursuer had an electric lamp and was fanning it across the ground.
Detection was inevitable, and, springing to her feet, she ran, doubling from side to side in the hope of outwitting him. Now she found the ground sloping under her feet, and that gave her additional speed. She had need of it, for he saw her against the skyline, and came on after her, a babbling, shrieking fury of a man. And now capture seemed inevitable. She made one wild leap to escape his outstretched hands, and her feet suddenly trod on nothing. Before she could recover, she was falling, falling. She struck a bush, and the shock and pain of the impact almost made her faint. She was falling down a steep slope, and her wild hands clutched tree and sand and grass, and then just as she had given up all hope, she found herself rolling over and over on a level plateau, and came to rest with one leg hanging over a sheer drop of two hundred feet. Happily, it was dark.

===Chinese===
“你在哪儿？回来，你这蠢货——”
那声音就在近旁。不出十几码远，她看见一条黑影在移动，她拼尽全力才压下涌到喉头的尖叫。她蹲伏到一丛灌木后面等着，接着便惊恐地看见一道光柱从黑暗中迸出。追她的人有一支手电，正把光束在地上来回扫动。
被发现已在所难免。她一跃而起，左闪右避地奔跑，指望能骗过他。这时她发觉脚下的地面正往下倾，这倒给她添了速度。她正需要这速度，因为他看见了映在天际线上的她，一路追了过来——一个语无伦次、尖声嚎叫的狂怒之人。此刻被抓住似乎已成定局。她拼命一跃，想躲开他伸来的双手，脚下忽然踏了个空。还不等她回过神来，她已经在坠落、坠落。她撞上一丛灌木，撞击的震荡和疼痛几乎使她晕厥。她正滚下一个陡坡，慌乱的双手抓过树木、沙土和荒草，随后，就在她彻底放弃希望的时候，她发觉自己正在一块平坦的台地上连滚翻滚，最后停住时，一条腿正悬在两百英尺的垂直陡落之上。幸好，天是黑的。

===Original===
Margaret Belman did not realize how near to death she had been till the dawn came up.
Below her was the sea and a stretch of yellow sand. She was looking into a little bay that held no sign of habitation so far as she could see. This was not astonishing, for the beach was only approachable from the water. Somewhere on the other side of the northern bluff, she guessed, was Siltbury. Beneath her a sheer fall over the chalky face of the cliff; above her, a terribly steep slope, which might be negotiated, she thought hopefully.
She had lost one shoe in her fall, and after a little search found this, so near to the edge of the cliff that she grew dizzy as she stooped to pick it up.
The plateau was about fifty yards long and was in the shape of a half moon, and almost entirely covered with gorse bushes. The fact that she found dozens of birds’ nests was sufficient proof that this spot was not visited even by the most daring of cliff climbers. She understood now the significance of the low rail on the side of the road, which evidently followed the sea coast westward for some miles. How far was she from Larmes Keep? she wondered﻿—until the absurdity of considering such a matter occurred to her. How near was she to starvation and death was a more present problem.

===Chinese===
直到黎明降临，玛格丽特·贝尔曼才明白自己方才离死亡有多近。
她的下方是海和一带黄沙。她正俯瞰一座小小的海湾，放眼望去不见丝毫人烟。这也不足为怪，因为这片海滩只有从水路才可抵达。她猜想，西尔特伯里（Siltbury）就在北面岬角的另一侧什么地方。她的下方，是白垩崖面上一落到底的绝壁；她的上方，是一道陡得吓人的山坡——兴许攀得上去，她想得挺乐观。
坠落时她掉了一只鞋，稍加寻找便找到了；鞋离崖边那么近，她弯腰去捡时一阵头晕目眩。
这块台地约有五十码长，呈半月形，几乎全被荆豆丛覆盖。她发现了几十个鸟巢，仅此一点便足以证明，连最大胆的攀崖好手也不曾光顾此地。她此刻才明白路边那道矮护栏的意味——那护栏显然沿着海岸向西延伸数英里。她离拉尔姆宅有多远？她自问——随即又想到，此刻盘算这种事是何等荒唐。她离饿死、离死有多近，才是眼下更要紧的问题。

===Original===
Her task was to escape from the plateau. There was a chance that she might be observed from the sea, but it was a remote one. The few pleasure boats that went out from Siltbury did not go westward; the fishing fleet invariably tacked south. Lying face downward, she looked over the edge in the vain hope that she would find an easy descent, but none was visible. She was hungry, but, though she searched the nests, there were no eggs to be found.
There was nothing to be done but to make a complete exploration of the plateau. Westward it yielded nothing, but on the eastern side she discovered a scrub-covered slope which apparently led to yet another plateau, not so broad as the one she was on.
To slide down was an easy matter; to check herself so that she did not go beyond the plateau offered greater difficulty. With infinite labour, she broke off two stout branches of a thick furze bush, and using these as a skier uses his stick to check her progress, she began to slide down, feet first. She could move slowly enough when the face of the declivity was composed of sand or loam, or when there were friendly bushes to hold, but there were broad stretches of weatherworn rock to slide across, and on these the stick made no impression and her velocity increased at an alarming rate.
And then, to her horror, she discovered that she was not keeping direction; that, try as she would, she was slipping to the left of the plateau, and though she strove desperately to move farther to the right, she made no progress.

===Chinese===
她的当务之急是逃离台地。从海上被人望见的机会不是没有，但微乎其微。西尔特伯里出海的几条游船从不往西去；渔船队向来抢风南行。她俯卧在地，探头朝崖沿下张望，徒然指望能找到一条好下的坡道，却什么也看不见。她饿了，可她翻遍了那些鸟巢，连一只蛋也没找到。
别无他法，只能把台地彻底探查一遍。往西一无所获，但在东面，她发现一道灌木覆盖的坡，看样子通向又一块台地，不比她脚下这块宽。
滑下去不难；难的是要刹住自己，别滑过了那块台地。她费了无穷的力气，从一丛茂密的荆豆上折下两根粗枝，像滑雪者用杖减速那样拄着它们，开始脚朝下滑降。坡面是沙土或壤土、或有可以攀附的灌木时，她还能滑得慢些；可还有大片的风化岩面要滑过，在这些地方，树枝杵不出半点阻力，她的速度越来越快，快得吓人。
接着她惊恐地发现，自己滑偏了方向；无论怎样挣扎，她正朝台地左侧溜去，她拼命想再往右挪，却寸步难移。

===Original===
The bushes that littered the upper slope were more infrequent here. There was indication of a recent landslide, which might continue down to the sea level or might end abruptly and disastrously over the edge of some steep cliff. Slipping, sometimes on her back, sometimes sideways, sometimes on her face, she felt her momentum increase with every yard she covered. The ends of the furze sticks were frayed to feathery splinters, and already the desired plateau was above her. Turning her head, she saw the white face of it dropping to the unseen deeps.
Now she knew the worst. The slope twisted round a huge rock and dropped at an acute angle into the sea. Almost before she could realize the danger ahead, she was slipping faster and faster through the loam and sand, the centre of a new landslide she had created. Boulders of a terrifying size accompanied her﻿—she escaped being crushed under one by a hair’s breadth.
And then without warning she was shot into the air as from a catapult. She had a swift vision of tumbling green below, and in another second the water had closed over her and she was striking out with all her strength.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
上坡满坡的灌木，到这里越来越稀。坡面上有新近滑坡的痕迹，这滑坡也许一路通到海面，也许在某道陡崖的边上戛然而止、酿成大祸。她时而仰面、时而侧身、时而扑面地滑着，每滑过一码，冲势便增一分。荆豆杖的杖头已磨成羽状的碎屑，而那块想去的台地已在她上方。她扭过头，看见它白色的坡面直坠入看不见的深处。
这下她知道了最坏的情形。坡道绕过一块巨岩拐了个弯，以一个锐角直插海中。几乎不等她看清前头的凶险，她已在壤土和沙砾间越滑越快，成了她自己引发的一场新滑坡的中心。大得骇人的巨石随她一同滚落——有一块，她只差毫发便被碾在底下。
随后，毫无预兆地，她像被弹弓射出一般抛向空中。她飞快地瞥见下方翻滚的绿浪，转眼间海水已在她头顶合拢，她拼尽全力划起水来。……

===Original===
It seemed almost an eternity before she came to the surface. Fortunately, she was a good swimmer, and, looking round, she saw that the yellow beach was less than fifty yards away. But it was fifty yards against a falling tide, and she was utterly exhausted when she dragged herself ashore and fell on the sand.
She ached from head to foot; her hands and limbs were lacerated. She felt that her body was one huge bruise. As she lay recovering her breath she heard one comforting sound, the splash of falling water. Halfway down the cliff face was a spring, and, staggering across the beach, she drank eagerly from her cupped hands. She was parched; her throat was so dry that she could hardly articulate. Hunger she might bear, but thirst was unendurable. She might remain alive for days, supposing she were not discovered before that time.
There was now no need for her to make a long reconnaissance of the beach; the way of escape lay open to her. A water-hollowed tunnel led through the bluff and showed her yet another beach beyond. Siltbury was not in sight. She had no idea how far she was from that desirable habitation of human beings, and did not trouble to think. After she had satisfied her thirst, she took off her shoes and stockings and made for the tunnel.

===Chinese===
仿佛过了几乎一个永恒，她才浮出水面。所幸她水性很好，环顾四周，看见那片黄色海滩就在五十码开外。可这是逆着退潮的五十码，待她把自己拖上岸、瘫倒在沙滩上时，已是力气耗尽。
她从头到脚无处不疼；双手和四肢都被划破了。她觉得自己整个人就是一大块青肿。她躺着缓气的当口，听见一个令人宽慰的声响——落水的溅泼声。崖壁半腰有一眼泉水。她跌跌撞撞地横过海滩，掬起双手急切地喝了起来。她渴得嗓子冒烟；喉咙干得几乎发不出声。饥饿尚可忍受，干渴却无法承受。倘若在那之前无人发现她，她兴许还能活上几天。
如今她用不着把海滩细细侦察一遍了；逃生之路已向她敞开。一条被水冲蚀的隧道穿过岬角，让她望见那边还有一片海滩。西尔特伯里仍不见踪影。她离那处令人向往的人烟之地还有多远，她毫无概念，也懒得去想。喝足了水，她脱下鞋袜，朝隧道走去。

===Original===
The second bay was larger and the beach longer. There were, she found, small masses of rocks jutting far into the sea that had to be negotiated with bare feet. The beach was longer than she had thought, and, so far as she could see, there was no outlet, nor did the cliff diminish in height. She had expected to find a cliff path, and this hope was strengthened when she discovered the rotting hull of a boat drawn high and dry on the beach.
It was, she judged, about eight o’clock in the morning. She had started wet through, but the warm sun dried her rags﻿—as rags they were. She had all the sensations of a shipwrecked mariner on a desert island, and after a while the loneliness and absence of all kinds of human society began to get on her nerves.
Before she reached the end of the beach she saw that the only way into the next bay was by swimming to where the rocky barrier was low enough to be climbed. She could with great comfort to herself have discarded what remained of her clothes, but beyond these rocks might lie civilization; so, tying her wet shoes and stockings together, she made fast her shoes, and, knotting the stockings about her waist, waded into the sea and swam steadily, looking for a likely place to land. This she found﻿—a step-shaped pyramid of rocks that looked easier to negotiate than in fact they were. By dint of hard climbing, she came to the summit.

===Chinese===
第二个海湾更大，海滩也更长。她发现有一堆堆礁石远远探进海里，必须赤脚攀越。这海滩比她想的还长，而且放眼望去没有出口，崖壁也不见低下去。她原指望找到一条崖径，而当她发现一条烂穿的船壳高高搁在干滩上时，这希望更浓了。
她估摸这时约莫早晨八点。动身时她浑身湿透，但温暖的太阳晒干了她那身破衣烂衫——那确已是烂衫了。她满心都是荒岛遇难水手的况味，过了一阵子，这份孤寂、这彻底不见人烟的处境，开始让她心神不宁。
还没走到海滩尽头她便看出，进入下一个海湾的唯一路子，是游到那道石障足够低、可以攀爬的地方。她本可以把身上剩下的衣物一丢了之，图个痛快自在，可这些礁石的那头兴许就是文明世界；于是她把湿鞋湿袜系在一起，把鞋扎牢，长袜缠在腰间，蹚进海里稳稳地游着，寻一处合适的登岸点。这地方她找到了——一座阶梯状的金字塔形礁石，看着好爬，实则不然。她拼力攀爬，总算登上了顶端。

===Original===
The beach here was shorter; the cliff considerably higher. Across the shoulder of rock running to the sea she saw the white houses of Siltbury, and the sight gave her courage. Descending from the rocky ridge was even more difficult than climbing, and she was grateful when at last she sat upon a flat ledge and dangled her bruised feet in the water.
Swimming back to the land taxed her strength to the full. It was nearly an hour before her feet touched firm sand and she staggered up the beach. Here she rested until the pangs of hunger drove her toward the last visible obstacle.
There was one which was not visible. After a quarter of an hour’s walk, she found her way barred by a deep sea river which ran under the overhung cliff. She had seen this place before﻿—where was it? And then she remembered, with an exclamation.

===Chinese===
这里的海滩更短；崖壁也高得多。越过那道伸向海中的岩肩，她望见了西尔特伯里的白色房屋，这景象给了她勇气。从岩脊下来比上去还难，待她终于坐上一块平整的石台、把磨伤的双脚浸在水里晃荡时，心里满是感激。
游回岸边耗尽了她的力气。差不多过了一个钟头，她的双脚才踩到坚实的沙地，跌跌撞撞地走上海滩。她在这里歇脚，直到饥饿的绞痛驱使她奔向最后一处看得见的障碍。
有一处障碍却是看不见的。走了一刻钟，她发现去路被一条幽深的海潮河拦住，河水在探出的崖壁下流淌。这地方她以前见过——在哪儿来着？随即她想起来了，不由轻呼一声。

===Original===
This was the cave that Olga had told her about, the cave that ran under Larmes Keep. Shading her eyes, she looked up. Yes, there was the little landslide part of the wall that had been carried away projected from a heap of rubble on the cliff side.
Suddenly Margaret saw something which made her breath come faster. On the edge of the deep channel which the water had cut in the sand was the print of a boot, a large, square-toed boot with a rubber heel. It had been recently made. She looked farther along the channel and saw another﻿—it led to the mouth of the cave. On either side of the rugged entrance was a billow of firm sand left by the retreating waters, and again she saw the footprint. A visitor to the cave, perhaps, she thought. Presently he would come out and she would explain her plight, though her appearance left little need for explanation.
She waited, but there was no sign of the man. Stooping, she tried to peer into its dark depths. Perhaps, if she were inside out of the light, she could see better. She walked gingerly along the sand ledge, but as yet her eyes, unaccustomed to the darkness, revealed nothing.
She took another step, passed into the entrance of the cave; and then, from somewhere behind, a bare arm was flung round her shoulder, a big hand closed over her mouth. In terror, she struggled madly, but the man held her in a grip of iron. Then her senses left her and she sank limply into his arms.

===Chinese===
这就是奥尔加（Olga）跟她说过的那个山洞，那条在拉尔姆宅底下穿行的洞。她手搭凉棚仰头望去。没错，从崖壁一堆碎石间支出来的，正是那一小段被塌方带走的围墙。
突然，玛格丽特看见一样东西，她的呼吸顿时急促起来。在水流于沙中切出的深槽边缘，有一个靴印——一只大方头、胶后跟的靴子。是新近踩下的。她顺着水槽再往前看，又看见一个——直通洞口。在那崎岖洞口的两侧，退水留下了一垄垄坚实的沙，上面又是那个脚印。大概是有个人来洞里做客吧，她想。他一会儿就会出来，她要把自己的窘境说明一番——虽然她这副模样，几乎不用说明什么了。
她等着，却不见那人的踪影。她弯下腰，竭力往洞里的黑暗深处张望。也许，到得洞内、避开亮处，她能看得更清楚。她小心翼翼地沿着沙台往里走，但她的眼睛还不习惯黑暗，暂时什么也看不见。
她又迈了一步，走进了洞口；就在这时，从身后的什么地方，一条赤裸的手臂揽住了她的肩头，一只大手捂住了她的嘴。她惊恐万分，拼命挣扎，可那男人用铁钳般的劲道攫住了她。随后她便失去了知觉，软软地瘫倒在他怀里。

## XV / 十五

===Original===
Mr. Reeder was not an emotional man. For the first time in his life Inspector Simpson learned that behind the calm and imperturbable demeanour of the Public Prosecutor’s chief detective lay an immense capacity for violent language. He fired a question at the officer, and Simpson nodded.

“Yes, the car returned. The driver said that he had orders to go back to London. I thought you had changed your plans. You’re staying with this bullion robbery, Reeder?”

Mr. Reeder glared across the desk, and despite his hardihood Inspector Simpson winced.

“Staying with hell!” hissed Reeder.

===Chinese===
里德先生不是个感情外露的人。平生头一遭，辛普森督察得知，在这位检察总长首席侦探平静无波的外表底下，竟潜藏着满腔疾言厉色的火气。他劈头向这位警官抛出一个问题，辛普森点了点头。

“是的，车回来了。司机说他奉了命令回伦敦去。我还以为你改变了计划。你还要留在这桩黄金劫案上吗，里德？”

里德先生隔着桌子怒目而视，辛普森督察尽管素来胆壮，也不由得瑟缩了一下。

“留个鬼！”里德从牙缝里嘶声挤出一句。

===Original===
Simpson was seeing the real and unsuspected J. G. Reeder and was staggered.

“I’m going back to interview that monkey-faced criminologist, and I’m going to introduce him to forms of persuasion which have been forgotten since the Inquisition!”

Before Simpson could reply, Mr. Reeder was out of the door and flying down the stairs.

---

===Chinese===
辛普森此刻见到的，是那个真实的、从未显露过的 J. G. 里德，不由得骇然。

“我要回去会一会那位猴脸犯罪学家，我要让他领教几种劝人开口的手段——自宗教裁判所以来就失传了的那种！”

辛普森还没来得及答话，里德先生已经出了门，一阵风似的冲下楼去。

---

===Original===
It was the hour after lunch, and Daver was sitting at his desk, twiddling his thumbs, when the door was pushed open unceremoniously and Mr. Reeder came in. He did not recognize the detective, for a man who in a moment of savage humour slices off his side whiskers brings about an amazing change in his appearance. And with the banishing of those ornaments, there had been a remarkable transformation in Mr. Reeder’s demeanour. Gone were his useless pince-nez which had fascinated a generation of lawbreakers; gone the gentle, apologetic voice, the shyly diffident manner.

“I want you, Daver!”

“Mr. Reeder!” gasped the yellow-faced man, and turned a shade paler.

Reeder slammed the door to behind him, pulled up a chair with a crash, and sat down opposite the hotel proprietor.

===Chinese===
午饭刚过的时辰，达弗正坐在书桌前摆弄着两只大拇指，房门忽然被人毫不客气地推开，里德先生走了进来。他没有认出这位侦探，因为一个人在一阵凶蛮的兴头上一刀削去自己的连鬓胡子，相貌便会起惊人的变化。而随着这两件点缀的消失，里德先生的气度也发生了显著的变化。那副叫一代罪犯看得出神、其实全无用处的夹鼻眼镜不见了；那温吞吞、赔着小心的嗓音，那怯生生的拘谨做派，也一并不见了。

“我要找的就是你，达弗！”

“里德先生！”黄脸人倒抽一口气，脸色又白了一层。

里德反手砰地把门关上，哐啷一声拖过一把椅子，在这位旅馆老板对面坐了下来。

===Original===
“Where is Miss Belman?”

“Miss Belman?”

Astonishment was expressed in every feature. “Good gracious, Mr. Reeder, surely you know? She went up to get your dactyloscope﻿—is that the word? I intended asking you to be good enough to let me see this﻿—”

“Where﻿—is﻿—Miss﻿—Belman? Spill it, Daver, and save yourself a lot of unhappiness.”

“I swear to you, my dear Mr. Reeder﻿—”

===Chinese===
“贝尔曼小姐在哪里？”

“贝尔曼小姐？”

他满脸五官都写着惊讶。“天哪，里德先生，您还不知道？她上楼去取您的指纹记录来着——是这么个词儿吧？我本想劳您的驾，容我看上一看这东西——”

“贝尔曼——小姐——到底——在哪里？统统吐出来，达弗，省得你自己多吃许多苦头。”

“我向您发誓，亲爱的里德先生——”

===Original===
Reeder leaned across the table and rang the bell.

“Do﻿—do you want anything?” stammered the manager.

“I want to speak to Mrs. Flack﻿—you call her Mrs. Burton, but Mrs. Flack is good enough for me!”

Daver’s face was ghastly now. He had become suddenly wizened and old.

===Chinese===
里德俯身过桌，按响了铃。

“您——您要些什么？”经理结结巴巴地说。

“我要同弗拉克太太说话——你们管她叫伯顿太太，可在我这儿，弗拉克太太这称呼就尽够了！”

达弗的脸这时已是一片死灰。他整个人蓦地干缩、见老。

===Original===
“I’m one of the few people who happen to know that John Flack is married,” said Reeder; “one of the few who knows he has a daughter. The question is, does John Flack know all that I know?”

He glowered down at the shrinking man.

“Does he know that after he was sent to Broadmoor his sneaking worm of a secretary, his toady and parasite and slave, decided to carry on in the Flack tradition, and use his influence and his knowledge to compel the unfortunate daughter of mad John Flack to marry him?”

===Chinese===
“世上碰巧知道约翰·弗拉克结过婚的人没有几个，我是其中之一，”里德说，“知道他有个女儿的也没有几个，我也是其中之一。问题在于：约翰·弗拉克知不知道我所知道的这一切？”

他虎视眈眈地俯视着那个缩作一团的人。

“他知不知道，自从他被送进布罗德莫尔，他那条鬼祟虫子一样的秘书——他的马屁精、寄生虫兼奴才——便打定主意承袭弗拉克家的传统，用他的势力和他的门路，逼疯子约翰·弗拉克那不幸的女儿嫁给他？”

===Original===
A frenzied, almost incoherent voice wailed:

“For God’s sake﻿—don’t talk so loud.”

But Mr. Reeder went on:

===Chinese===
一个狂乱的、语无伦次的声音哀告道：

“看在上帝份上——别这么大声说话。”

可里德先生只顾说下去：

===Original===
“Before Flack went to prison he entrusted to his daughter his famous encyclopaedia of crime. She was the only person he trusted; his wife was a weak slave whom he had always despised. Mr. Daver, the secretary, got possession of those books a year after Flack was committed to Broadmoor. He organized his own little gang at Flack’s old headquarters, which were nominally bought by you. Ever since you knew John Flack was planning an escape﻿—an escape in which you had to assist him﻿—you’ve been living in terror that he would discover how you had double-crossed him. Tell me I’m a liar and I’ll beat your miserable little head off. Where is Margaret Belman?”

“I don’t know,” said the man sullenly. “Flack had a car waiting for her﻿—that’s all I know.”

===Chinese===
“弗拉克入狱之前，把他那部有名的犯罪百科全书托付给了女儿。她是他唯一信得过的人；他的妻子不过是个懦弱的奴隶，一向遭他鄙夷。达弗先生，那位秘书，在弗拉克被关进布罗德莫尔一年之后，把那些书弄到了手。他在弗拉克的老巢组织起自己的一小伙人马，那处老巢名义上还是买在你名下的。自从你知道约翰·弗拉克在策划越狱——一桩你不得不从旁出力的越狱——你就一直战战兢兢地度日，生怕他发现你是怎样出卖了他。说我撒谎，我就把你那颗可怜的小脑袋揍扁。玛格丽特·贝尔曼在哪里？”

“我不知道。”那汉子阴沉着脸说。“弗拉克备了一辆车等她——我知道的就这么些。”

===Original===
Something in his tone, something in the shifty slant of his eyes infuriated Reeder. He stretched out a long arm, gripped the man by the collar and jerked him savagely across the desk. As a feat of physical strength it was remarkable; as a piece of propaganda of the frightfulness that was to follow, it had a strange effect upon Daver. He lay limp for a second, and then, with a quick jerk of his collar, he wrenched himself from Reeder’s grip and fled from the room, slamming the door behind him. By the time Reeder had kicked an overturned chair from his path, and opened the door, Daver had disappeared.

When Reeder reached the hall, it was empty. He met none of the servants (he learned later that the majority had been discharged that morning, paid a month’s wages, and sent to town by the first train). He ran out of the main entrance on to the lawn, but the man he sought was not in sight. The other side of the house drew blank. One of the detectives on duty in the grounds, attracted by Mr. Reeder’s hasty exit, came running into the vestibule as he reached the bottom of the stairs.

===Chinese===
他话音里的某种东西，他眼里那道贼溜溜的斜光里的某种东西，把里德惹得火起。他伸出长长的胳膊，一把攥住那人的衣领，隔着书桌狠狠将他猛拽过来。论膂力，这一手着实惊人；而作为对接下来那场雷霆手段的预告，它在达弗身上起了奇效。他瘫软了一秒钟，随即猛地一挣衣领，从里德的掌中挣脱出去，逃出了房间，反手把门砰地摔上。等里德踢开挡路的一把翻倒的椅子、推门出去时，达弗已经无影无踪。

里德赶到大厅时，里面空无一人。他一个仆人也没撞见（后来他才得知，大部分仆人当天上午已被辞退，多给一个月的工钱，打发头班火车进城去了）。他跑出正门奔到草坪上，可他要找的人踪影全无。宅子另一头也扑了个空。他刚到楼梯脚下，一名在庭院里执勤的侦探被里德先生这阵仓皇的奔出引来，跑进门厅来。

===Original===
“Nobody came out, sir,” he said, when Reeder explained the object of his search.

“How many men are there in the grounds?” asked Reeder shortly. “Four? Bring them into the house. Lock every door, and bring back a crowbar with you. I am going to do a little investigation that may cost me a lot of money. No sign of Brill?”

“No, sir,” said the detective, shaking his head sadly. “Poor old Brill! I’m afraid they’ve done him. The young lady get to town all right, sir?”

===Chinese===
里德说明了要找的是谁之后，他说道：“没有人出来过，先生。”

“庭院里一共有几个人？”里德简短地问。“四个？把他们叫进宅子里来。锁上每一扇门，随身带一根撬棍回来。我要做一点小小的查勘，兴许要破费我一大笔钱。布里尔还没有踪影？”

“没有，先生。”侦探说着，难过地摇了摇头。“可怜的老布里尔！恐怕他们把他干掉了。小姐平安到城里了吗，先生？”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder scowled at him.

“The young lady﻿—what do you know about her?” he asked sharply.

“I saw her to the car,” said Detective Gray.

Reeder gripped him by the coat and led him into the vestibule.

===Chinese===
里德先生朝他沉下脸来。

“那位小姐——你知道她些什么？”他厉声问。

“我送她上的车。”格雷侦探说。

里德一把抓住他的上衣，把他领进了门厅。

===Original===
“Now, tell me, and tell me quickly, what sort of car was it?”

“I don’t know, Mr. Reeder,” said the man in surprise. “An ordinary kind of car, except that the windows were shuttered, but I thought that was your idea.”

“What sort of body had it?”

===Chinese===
“好，告诉我，快告诉我，那是辆什么样的车？”

“说不上来，里德先生。”那人诧异地说。“就是一辆寻常样式的车，只是车窗都封了挡板，我还当那是您的意思呢。”

“车身是哪一种的？”

===Original===
The man described the machine as accurately as possible; he had only made a superficial inspection. He thought, however, it was an all-weather body. The news was no more than Reeder had expected; neither added to nor diminished his anxiety. When Gray had returned with his three companions and the doors had been locked, Mr. Reeder, from the landing above, called them to the first floor. A very thorough search had already been made by the police that morning; but, so far, Daver’s room had escaped anything but superficial attention. It was situated at the far end of the corridor, and was locked when the search party arrived. It took less than two minutes to force an entrance. Mr. Daver’s suite consisted of a sitting room, a bedroom, and a handsomely fitted bathroom. There were a number of books in the former, a small empire table on which were neatly arranged a pile of accounts, but there was nothing in the way of documents to reveal his relationship with the Flack gang.

The bedroom was beautifully furnished. Here again, from Reeder’s point of view, the search was unsatisfactory.

===Chinese===
那人尽其所能把那辆车描述了一遍；他原本也只草草看过一眼。不过他记得，那是一副全天候的车身。这消息不出里德所料，既没有增添也没有减轻他的忧急。格雷带着三个同伴回来、各处房门都已锁上之后，里德先生从上面的楼梯平台上唤他们上二楼去。警方当天上午已做过一番十分彻底的搜查；但直到此时，达弗的房间所受的仍旧不过是走马观花的一瞥。它位于走廊尽头，搜查的人到时房门锁着。破门而入没花上两分钟。达弗先生的套房由一间起居室、一间卧室和一间陈设华丽的浴室组成。起居室里有不少书籍，一张帝政式小桌上整整齐齐码着一叠账目，却没有任何文件能揭示他与弗拉克帮的关系。

卧室陈设精美。在里德看来，这一处的搜查依旧不能令人满意。

===Original===
The suite formed one of the angles of the old Keep, and Reeder was leaving the room when his eyes, roving back for a last look around, were arrested by the curious position of a brown leather divan in one corner of the room. He went back and tried to pull it away from the wall, but apparently it was a fixture. He kicked at the draped side and it gave forth a hollow wooden sound.

“What has he got in that divan?” he asked.

After considerable search Gray found a hidden bolt, and, throwing this back, the top of the divan came up like the lid of a box. It was empty.

“The rum thing about this house, sir,” said Gray as they went downstairs together, “is that one always seems on the point of making an important discovery and it always turns out to be a dud.”

===Chinese===
这套房间位于古老主堡的一角。里德正要离开，目光回转来最后扫视一周，忽然被墙角里一张棕色皮面长榻的古怪位置摄住了。他走回去，想把榻从墙边拉开，它却分明是固定死的。他朝蒙着罩幔的榻身踢了一脚，它发出空木头的声响。

“他那张长榻里藏了什么？”他问。

找了好一番，格雷才寻到一处暗藏的闩，把闩拨了回去，长榻的顶面便像箱盖一样掀了起来。里面空空如也。

“这宅子有桩邪门的地方，先生，”两人一同下楼时格雷说，“人老像是马上要有一桩重大的发现了，可到头来回回都是空炮。”

===Original===
Reeder did not reply; he was too preoccupied with his growing distress. After a while, he spoke.

“There are many queer things about this house﻿—” he began.

And then there came a sound which froze the marrow of his bones. It was a shrill shriek; the scream of a human soul in agony.

===Chinese===
里德没有答话；他满心都是愈积愈深的忧惧，顾不上别的。过了一会儿，他才开口。

“这座宅子有许多蹊跷——”他刚起了个头。

便在这时，传来一个声音，把他骨头缝里的骨髓都冻住了。那是一声尖利的惨叫，是一个人的灵魂在极痛中的呼号。

===Original===
“Help! Help, Reeder!”

It came from the direction of the room he had left, and he recognized Daver’s voice.

“Oh, God﻿—!”

===Chinese===
“救命！救命，里德！”

声音来自他方才离开的那个房间的方向，他听出那是达弗的嗓音。

“哦，上帝——！”

===Original===
The sound of a door slamming. Reeder took the stairs three at a time, the detectives following him. Daver’s door he had left ajar, but in the short time he had been downstairs it had been shut and bolted.

“The crowbar, quick!”

Gray had left it below and, flying down, returned in a few seconds.

===Chinese===
一声摔门的巨响。里德三级并作一级地蹿上楼梯，侦探们紧随其后。达弗的房门他走时是虚掩着的，可就在他下楼的这一小会儿工夫里，门已经关上，还落了闩。

“撬棍，快！”

撬棍被格雷丢在了楼下，他飞奔下去，几秒钟便取了回来。

===Original===
No sound came from the room. Pushing the claw of the crowbar between architrave and door at the point where he had seen the bolt, Reeder levered it back, and the door flew open with a crash. One step into the apartment, and then he stood stock still, glaring at the bed, unable to believe his eyes.

On the silken counterpane, sprawled in an indescribable attitude, his round, sightless eyes staring at the ceiling, was Daver. Mr. Reeder knew that he was dead before he saw the terrible wound or the brown-hilted knife that stuck out from his side.

Reeder leaned down and listened for a heartbeat﻿—felt the still warm wrist, but it was a waste of time, as he knew.

===Chinese===
房间里没有一丝声息。里德把撬棍的弯爪插进门框与门扇之间、正对着他方才瞧见门闩的地方，用力一别，房门砰的一声弹开了。他一步跨进房间，随即钉在原地，死死瞪着那张床，不敢相信自己的眼睛。

绸缎床罩上，达弗摊成一副难以言状的姿势，圆睁的、已无神采的眼睛直瞪着天花板。还没看见那可怕的伤口，还没看见从他肋侧戳出来的棕色柄小刀，里德先生就知道他已经死了。

里德俯下身去听了听心跳——又摸了摸那尚有余温的手腕，但他心里明白，这是白费工夫。

===Original===
He made a quick search of the clothing. There was an inside pocket in the waistcoat, and here he found a thick pad of banknotes.

“All thousands,” said Mr. Reeder, “and ninety-five of them. What’s in that packet?”

It was a little cardboard folder and contained a steamship ticket from Southampton to New York, made out in the name of “Sturgeon,” and in the coat pocket Reeder found a passport which was stamped by the American Consul and bore the same name.

===Chinese===
他把死者的衣服迅速搜了一遍。背心上有个内袋，他在里面摸到厚厚一沓钞票。

“全是千镑的大票，”里德先生说，“一共九十五张。那只纸夹里是什么？”

那是个小小的硬纸夹，里面装着一张南安普敦至纽约的轮船票，抬头开的姓名是“斯特金”；在外衣口袋里，里德又找到一本护照，上面盖着美国领事的签章，用的也是同一个名字。

===Original===
“He was ready to jump﻿—but he delayed it too long,” he said. “Poor devil!”

“How did he get here, sir?” asked Gray. “They couldn’t have carried him﻿—”

“He was alive enough when we heard him,” said Reeder curtly. “He was being killed when we heard him shriek. There is a way into this room we haven’t discovered yet. What’s that?”

===Chinese===
“他早就准备好开溜了——只是拖得太久了。”他说。“可怜的家伙！”

“他是怎么进这屋里来的，先生？”格雷问。“总不可能是被人抬进来的——”

“我们听见他声音的时候，他还活得好好的。”里德简截地说。“我们听见他惨叫的时候，他正被人结果。这房间有一条我们还没发现的通路。那是什么？”

===Original===
It was the sound of a muffled thud, as if a heavy door had been closed. It seemed to come from somewhere in the room. Reeder took the crowbar from the detective’s hand and attacked the panel behind the settee. Beneath was solid wall. He ripped down another strip, with no more enlightening result. Again he opened the divan. Its bottom was made of a thin layer of oak. This, too, was ripped off; beneath this again was the stone floor.

“Strip it,” said Reeder, and when this was done he stepped inside the divan and seesawed gingerly from one end to the other.

“There’s nothing here,” he said. “Go downstairs and phone Mr. Simpson. Tell him what has happened.”

When the man had gone, he resumed his examination of the body. Daver had carried, attached to one of the buttons of his trousers, a long gold chain. This was gone; he found it broken off close to the link, and the button itself hanging by a thread. It was while he was making his examination that his hand touched a bulky package in the dead man’s hip pocket. It was a worn leather case, filled with scraps of memoranda, mostly indecipherable. They were written in a formless hand, generally with pencil, and the writing was large and irregular, while the paper used for these messages was of every variety. One was a scrawled chemical formula; another was a brief note which ran:

===Chinese===
那是一声闷闷的钝响，仿佛一扇沉重的门被合上了。声音似乎就出自房间里某个地方。里德从侦探手里拿过撬棍，撬向长椅后面的壁板。壁板底下是结结实实的墙壁。他又撕下一条，结果同样不见分晓。他再次掀开长榻。榻底是一层薄橡木板。这层板也撬了下来；板下又是石头的地面。

“全撬了。”里德说。撬完之后，他跨进长榻里，小心翼翼地从这一端晃到那一端。

“这里头什么也没有。”他说。“下楼去打电话给辛普森先生。把出的事情告诉他。”

那人走后，他继续查验尸体。达弗原先在裤腰的一粒扣子上拴着一条长长的金链。链子不见了；他发现它在链环根上给扯断了，那粒扣子本身也只靠一根线头挂着。正是在查验之际，他的手触到了死者后裤袋里一个鼓鼓囊囊的包裹。那是一只磨旧的皮夹，塞满了记事的碎纸片，大都字迹难辨。那些字出自一种不成体统的笔迹，一般用铅笔写成，字大而歪斜，写这些字条的用纸则五花八门，应有尽有。有一张是潦草涂出的一个化学式；另一张是一则简短的便条，写的是：

===Original===
> “House opposite Reeder to let. Engage or get key. Communicate usual place.”

Some of these notes were understandable, some beyond Mr. Reeder’s comprehension. But he came at last to a scrap which swept the colour from his cheeks. It was written in the same hand on the margin of a newspaper and was crumpled into a ball:

===Chinese===
> “里德对面的房子招租。租下或弄到钥匙。照老地方联络。”

这些字条有的看得懂，有的则超出了里德先生的理解力。但他终于翻到一张纸片，登时把他脸颊上的血色一扫而光。它同样出自那笔字迹，写在一张报纸的边栏上，揉成了一团：

===Original===
> “Belman fell over cliff six miles west Larme. Send men to get body before police discover.”

Mr. J. G. Reeder read and the room spun round.

===Chinese===
> “贝尔曼坠崖，拉尔姆以西六英里。派人赶在警察发现前把尸体弄回。”

J. G. 里德先生读罢，整个房间在他眼前旋转了起来。

## XVI / 十六

===Original===
When Margaret Belman recovered consciousness, she was in the open air, lying in a little recess, effectively hidden from the mouth of the cave. A man in a torn shirt and ragged trousers was standing by her side, looking down at her. As she opened her eyes she saw him put his finger to his mouth, as though to signal silence. His hair was unkempt; streaks of dried blood zigzagged down his face, and the hair above, she saw, was matted. Yet there was a certain kindliness in his disfigured face which reassured her, as he knelt down and, making a funnel of his hands, whispered:

“Be quiet! I’m sorry to have frightened you, but I was scared you’d shout if you saw me. I suppose I look pretty awful.”

His grin was very reassuring.

“Who are you?” she answered in the same tone.

“My name’s Brill, C.I.D.”

===Chinese===
玛格丽特·贝尔曼恢复知觉时，人已在露天之下，躺在一处小小的凹壁里，正好全然避开了洞口的视线。一个衬衫撕裂、长裤破烂的男人站在她身旁，低头打量她。她睁开眼睛，看见他把手指按在唇上，像是示意噤声。他头发蓬乱，一道道干涸的血痕在脸上纵横交错，血痕上方的头发，她看得出，已纠结成毡。然而那张破了相的脸上自有一份和善，叫她安下心来。他跪下身，双手拢成喇叭口，低声道：

“别作声！对不起，吓着你了，我怕你一看见我会喊出声来。我想我这副模样一定难看得很。”

他咧嘴一笑，极叫人安心。

“你是谁？”她用同样低的声调应道。

“我叫布里尔，C.I.D.（刑事部）的。”

===Original===
“How did you get here?” she asked.

“I’d like to be able to tell you,” he answered grimly. “You’re Miss Belman, aren’t you?”

She nodded. He lifted his head, listening, and, flattening himself against the rock, craned out slowly and peeped round the edge of his hiding place. He did not move for about five minutes, and by this time she had risen to her feet. Her knees were dreadfully shaky; she felt physically sick; her mouth was dry and parched.

Apparently satisfied, he crept back to her side.

“I was left on duty in Reeder’s room. I thought I heard him calling from the window﻿—you can’t distinguish voices when they whisper﻿—asking me to come out quick as he wanted me. I’d hardly dropped to the ground before﻿—gosh!” He touched his head gingerly and winced. “That’s all I remember till I woke up and found myself drowning. I’ve been in the cave all the morning﻿—naturally.”

===Chinese===
“你是怎么到这儿来的？”她问。

“我倒希望能说得上来，”他沉着脸答道，“你是贝尔曼小姐，对不对？”

她点了点头。他抬起头听了听，随即贴着岩壁，缓缓探出身去，从藏身处的边沿窥望外面。他约莫五分钟一动不动，这时她已站起身来。她的膝盖抖得厉害，一阵阵地犯恶心，嘴里又干又燥。

看来是放下了心，他爬回她身边。

“我被留在里德的房间里执勤。我仿佛听见他从窗口唤我——人咬着耳朵说话，你是分不清嗓音的——叫我快些出去，说他用得着我。我脚刚一沾地就——天哪！”他小心翼翼地摸了摸脑袋，疼得直咧嘴。“再往后我记得的，就是醒来发觉自己正在水里没顶。我一上午都待在洞里——这很自然。”

===Original===
“Why naturally?” she whispered.

“Because the beach is covered with water at high tide and the cave’s the only place. It is a little too densely populated for me just now.”

She stared at him in amazement.

“Populated? What do you mean?”

“Whisper!” he warned her, for she had raised her voice.

===Chinese===
“为什么很自然？”她悄声问。

“因为涨潮时海滩全让水盖住了，能待的只有这洞。只是眼下这里‘人口’稍稍稠密了些，不大合我的意。”

她惊异地瞪着他。

“人口？你这是什么意思？”

“小声！”他告诫她，因为她抬高了嗓门。

===Original===
Again he listened.

“I’d like to know how they get down﻿—Daver and that old devil.”
