She felt herself going white.

“You mean﻿—Flack?”

He nodded.

===Chinese===
他又听了听。

“我很想知道他们是怎么下来的——达弗，还有那个老魔鬼。”

她觉得自己脸上发白。

“你是说——弗拉克？”

他点了点头。

===Original===
“Flack’s only been here about an hour, and how he got down, God knows. I suppose our fellows are patrolling the house?”

“The police?” she asked in astonishment.

“Flack’s headquarters﻿—didn’t you know it? I suppose you wouldn’t. I thought Reeder﻿—I mean Mr. Reeder﻿—told you everything.”

He was rather a talkative young man, more than a little exuberant at finding himself alive, and with good reason.

“I’ve been dodging in and out the cave all the morning. They’ve got a sentry on duty up there”﻿—he nodded toward Siltbury. “It’s a marvellous organization. They held up a gold convoy this morning and got away with it﻿—I heard the old man telling his daughter. The strange thing is that, though he wasn’t there to superintend the steal, his plan worked out like clockwork. It’s a curious thing, any crook will work for old Flack. He’s employed the cleverest people in the business, and Ravini is the only man that ever sold him.”

===Chinese===
“弗拉克到这儿才一个来钟头，他是怎么下来的，只有天晓得。我估摸着，咱们的人正在宅子里巡逻吧？”

“警察？”她愕然问。

“弗拉克的老巢——你不知道吗？也难怪，你不会知道的。我还以为里德——我是说里德先生——什么都告诉你了。”

他是个挺健谈的年轻人，发现自己居然还活着，那份兴高采烈未免过了头，倒也事出有因。

“我一上午都在洞里钻进躲出。他们上头设了岗哨”——他朝西尔特伯里的方向点了点头——“组织得妙极了。今天早上他们劫了一支黄金押运队，得手跑掉了——是那老头亲口对女儿说的。奇怪的是，那一票虽不是他亲临主持，他的计划却走得像钟表一样准。还有一桩怪事：哪一路的贼都肯替老弗拉克卖命。这一行里最聪明的人物，他雇了个遍，拉维尼是唯一出卖过他的人。”

===Original===
“Do you know what has happened to Mr. Ravini?” she asked, and he shook his head.

“He’s dead, I expect. There are a lot of things in the cave that I haven’t seen, and some that I have. They’ve got a petrol boat inside as big as a church﻿—the boat I mean﻿—Hush!”

Again he shrank against the cliff. Voices were coming nearer and nearer. Perhaps it was the peculiar acoustics of the cave which gave him the illusion that the speakers were standing almost at their elbow. Brill recognized the thin, harsh voice of the old man and grinned again, but it was not a pleasant smile to see.

“There’s something wrong, something damnably wrong. What is it, Olga?”

“Nothing, Father.”

===Chinese===
“你知道拉维尼先生出了什么事吗？”她问。他摇了摇头。

“我料想他死了。这洞里有许多东西我还没见过，也有一些我见过了。他们里头藏着一条汽油艇，有座教堂那么大——我是说那条船——嘘！”

他又缩身贴上崖壁。人声愈来愈近。也许是洞里奇特的传声效果叫他生出错觉，以为说话的人几乎就贴着他们的胳膊肘。布里尔听出那老头又细又刺的嗓音，又咧嘴一笑，只是那笑容瞧着并不叫人受用。

“有点不对劲，有件大大不对劲的事。怎么回事，奥尔加？”

“没什么，父亲。”

===Original===
Margaret recognized the voice of Olga Crewe.

“You have been very good and very patient, my love, and I would not have planned to come out, but I wanted to see you settled in life. I am very ambitious for you, Olga.”

A pause, and then:

“Yes, Father.”

Olga Crewe’s voice was a little dispirited, but apparently the old man did not notice this.

===Chinese===
玛格丽特听出了奥尔加·克鲁的声音。

“你一向乖得很，也耐心得很，我的宝贝。我本不打算谋划出来，可我想亲眼看你终身有托。我为你怀着极大的期望，奥尔加。”

停了停，接着是：

“是的，父亲。”

奥尔加·克鲁的声音有些无精打采，但老头显然没有察觉。

===Original===
“You are to have the finest husband in the land, my dear. You shall have a house that any princess would envy. It shall be of white marble with golden cupolas﻿—you shall be the richest woman in the land, Olga. I have planned this for you. Night after night as I lay in bed in that dreadful place I said to myself: ‘I must go out and settle Olga’s future.’ That is why I came out﻿—only for that reason. All my life I have worked for you.”

“Mother says﻿—” began the girl.

“Pah!” Old John Flack almost spat the word. “An unimaginative commoner with the soul of a housekeeper! She has looked after you well? Good. All the better for her. I would never have forgiven her if she had neglected you. And Daver? He has been respectful? He has given you all the money you wanted?”

“Yes, Father.”

Margaret thought she detected a catch in the girl’s voice.

===Chinese===
“你将嫁全国最出色的丈夫，亲爱的。你会有一座任何公主见了都要眼红的宅邸。它要用白大理石筑成，覆着金色的穹顶——你要做全国最有钱的女人，奥尔加。这一切我都为你谋划好了。在那个可怕的地方，我一夜一夜躺在床上，心里对自己说：‘我必须出去，把奥尔加的前途安顿好。’我为这才出来——只为这一件事。我这一辈子都在为你操劳。”

“母亲说——”姑娘开口道。

“呸！”老约翰·弗拉克几乎是把字啐出来的。“一个全无想象力的庸俗人，一副管家婆的灵魂！她把你照看得好？很好，那对她只有更好。她若怠慢了你，我绝不会饶她。还有达弗？他一直恭恭敬敬？你要的钱，他都如数给了？”

“是的，父亲。”

玛格丽特觉得，她听出姑娘的嗓音里带着一丝哽咽。

===Original===
“Daver is a good servant. I will make his fortune. The scum of the gutter﻿—but faithful. I told him to be your watchdog. I am pleased with him. Be patient a little while longer. I am going to see all my dreams come true.”

The voice of the madman was tender, so transfigured by love and pride that it seemed to be a different man who was speaking. Then his voice changed again.

“The Colonel will be back tonight. He is a trustworthy man﻿—Gregory also. They shall be paid like ambassadors. You must bear with me a little while longer, Olga. All these unpleasant matters will be cleared up. Reeder we shall dispose of. Tomorrow at high tide we leave﻿ ﻿…”

The sound of the voices receded until they became an indistinguishable murmur. Brill looked round at the girl and smiled again.

“Can you beat him?” he whispered admiringly. “Crazy as a barn coot! But he has the cleverest brain in London﻿—even Reeder says that. God! I’d give ten years’ salary and all my chance of promotion for a gun!”

===Chinese===
“达弗是个好仆人。我会让他发财的。阴沟里的渣滓——可忠心。我吩咐他做你的看门狗。我很满意他。再耐心稍待些时日。我将亲眼看着我全部的梦想一一成真。”

疯子的声音是温柔的，那份爱与骄傲几乎把它整个照亮，仿佛说话的已是另外一个人。随后他的语调又是一变。

“上校今夜回来。他是个可靠的人——格雷戈里也是。给他们的报酬要给得像大使一般。你再容我些时日，奥尔加。这些不痛快的事情都会料理干净。里德嘛，我们把他除掉。明天涨潮时分，我们就动身……”

说话声渐渐远去，终于成了分辨不清的絮语。布里尔扭头看看姑娘，又笑了笑。

“你能不服吗？”他赞叹地悄声说，“疯得像谷仓里的呆鸟！可他长着全伦敦最聪明的头脑——连里德都这么说。天哪！为了换一支枪，我情愿搭上十年的薪水，外加我全部升迁的机会！”

===Original===
“What shall we do?” she asked after a long silence.

“Stay here till the tide turns, then we’ll have to take our chance in the cave. We’d be smashed to pieces if we waited on the beach.”

“There’s no way up the cliff?”

He shook his head.

“There’s a way out through the cave if we can only find it,” he said. “One way? A dozen! I tell you that this cliff is like a honeycomb. One of these days it will collapse like froth on a glass of beer! I heard Daver say so, and the mad fellow agreed. Mad? I wish I had his brain! He’s going to dispose of Reeder, is he? The cemeteries are full of people who’ve tried to dispose of Reeder!”

===Chinese===
“我们该怎么办？”好一阵沉默之后，她问。

“在这儿待到潮水回头，然后只好进洞去碰碰运气。要是待在滩上，我们会给砸得粉碎。”

“崖上没有路吗？”

他摇了摇头。

“只要找得到，穿过洞去就有一条出路，”他说，“一条路？一打也不止！我告诉你，这崖壁活像一只蜂窝。早晚有一天，它会像一杯啤酒面上的泡沫那样塌下去！这话我是听达弗说的，那疯家伙也点头称是。疯？我巴不得有他那样的脑子！他要除掉里德，是么？墓园里可躺满了想除掉里德的人！”

## XVII / 十七

===Original===
It seemed an eternity before the tide turned and began slowly to make its noisy way up the beach. Most of the time Margaret was alone in the little recess, for Brill made periodical reconnaissances into the mouth of the cave. She would have accompanied him, but he explained the difficulties she would find.

“It is quite dark until the tide comes in, and then we get the reflected light from the water and you can see your way about quite easily.”

“Is there anybody there?”

He nodded.

“Two chaps who are tinkering about with a boat. She’s high and dry at present on the bed of the channel, but she floats out quite easily.”

===Chinese===
仿佛隔了无穷无尽的时间，潮水才终于回头，开始喧闹地、缓缓地一路漫上滩头。多数时候，玛格丽特独自待在那小小的凹壁里，因为布里尔要定期潜往洞口一带侦察。她本想陪他同去，可他向她说明了她会碰到的种种难处。

“没涨潮之前里头一片漆黑，等潮水进来，水面会反光，你就能毫不费力地看清脚下的路。”

“那儿有人吗？”

他点了点头。

“有两个家伙，正摆弄着一条船。这会儿它还高高干搁在水道的底上，不过等水一来，很容易就能浮出去。”

===Original===
The first whirl of water was around them when he came out from the cave and beckoned her.

“Keep close to the wall,” he whispered, “and hold fast to my sleeve.”

She obeyed and followed him, and they slipped round to the left, following a fairly level path. Before they had come into the cave, he had warned her that under no circumstances must she speak, not even whisper, except through hollowed hands placed against his ear. The acoustics of the cave were such that the slightest sound was magnified.

They went a long way to the left, and she thought that they were following a passage; it was not until later that she discovered the huge dimensions of this water-hollowed cavern. After a while, he reached back and touched her right hand, as a signal that he was turning to the right.

Whilst they were waiting on the beach, he had drawn a rough plan in the sand, and assured her that the ledge on which they now walked offered no obstacle. He pressed her hand to warn her he was stopping, and, bending down, he groped at the rocky wall where he had left his shoes. Up and up they went; she began to see dimly now, though the cave remained in darkness and she was unable with any accuracy to pick out distant objects. His arm came back and she found herself guided into a deep niche, and he patted her shoulder to tell her she could sit down.

===Chinese===
他从洞里出来向她招手时，第一股打着旋的潮水已经漫到他们脚边。

“紧贴着石壁走，”他悄声说，“牢牢抓住我的袖子。”

她依言跟着他，两人悄悄绕向左边，走上一条相当平整的小径。进洞之前，他就告诫过她，无论如何不可出声，连咬耳朵低语也不行，除非把双手拢成筒状贴着他的耳朵说话。这洞里的传声效果如此，连最轻微的声响也会被放大。

他们朝左走了老远，她还以为是在沿一条通道前进；直到后来她才知道，这座被水掏空的巨洞竟何等宏大。走了一阵，他反手碰了碰她的右手，示意自己要向右转了。

在滩上等候的时候，他已在沙地上画过一幅粗略的图样，并向她保证，他们此刻走着的这道岩台全无阻碍。他捏了捏她的手，示意要停下来，随即俯下身，在岩壁上摸索他先前存放鞋子的地方。他们愈走愈高；这时她已能朦胧视物，虽然洞里依旧一片昏暗，远处的东西她仍辨不真切。他的手臂伸了回来，她便被引进一处深深的凹龛，他拍拍她的肩，告诉她可以坐下了。

===Original===
They had to wait another hour before a thin sheet of water showed at the mouth of the cave, and then, as if by magic, the interior was illuminated by a ghostly green light. The height of it was impossible to tell from where she sat, because just above them was a low and jagged roof. The farther side of the cave was distant some fifty yards, and here the rocky wall seemed to run straight down from the roof to the sandy bottom. It was under this that she saw the motor boat, a long gray craft, entirely devoid of any superstructure. It lay heeled over on its side, and she saw a figure walk along the canted deck and disappear down a hatchway. The farther the water came into the cave, the brighter grew the light. He circled his two hands about her ear and whispered:

“Shall we stay here or try to find a way out?” and she replied in like fashion:

“Let us try.”

===Chinese===
他们又等了一个钟头，才见洞口泛起一层薄薄的水，随即仿佛有魔法一般，洞内被一种鬼魅似的绿光照亮。从她坐的地方无从判断洞有多高，因为他们头顶正上方便是一道低矮参差的洞顶。洞的另一侧离他们约有五十码远，那里的岩壁仿佛从洞顶笔直插到沙底。她看见的汽油艇便停在这一侧之下——一条狭长的灰色船身，浑然不见半点上层建筑。它侧倾着卧在那里，她看见一个人影顺着倾斜的甲板走去，从一处舱口钻了下去。水愈往洞里漫，光便愈亮。他把两只手围拢在她耳边，悄声道：

“我们是待在这儿，还是想法子找条出路？”她以同样的方式答道：

“试试看吧。”

===Original===
He nodded, and silently led the way. It was no longer necessary for her to hold on to him. The path they were following had undoubtedly been shaped by human hands. Every dozen yards was a rough-hewn block of stone put across the path step fashion. They were ascending, and now had the advantage of being screened by the cave from people on the boat, for on their right rose a jagged screen of rock.

They had not progressed a hundred yards before screen and wall joined, and beyond this point progress seemed impossible. The passage was in darkness. Apparently Brill had explored the way, for, taking the girl by the arm, he moved to the right, feeling along the uneven wall. The path beneath was more difficult, and the rocky floor made walking a pain. She was near to exhaustion when she saw, ahead of her, an irregular patch of gray light. Apparently this curious gallery led back to the far end of the cave, but before they reached the opening, Brill signalled her to halt.

“You’d better sit down,” he whispered. “We can put on our shoes.”

The stockings that she had knotted about her waist were still wet, and her shoes two soggy masses, but she was glad to have some protection for her feet. Whilst she was putting them on, Brill crept forward to the opening and took observation.

===Chinese===
他点了点头，默默在前引路。她已用不着再抓着他了。他们走着的这条小径，无疑经过人手的修凿。每隔十来码，便有一块粗凿的石块横置路上，权作台阶。他们在往上走，此刻又得了一重便利：洞体替他们挡住了船上人的视线，因为右侧正立着一道犬牙交错的石屏。

他们还没走满一百码，石屏与洞壁便合拢为一，过了这一点，似乎便再无路可走。通道里一片漆黑。布里尔显然探过这条路，因为他挽起姑娘的手臂，向右边移动，一路摸索着高低不平的洞壁。脚下的路愈发难走，嶙峋的石底硌得人举步维艰。她已濒于力竭，忽见前方现出一块轮廓不规则的灰光。这条古怪的廊道显然又通回洞的尽头，但还没走到那开口处，布里尔便示意她止步。

“你最好坐下，”他悄声说，“咱们可以把鞋穿上了。”

她打结系在腰间的长袜仍是湿的，两只鞋也是湿塌塌的两团，但能给双脚多少添些保护，她已觉得庆幸。趁她穿鞋的当儿，布里尔爬到开口处观望了一番。

===Original===
The water which had now flooded the cave was some fifty feet below him, and a few paces would bring them to a broad ledge of rock which formed a natural landing for a flight of steps leading down from the misty darkness of the roof to water level. The steps were cut in the side of the bare rock; they were about two feet in breadth and were unprotected even by a makeshift handrail. It would be, he saw, a nerve-racking business for the girl to attempt the climb, and he was not even sure that it would be worth the attempt. That they led to one of the many exits from the cave, he knew, because he had seen people climbing up and down those steps and disappearing in the darkness at the top. Possibly the stairs broadened nearer the roof, but even so it was a very severe test for a half-starved girl who he guessed was on the verge of hysteria; he was not quite certain that he himself would not be attacked by vertigo if he made the attempt.

There was a space behind the steps that brought him to the edge of the rock, part of the floor of the cave, and it was this way that he intended to guide Margaret. There was no sound; far away to his right the men on the launch were apparently absorbed in their work. Returning, he told the girl his plan, and she accompanied him to the foot of the steps. At the sight of that terrifying stairway, she shuddered.

“I couldn’t possibly climb those,” she whispered as he pointed upward into the gloom.

“I have an idea there is a sort of balcony running the width of the cave, and it was from there I was thrown,” he said. “I have reason to know that there is a fairly deep pool at the foot of it. When the tide is up, the water reaches the back wall﻿—that is where I found myself when I came to my senses.”

===Chinese===
此刻漫进洞里的水已在他脚下约五十英尺深的地方，再走几步，便是一道宽阔的岩台，天然充当一段石级的落脚台，那石级自洞顶雾蒙蒙的幽暗处直落水面。石级凿在光秃秃的岩壁上，宽约两英尺，连一条将就的扶手也没有。他看得分明，叫姑娘去攀这一趟，是桩把神经绷到断裂的营生，况且他甚至拿不准这一趟是否值得。这些石级通到此洞众多出口之一，这他是知道的，因为他亲眼见过有人在那石级上攀上攀下，隐没在顶端的黑暗里。也许临近洞顶处石级宽了些，但即便如此，对一个饿得半死、他猜想已濒临歇斯底里的姑娘而言，这依旧是极严酷的考验；他自己若去攀，会不会眩晕发作，他也没有十分把握。

石级背后有一片空档，能把他引到岩石的边沿——那也是洞底的一部分——他打算领玛格丽特走的正是这条路。四下无声；他右侧远处，艇上的人显然正埋头干着活。他折回来，把计划告诉姑娘，她便随他走到石级脚下。一望见那可怖的梯道，她便打了个寒噤。

“那石级我可爬不了，”他朝上方的幽暗处指了指，她悄声说。

“我印象里，沿洞的宽度有一道类似阳台的东西，我就是从那儿被扔下来的，”他说，“我有理由知道，阳台底下有一泓相当深的水潭。涨潮的时候，水一直漫到后壁——我恢复知觉的时候，人就在那儿。”

===Original===
“Is there any other way from the cave?” she asked.

He shook his head.

“I’m blessed if I know. I’ve only had a very hasty look round, but there seems to be a sort of tunnel at the far end. It’s worth while exploring﻿—nobody is about, and we are too far from the boat for them to see us.”

They waited for a while, listening, and then, Brill walking ahead, they passed the foot of the stairs and followed a stony path which, to the girl’s relief, broadened as they progressed.

Margaret Belman never forgot that nightmare walk, with the towering rock face on her left, the straight drop to the floor of the cave on her right hand.

===Chinese===
“这洞还有别的出路吗？”她问。

他摇了摇头。

“我要是知道才怪。我只是草草环顾了一遭，不过尽头那边似乎有一条隧道模样的通道。值得去探一探——四下无人，咱们离船又远，他们瞧不见咱们。”

他们静听了一阵，随后由布里尔在前引路，走过石级的脚下，沿一条石头小径前行——叫姑娘宽心的是，这小径愈往前愈发宽阔。

玛格丽特·贝尔曼毕生难忘那场噩梦般的行走：左侧是巍然高耸的岩壁，右手边是笔直垂落洞底的陡崖。

===Original===
They had now reached the limit of the rocky chamber, and found themselves confronted with the choice of four openings. There was one immediately facing them; another﻿—and this was also accessible﻿—about forty feet to the right; and two others which apparently could not be reached.

Leaving Margaret, Brill groped his way into the nearest. He was gone half an hour before he returned with a story of failure.

“The whole cliff is absolutely bored with rock passages,” he said. “I gave it up because it is impossible to go far without a light.”

The second opening promised better. The floor was even and it had this advantage, that it ran straight in line with the mouth of the cave, and there was light for a considerable distance. She followed him along this passage.

“It is worth trying,” he said, and she nodded her agreement.

===Chinese===
这时他们已到达石洞的尽头，面前摆着四个洞口可供抉择。一个正对着他们；另一个——这一个也上得去——在右边约四十英尺处；还有两个，显然是够不着的。

布里尔丢下玛格丽特，摸索着进了最近的一个。他去了半个钟头才回来，带回的是一无所获的消息。

“整座崖壁简直叫岩石通道钻得千疮百孔，”他说，“我半途放弃了，因为没有灯根本走不远。”

第二个洞口看来有希望些。洞底平整，又有一样好处：它与洞口笔直连成一线，相当一段路程之内都有光亮。她跟着他走上了这条通道。

“值得一试，”他说。她点头表示赞同。

===Original===
They had not gone far before he discovered something which he had overlooked on his first trip. At regular intervals there were niches in the wall. He had noticed these but had failed to observe their extraordinary regularity. The majority were blocked with loose stone, but he found one that had not been so guarded and felt his way round the wall. It was a square, cell-like chamber, so exactly proportioned that it must have been created by the hand of man. He came back to announce his intention of exploring the next of the closed cells.

“These walls haven’t been built up for nothing,” he told her, and there was a note of suppressed excitement in his voice.

The farther they progressed, the poorer and more inadequate was the light. They had to feel their way along the wall until the next recess was reached. Flat slabs of rock, laid one on the other, had been piled up in the entrance, and the work of removing the top layers was a painful one. Margaret could not help him. She sat with her back to the wall and fell into the uneasy sleep of exhaustion. She had almost ceased to be hungry, though her throat was parched with a maddening thirst. She woke heavily and found Brill shaking her shoulder.

“I’ve been inside.” His voice was quavering with excitement. “Hold out your hands, both together!”

===Chinese===
他们没走出多远，他便发现了一样头一回进来时看漏了的东西。洞壁上按一律的间距开着一个个壁龛。这些他先前是见到了的，却没看出它们那异乎寻常的规整。大多数壁龛都用碎石封死，但他寻到一个不曾封死的，便沿洞壁摸索进去。那是一间四四方方、囚室似的石屋，比例端正得分毫不差，必定出自人手。他回来宣布，打算去探下一间封闭的龛室。

“这些墙封起来绝非无缘无故，”他对她说，嗓音里透着一丝按捺的兴奋。

他们愈往前走，光便愈弱愈不济事。到下一个壁龛之前，他们只得扶着洞壁一路摸索。入口处堆叠着一块压一块的扁平石板，搬去顶上几层的活计苦不堪言。玛格丽特帮不上忙。她背靠洞壁坐下，坠入筋疲力尽带来的不安的睡眠。她几乎已不觉得饿，喉咙却干渴得叫人发狂。她沉沉地醒来，发觉布里尔正在摇她的肩膀。

“我进去过了。”他的声音激动得发颤。“把两只手一齐伸出来！”

===Original===
She obeyed mechanically and felt something cold drop into her palm, and, drooping her head, drank. The sting of wine took her breath away.

“Champagne,” he whispered. “Don’t drink too much or you’ll get tight!”

She sipped again. Never had wine tasted so delicious.

“It’s a storehouse; boxes of food, I think, and hundreds of bottles of wine. Hold your hand.”

He poured out another portion of wine; most of it escaped through her fingers, but she drank eagerly the few drops that remained.

===Chinese===
她机械地照办，觉出一样冰凉的东西落进掌心，便低下头去喝。酒的辛辣呛得她一时透不过气来。

“香槟，”他悄声说，“别喝得太多，不然你要醉的！”

她又呷了一口。从来没有哪一口酒尝来这样甘美。

“是个仓库；我看是整箱的吃食，还有几百瓶酒。把手伸过来。”

他又斟了一份酒；大半从她指缝间流走了，余下的几滴她也急切地饮下了。

===Original===
“Wait here.”

She was very much awake now; she peered into the darkness toward the place where he had disappeared. Ten minutes, a quarter of an hour passed, and then, to her joy, there appeared from behind the stony barrier, revealing in silhouette the hole through which Brill had crawled, a white and steady light. She heard the creak and crash of a box being opened, saw the bulk of the detective as he appeared in the hole, and in a second he was by her side.

“Biscuits,” he said. “Luckily the box was labelled.”

“What was the light?” she asked, as she seized the crackers eagerly.

“A small battery lantern; I knocked it over as I was groping. The place is simply stocked with grub! Here’s a drink for you.”

===Chinese===
“在这儿等着。”

她此刻已全然清醒；她朝他消失的那处黑暗张望。十分钟过去了，一刻钟也过去了，随后，令她欣喜的是，一道白亮而稳定的光从石垒后面透出来，映出布里尔爬进去的那个洞孔的轮廓。她听见开箱的吱嘎声与磕撞声，看见那侦探魁伟的身影出现在洞孔处，一转眼他已到了她身旁。

“饼干，”他说，“幸亏箱子上贴着标签。”

“那灯是什么？”她一边急切地抓起饼干，一边问。

“一盏小的电池灯；我摸索的时候把它碰倒了。那地方的吃食简直堆得满满的！这儿有你喝的。”

===Original===
He handed her a flat, round tin, guided her finger to the hole he had punched.

“Preserved milk﻿—German, and good stuff,” he said.

She drank thirstily, not taking her lips from the tin until it was empty.

“This seems to be the ship’s store,” he said, “but the great blessing is the lamp. I’m going in to see if I can find a box of refills; there isn’t a great deal of juice left in the battery.”

His search occupied a considerable time, and then she saw the light go out and her heart sank, until it flashed up again, this time more brilliant than ever. He scrambled out and dropped down the rugged wall and pushed something heavy into her hand.

===Chinese===
他递给她一只扁圆的铁罐，引着她的手指找到他戳开的孔。

“炼乳——德国货，上等东西。”他说。

她渴极了，一口一口喝着，嘴唇不离铁罐，直到喝得涓滴不剩。

“这儿像是船上的储藏室，”他说，“可最大的恩赐是那盏灯。我进去瞧瞧能不能找到一盒备用电池；电池里剩的电不多了。”

他寻摸了好半天，随后她看见灯光熄灭，心往下一沉，直到它又倏地亮起，这一回比先前更亮。他手脚并用地爬出来，顺着嶙峋的石壁落下，把一样沉甸甸的东西塞进她手里。

===Original===
“A spare lamp,” he said. “There are half a dozen there and enough refills to last us a month.”

He struck the stone wall with something that clanged.

“A case opener,” he explained, “and a useful weapon. I wonder which of these storehouses holds the guns?”

The exploration of the passage could now be made in comparative comfort. There was need of the lamps, for a few yards farther on the tunnel turned abruptly to the right and the floor became more irregular. Brill turned on his light and showed the way. Now the passage turned to the left, and he pointed out how smooth were the walls.

“Water action,” he said. “There must have been a subterranean river here at one time.”

===Chinese===
“一盏备用灯，”他说，“那儿还有半打，备用的电池够咱们用上一个月。”

他用一样东西敲了敲石壁，敲得叮当作响。

“一根开箱撬棍，”他解释道，“也是件得手的兵器。不知道这些仓库里哪一间藏的是枪？”

这下探起通道来便舒服多了。灯是少不得的，因为再往前几码，隧道陡然向右一折，洞底也愈发坎坷。布里尔拧亮自己的灯，在前引路。这当儿通道又转向左边，他指给她看，洞壁是何等光滑。

“水的功夫，”他说，“这里从前想必有过一条地下河。”

===Original===
Twisting and turning, the gallery led now up, now down, now taking almost a hairpin turn, now sweeping round in an almost perfect curve, but leading apparently nowhere.

Brill was walking ahead, the beam of his lamp sweeping along the ground, when she saw him stop suddenly. Stooping, he picked something from the ground.

“How the dickens did this get here?”

On the palm of his hand lay a bright silver florin, a little battered at the edge, but unmistakably a two-shilling piece.

“Somebody has been here﻿—” he began and then she uttered a cry.

===Chinese===
这条廊道曲折回环，忽而上，忽而下，时而几乎折出一个发夹弯，时而掠出一条近乎完美的弧线，可看来到头来哪儿也不通。

布里尔走在前面，灯光的光束扫着地面，忽然她见他猛地站住了。他俯下身，从地上拾起一样东西。

“这鬼东西怎么会到这儿来的？”

他掌心里躺着一枚亮闪闪的银弗罗林，边缘磕碰得有些残损，可错不了，是枚两先令的硬币。

“有人到过这儿——”他刚开口，她就叫出了声。

===Original===
“Oh!” gasped Margaret. “That was Mr. Reeder’s!”

She told him of the incident at the well; how J. G. Reeder had dropped the coin to test the distance. Brill put the light of his lamp on the ceiling; it was solid rock. And then he sent the rays moving along, and presently the lamp focused on a large round opening.

“Here is the well that never was a well,” he said grimly and, flashing the light upward, looked open-mouthed at the steel rungs fitted every few inches in the side of the well.

“A ladder,” he said slowly. “What do you know about that?”

He reached up, standing on tiptoe, but the nearest rung was at least a yard beyond his hand, and he looked round for some loose stones which he could pile up and from the top of which he could reach the lowest bar of the ladder. But none was in sight, except a few splinters of stone which were valueless for his purpose. And then he remembered the case-opener; it had a hook at the end. Holding this above his head, he leapt. The first time he missed; the second time the hook caught the steel rung and the handle slipped from his grip, leaving the case-opener dangling. He rubbed his hands on the dusty floor and sprang again. This time he caught and held, and with a superhuman effort pulled himself up until his hand gripped the lower rung. Another struggle, and he had drawn himself up hand over hand till his feet rested on the bar.

===Chinese===
“哦！”玛格丽特倒吸一口气，“那是里德先生的！”

她把井边那桩事讲给他听；J. G. 里德怎样丢下那枚硬币去试探深浅。布里尔把灯光打在顶上；是整块的岩石。随后他让光束缓缓移过去，不多时，灯光便聚在一个圆圆的大洞口上。

“这就是那口不是井的井，”他冷冷地说，随即将灯光向上扫去，张大了嘴，呆望着井壁上每隔几英寸便嵌着一根的钢横档。

“一架梯子，”他慢慢说道，“你说稀奇不稀奇？”

他踮起脚尖向上够，可最近的一根横档离他的手至少还差一码。他环顾四周，想寻些散石垒起来，好站在上面够着梯子最底下的一档。可四下里一块也寻不见，只有几片碎石，派不上用场。这时他想起那根开箱撬棍；它末端带着钩子。他把撬棍举过头顶，纵身一跃。头一回落了空；第二回，钩子挂住了钢横档，棍柄却从掌中滑脱，开箱撬棍悬空晃荡起来。他在积尘的地上擦了擦双手，又跃起来。这一回他抓住了，抓牢了，凭着超人的力气把自己一点点拽上去，直到一只手攥住下面那根横档。又一番挣扎，他两手交替节节攀升，直到双脚稳稳站上了横档。

===Original===
“Do you think if I pulled you up you have strength to climb?” he asked.

She shook her head.

“I’m afraid not. Go up alone; I will wait here.”

“Keep clear of the bottom,” he warned her. “I may not fall, but as likely as not I shall dislodge a few chunks of rock in my progress.”

The warning was well justified, she found. There was a continuous shower of stone and earth as he progressed. From time to time, he stopped to rest. Once he shouted down something which she could not distinguish. It was probably a warning, for a few seconds later a mass of rock as large as a man’s head crashed down and smashed on the floor, sending fragments flying in all directions.

===Chinese===
“要是我把你拽上去，你看你还有力气攀吗？”他问。

她摇了摇头。

“怕是不行。你一个人上去吧；我在这儿等。”

“离井底远着些，”他告诫她，“我兴许摔不下来，可一路攀爬，十有八九要碰落几块岩石。”

她发觉这告诫半点不虚。他一路攀爬，石块泥土如阵雨般落个不停。他不时停下来歇息。有一回他朝下喊了句什么，她听不真切。多半是句警告，因为几秒钟后，一大块人头大小的岩石轰然砸下，在井底摔得粉碎，碎片四下横飞。

===Original===
Peeping up from time to time, she could see the glimmer of his lamp growing fainter; and now, left alone, she began to grow nervous, and for company switched on her light. She had hardly done so when she heard a sound which brought her heart to her mouth. It was the sound of footsteps; somebody was walking along the passage toward her.

She turned the switch of the lamp and listened. The old man’s voice! Only his, and none other. He was talking to himself, a babble of growling sound that was becoming more and more distinct. And then, far away, she saw the glow of a reflected light, for the passage swept around at this point and he would not be visible until he was upon her.

Slipping off her shoes, she sped along in the darkness, tumbling and sliding on the uneven pathway. After a while panic left her and she stopped and looked back. The light was no longer visible; there was neither sound nor sign of him; and, plucking up courage, after a few minutes she retraced her steps. She dared not put on the light, and must guess where the well opening was. In the darkness she passed it and she was soon a considerable distance beyond the place where Brill had left her.

Where had Flack gone? There were no side passages. She was standing by one of the recesses, her hand resting on the improvised stone screen, when to her horror she felt it moving away from her, and had just time to shrink back when she saw a crack of light appear on the opposite wall and broaden until there was outlined the shape of a doorway.

===Chinese===
她不时向上窥望，看得见他灯光的微芒愈来愈淡；这时只剩她孤身一人，她渐渐心慌起来，便拧亮了灯给自己作伴。灯刚点亮，她便听见一个声音，唬得心都提到了嗓子眼。那是脚步声；有人正沿着通道朝她走来。

她关了灯，侧耳细听。是那老头的声音！只可能是他，绝非旁人。他在自言自语，一串咆哮似的咕哝，愈来愈清晰。随后，远远地，她看见一片反照的光晕，因为通道在此拐了个弯，不等那人逼到她跟前，她根本望不见他的身影。

她甩掉鞋子，在黑暗中一路疾奔，在高高低低的小径上跌跌撞撞、连滚带滑。过了一阵，恐慌退去了，她停下脚步回头张望。那光已再看不见；他声息全无，踪影俱灭；她壮起胆子，几分钟后循原路折回。她不敢开灯，只好揣度井口的位置。黑暗中她走过了头，不多时便已远远越过布里尔丢下她的地方。

弗拉克上哪儿去了？这里没有岔道。她正站在一个壁龛旁，手搭在那道权充遮掩的石屏上，忽然惊恐地觉出它正从她手边移开；她堪堪来得及缩身后退，便见对面壁上裂开一线光，渐渐展宽，终于勾出一道门框的轮廓。

===Original===
“… Tonight, my dear, tonight﻿ ﻿… I’m going up to see Daver. Daver is worrying me.﻿ ﻿… You are sure nothing has happened that might shake my confidence in him?”

“Nothing, Father. What could have happened?”

It was Olga Crewe’s voice. She said something else which Margaret could not hear, and then she heard the chuckling laugh of the old man.

“Reeder? He’s busy in London! But he’ll be back tonight.﻿ ﻿…”

Again a question which Margaret could not catch.

===Chinese===
“……今夜，亲爱的，今夜……我要上去见达弗。达弗让我放心不下。……你能肯定，没有出过什么足以动摇我对他的信任的事？”

“没有，父亲。能出什么事呢？”

是奥尔加·克鲁的声音。她又说了句什么，玛格丽特听不见，随后她听见老头咯咯的笑声。

“里德？他在伦敦忙着呢！可他今夜就回来了。……”

又是一句玛格丽特听不真切的问话。

===Original===
“The body hasn’t been found. I didn’t want to hurt the girl, but she was useful﻿ ﻿… my best card.﻿ ﻿… I could have caught Reeder with her﻿—had it all arranged.”

Another question.

“I suppose so. The tide is very high. Anyway, I saw her fall.﻿ ﻿…”

Margaret knew they were talking about her, but this interested her less than the possibility of discovery. She walked backward, step by step, hoping and praying that she would find a niche into which she could shrink. Presently she found what she wanted.

===Chinese===
“尸体还没有被发现。我本不想伤害那姑娘，可她大有用处……是我手里最好的一张牌。……我本可以用她把里德擒住——一切都已安排停当了。”

又是一句问话。

“我想是吧。潮水涨得很高。反正，我亲眼看见她坠落了。……”

玛格丽特知道他们说的是她，可比起被发觉的危险，这已算不得什么。她一步一步向后退，一面盼着、一面祷告，但愿寻得一个容她蜷身的凹龛。不多时，她如愿以偿了。

===Original===
Flack had come out into the passage and was standing talking back into the room.

“All right, I’ll leave the door open.﻿ ﻿… Imagination. There’s plenty of air. The well supplies that. I’ll be back this evening.”

She dared not look, but after a while his footsteps became fainter. The door was still open, and she saw a shadow growing larger on the opposite wall as Olga approached the entrance. Presently she heard a sigh; the shadow became small again and finally disappeared. Margaret crept forward, hardly daring to breathe, until she came up behind the open door.

It was, she guessed, made of stout oak, and the surface had been so cunningly camouflaged with splinters of rock that it differed in no respect from the walled recess into which Brill had broken.

===Chinese===
弗拉克已出来走进通道，正站在那儿朝屋里说话。

“好吧，门我就敞着不关。……那是瞎操心。空气多得很。井自会供给。今儿晚上我就回来。”

她不敢张望，但过了一阵，他的脚步声愈去愈微。门还敞着，她看见一条影子在对面壁上渐渐放大——奥尔加走近了门口。随即她听见一声叹息；那影子又缩小了，终于消失。玛格丽特向前爬去，几乎不敢喘气，一直挪到那扇敞开的门背后。

她猜这门是用坚牢的橡木做的，门面上用碎石片伪装得这般巧妙，同布里尔破开的那类封砌的壁龛相比，竟无一处分别。

===Original===
Curiosity is dominant in the most rational of individuals, and, despite her terrible danger, Margaret was curious to see the inside of that rocky home of the Flacks. With the utmost caution she peeped round. She was surprised at the size of the room and a little disappointed in its furnishing. She had pictured rich rugs and gorgeous furniture, the walls perhaps covered with silken hangings. Instead, she saw a plain deal table on which stood a lamp, a strip of threadbare carpet, two basket chairs and a camp bed. Olga was standing by the table, looking down at a newspaper; her back was toward the girl, and Margaret had time to make a more prolonged scrutiny.

Near the table were three or four suitcases, packed and strapped as though in preparation for a journey. A fur coat lay across the bed, and that was the only evidence of luxury in this grim apartment. There was a second person in the room. Margaret distinguished in the shadow the drooping figure of a woman﻿—Mrs. Burton.

She took a step forward to see better, her feet slipped upon the smooth surface of the rock and she fell forward against the door, half closing it.

===Chinese===
便是最理智的人身上，好奇心也占着上风；玛格丽特虽身处可怕的危险之中，仍忍不住想看看弗拉克一家这石头居所的内里。她极其小心地探头窥望。房间之大出她意料，陈设却叫她略感失望。她原先设想的是华贵的地毯、炫目的家具，四壁或许还张着丝绸帷幔。然而她看见的，是一张寻常的松木桌，桌上立着一盏灯，一条磨秃了绒的地毯，两把柳条椅，一张行军床。奥尔加站在桌旁，低头看着一份报纸；她背朝着姑娘，玛格丽特便得了工夫，把她细细端详了一番。

桌边放着三四只手提箱，都已装好捆牢，仿佛整装待发。一件皮大衣横搭在床上，那是这间阴郁的屋子里唯一的奢华痕迹。屋里还有第二个人。玛格丽特在暗影里辨出一个颓然垂首的女人身影——伯顿太太。

她上前一步想看得更清楚，脚在光滑的岩面上一滑，整个人朝门扑去，把门带得半掩上了。

===Original===
“Who is there? Is that you, Father?”

Margaret’s heart nearly stopped beating, and for a moment she stood paralyzed, incapable of movement. Then, as Olga’s footsteps sounded, she turned and fled along the passage, gripping tight her lantern. Olga’s voice challenged her, but on and on she ran. The corridor was growing lighter, and with a gasp of horror she realized that, in the confusion of the moment, she had taken the wrong direction and she was running toward the great cave, possibly into the old madman’s hands.

She heard the quick patter of footsteps behind her and flew on. And now she was in the almost bright light of the huge cavern. There was nobody in sight, and she followed the twisting ledge that ran under the wall of rock until she came to the foot of the long stairs. And then she heard a shout. Somebody on the boat had seen her. As she stood motionless with fear, mad John Flack appeared. He was coming toward her through the passage by which she and Brill had reached the interior of the cave. For a second he stared at her as though she were some ghastly apparition of his mad dreams, and then with a roar he leaped toward her.

===Chinese===
“是谁？是你吗，父亲？”

玛格丽特的心脏几乎停止了跳动，一时僵在原地，动弹不得。随后，奥尔加的脚步声一响，她转身便沿通道奔逃，手里紧紧攥着灯。奥尔加出声喝问，她却只顾没命地跑。廊道愈来愈亮，她惊恐得倒抽一口冷气，这才省悟：方才一阵慌乱里，自己走反了方向——她正向那大洞奔去，说不定正一头撞进那老疯子的手掌。

她听见身后轻快急促的脚步声，愈发飞奔。这时她已跑进巨洞里近乎明亮的光线之中。四下不见人影，她沿着岩壁下蜿蜒的岩台一路奔去，直奔到那道长长石级的脚下。就在这时，她听见一声呼喊。船上有人瞧见她了。正当她吓得木立不动时，疯子约翰·弗拉克现身了。他正顺着她和布里尔进洞的那条通道朝她走来。有一秒钟，他死死盯着她，仿佛她是他癫狂的梦魇里某个可怕的鬼影，随即他大吼一声，朝她猛扑过来。

===Original===
She hesitated no longer. In a second, she was flying up that awful staircase, death on her right hand, but a more hideous fate behind. Higher and higher up those unrailed stairs﻿—she dared not look, she dared not think; she could only keep looking steadfastly upward into the misty gloom where this interminable Jacob’s ladder ended on some solid floor. Not for a fortune would she have looked behind, or vertigo would have seized her. Her breath was coming in long sobs; her heart beat as though it would burst. She dared pause for an infinitesimal time to recover breath before she continued her flight. He was an old man; she could outdistance him. But he was a madman, a thing of terrible and abnormal energy. Panic was leaving her; it exhausted too much of her strength. Upward and upward she climbed, until she was in gloom, and then, when it seemed that she could get no farther, she reached the head of the stairs. A broad, flat space, with a rocky roof which, for some reason, had been straightened with concrete pillars. There were dozens of these pillars﻿ ﻿… once she had taken a fortnight’s holiday in Spain; there was a cathedral in Cordoba of which this broad vault reminded her﻿ ﻿… all sense of direction was lost now. She came with terrifying suddenness to a blank wall; ran along it until she came to a narrow opening where there were five steps and here she stopped to turn on her light. Facing her was a steel door with a great iron handle, and the steel door was ajar.

She pulled it toward her, ran through, pulled the door behind her; it fastened with a click. It had something attached to its inner side, a steel projection﻿—as she shut the door a box fell with a crash. There was yet another door before her, and this was immovable. She was in a tiny white box of a room, three feet wide, little more in depth. She had no time to continue her observations. Someone was fumbling with the handle of the door through which she had come. She gripped in desperation at the iron shelf and felt it slide a little to the right. Though she did not know this, the back part of the shelf acted as a bolt. Again she heard the fumbling at the handle and the click of a key turning, but the steel door remained immovable, and Margaret Belman sank in a heap to the ground.

===Chinese===
她再不迟疑。转眼间她已飞身扑上那道可怖的梯道，右手边就是死亡，身后的命运却更为可怖。沿着那道没有栏杆的石级愈攀愈高——她不敢看，也不敢想；她只能目不转睛地仰望着那雾蒙蒙的幽暗，这道漫无尽头的雅各天梯，尽头便落在某处坚实的地面上。便是拿万贯家财来换，她也不肯回头望上一眼，否则眩晕便会将她攫住。她的呼吸化成一声声长长的抽咽；心跳得仿佛要炸裂。她只敢停留无法再短的一瞬，缓过一口气，便又继续奔逃。他是老人；她能把他甩在身后。可他是个疯子，是一团可怕而反常的精力。恐慌正离她而去；恐慌耗去了她太多气力。她向上攀呀攀呀，直到没入幽暗，随后，在眼看得再也挪不动一步的时候，她到了石级的顶端。一片宽阔平坦的空场，顶着岩石的洞顶——不知出于什么缘故，洞顶用混凝土柱子撑得平直了。这样的柱子足有几十根……她有一回在西班牙度了两星期的假；科尔多瓦有一座大教堂，这片宽阔的穹隆便叫她想起它……如今她已全然失去了方向感。她猝不及防地撞到一堵死墙跟前；沿墙奔去，来到一处窄窄的洞口，口内有五级台阶，她便停步拧亮了灯。面前是一扇钢门，带着一根粗大的铁把手，钢门虚掩着。

她把门朝自己拉过来，冲了进去，又把门在身后带上；咔哒一声，门扣上了。门内侧附着一样东西，一处钢质的凸出物——她一关门，一只箱子便哐啷一声摔落下来。她面前还有一扇门，这一扇却纹丝不动。她置身在一个白匣子似的小房间里，三英尺宽，进深不过略多于此。她已无暇再观察下去。有人正在她进来那扇门的把手上摸索。她绝望地抓紧那道铁搁板，觉出它向右滑了一点点。她并不知道，那搁板的后半截起着门闩的作用。她又听见门把上的摸索声和钥匙转动的咔哒声，可那扇钢门依旧纹丝不动，玛格丽特·贝尔曼瘫作一团，倒在了地上。

## XVIII / 十八

===Original===
J. G. Reeder came downstairs, and those who saw his face realized that it was not the tragedy he had almost witnessed which had made him so white and drawn.

He found Gray in Daver’s office, waiting for his call to London. It came through as Reeder entered the room, and he took the instrument from his subordinate’s hand. He dismissed the death of Daver in a few words, and went on:

“I want all the local policemen we can muster, Simpson, though I think it would be better if we could get soldiers. There’s a garrison town five miles from here; the beaches have to be searched, and I want these caves explored. There is another thing: I think it would be advisable to get a destroyer or something to patrol the waters before Siltbury. I’m pretty sure that Flack has a motor boat﻿—there’s channel deep enough to take it, and apparently there is a cave that stretches right under the cliff.﻿ ﻿… Miss Belman? I don’t know. That is what I want to find out.”

===Chinese===
J. G. 里德走下楼来，见到他脸色的人都明白：使他这样苍白憔悴的，并不是他险些亲眼目睹的那场惨剧。

他在达弗的办公室里找到了格雷，他正等着接通伦敦的电话。里德一进屋，电话恰好接了过来，他从下属手里接过听筒。三言两语交代过达弗之死，他便接着说道：

“辛普森，我要我们能调集到的全部当地警员，不过我想，要是能调来军队就更好。离这儿五英里有一座驻防城镇；海滩必须搜查，这些洞窟我也要人探一遍。还有一件事：我看最好调一艘驱逐舰之类的船，在西尔特伯里外海巡逻。我相当有把握，弗拉克有一条摩托艇——水道深得足够它进出，而且显然有个洞窟一直通到悬崖底下。……贝尔曼小姐？我不知道。那正是我要查明的事。”

===Original===
Simpson told him that the lorry carrying the gold had been seen at Sevenoaks, and it required a real effort on Mr. Reeder’s part to bring his mind to such a triviality.

“I think soldiers will be best. I’d like a strong party posted near the quarry. There’s another cave there where Daver used to keep his wagons. I have an idea you might pick up the money tonight. That,” he added, a little bitterly, “will induce the authorities to use the military!”

===Chinese===
辛普森告诉他，运黄金的那辆卡车已在七橡树（Sevenoaks）被人瞧见；里德先生费了好一番力气，才把心思拉到这样一桩小事上来。

“我想还是军队最好。我要一支人马在采石场附近守着。那边还有一个洞窟，达弗从前在那儿存放货车。我有个预感，你们今晚说不定就能起获那笔钱。那件事，”他略带苦涩地补了一句，“才说动得了当局动用军队！”

===Original===
After the ambulance had come and the pitiable wreck of Daver had been removed, he returned to the man’s suite with a party of masons he had brought up from Siltbury. Throwing open the lid of the divan, he pointed to the stone floor.

“That flag works on a pivot,” he said, “but I think it is fastened with a bolt or a bar underneath. Break it down.”

A quarter of an hour was sufficient to shatter the stone flooring, and then, as he had expected, he found a narrow flight of stairs leading to a square stone room which remained very much as it had been for six hundred years. A dusty, bare apartment which yielded its secret. There was a small open door and a very narrow passage, along which a stout man would walk with some difficulty, and which led to behind the panelling of Daver’s private office. Mr. Reeder realized that anybody concealed here could hear every word that was spoken. And now he understood Daver’s frantic plea that he should lower his voice when he spoke of the marriage. Crazy Jack had learned the secret of his daughter’s degradation. From that moment Daver’s death was inevitable.

===Chinese===
救护车来过、达弗那副可怜的残躯被抬走之后，他带着一队从西尔特伯里请来的石匠，回到那人的套间。他掀开矮榻的盖板，指着石头铺的地面。

“那块石板装在枢轴上，”他说，“不过我想底下还有门闩或铁条扣着。把它砸开。”

一刻钟工夫便足以把石地砸碎；随后，不出他所料，他发现一道窄窄的楼梯，通向一间四四方方的石屋——六百年来，它几乎保持着老样子。一间积尘累累、空空荡荡的屋子，交出了它的秘密。屋里有扇敞开的小门，连着一条极窄的通道，胖人走起来颇要费些力气；通道尽头，正是达弗私人办公室嵌板的背后。里德先生明白了：不管谁藏在这里，人家说的每一个字都逃不过他的耳朵。这时他也懂了，为什么一提起那桩婚事，达弗会那样没命地恳求他压低嗓门。疯子杰克得知了女儿堕落的秘密。从那一刻起，达弗就死定了。

===Original===
How had the madman escaped? That required very little explanation. At some remote period, Larmes Keep had evidently been used as a show place. He found an ancient wooden inscription fixed to the wall, which told the curious that this was the torture chamber of the old Counts of Larme; it added the useful information that the dungeons were immediately beneath and approached through a stone trap. This the detectives found, and Mr. Reeder had his first view of the vaulted dungeons of Larmes Keep.

It was neither an impressive nor a thrilling exploration. All that was obvious was that there were three routes by which the murderer could escape, and that all three ways led back to the house, one exit being between the kitchen and the vestibule.

“There is another way out,” said Reeder shortly, “and we haven’t found it yet.”

===Chinese===
那疯子是怎么逃出去的？这用不着多少解释。在某个久远的年代，拉尔姆宅显然曾被辟作供人游览的名胜。他发现墙上钉着一块古老的木牌，向好奇的来客宣告：这里是历代老拉尔姆伯爵的刑讯室；木牌还附了一条有用的信息——地牢就在正下方，须经一道石头活板门下去。侦探们找到了那道活板门，里德先生这才初睹拉尔姆宅那几间拱顶地牢的真容。

这番探察既不壮观，也不惊心动魄。显而易见的只有一点：凶手有三条路可逃，而三条路全都通回宅子，其中一个出口就在厨房和门厅之间。

“还有一条出路，”里德简短地说，“咱们还没有找到。”

===Original===
His nerves were on edge. He roamed from room to room, turning out boxes, breaking open cupboards, emptying trunks. One find he made: it was the marriage certificate, and it was concealed in the lining of Olga Crewe’s dressing case.

At seven o’clock, the first detachment of troops arrived by motor van. The local police had already reported that they had found no trace of Margaret Belman. They pointed out that the tide was falling when the girl left Larmes Keep, and that, unless she was lying on some invisible ledge, she must have reached the beach in safety. There was a very faint hope that she was alive. How faint J. G. Reeder would not admit.

A local cook had been brought in to prepare dinner for the detective, but Reeder contented himself with a cup of strong coffee﻿—food, he felt, would have choked him.

===Chinese===
他的神经绷到了极点。他一间屋一间屋地转，翻箱子，撬柜子，倒皮箱。他搜出了一件东西：那份结婚证书，就藏在奥尔加·克鲁梳妆箱的衬里内。

七点钟，第一批部队乘卡车赶到。当地警察已经报告，没有发现玛格丽特·贝尔曼的任何踪迹。他们指出，姑娘离开拉尔姆宅时正值退潮，除非她躺在某处看不见的岩架上，否则必定已安然抵达海滩。她还活着的希望非常渺茫。渺茫到什么地步，J. G. 里德不肯承认。

一位当地厨子已被请来给侦探做晚饭，可里德有一杯浓咖啡就够了——他觉得，饭菜是会噎住他的。

===Original===
He had posted a detachment in the quarry and, returning to the house, was sitting in the big hall, pondering the events of the day, when Gray came flying into the room.

“Brill!” he gasped.

J. G. Reeder sprang to his feet with a bound.

“Brill?” he repeated huskily. “Where is Brill?”

There was no need for Gray to point. A dishevelled and grimy figure, supported by a detective, staggered through the doorway.

===Chinese===
他已派一小队人驻守采石场；回到宅子，正坐在大厅里琢磨这一天的事，格雷飞奔着闯进屋来。

“布里尔！”他喘着气喊。

J. G. 里德霍地跳了起来。

“布里尔？”他哑着嗓子追问，“布里尔在哪儿？”

用不着格雷指点。一个蓬头散发、满身污垢的身影，由一名侦探搀着，踉踉跄跄地从门口走了进来。

===Original===
“Where have you come from?” asked Reeder.

The man could not speak for a second. He pointed to the ground, and then, hoarsely:

“From the bottom of the well.﻿ ﻿… Miss Belman is down there now!”

Brill was in a state of collapse, and not until he had had a stiff dose of brandy was he able to articulate a coherent story. Reeder led a party to the shrubbery and the windlass was tested.

===Chinese===
“你从哪儿来的？”里德问。

那人一时说不出话来。他指了指地面，随后哑着嗓子道：

“从井底。……贝尔曼小姐现在就在下面！”

布里尔整个人都垮了，灌下一大口烈性白兰地，才把事情连贯地讲了出来。里德带一队人到灌木林去，绞盘经受了一次检验。

===Original===
“It won’t bear even the weight of a woman, and there’s not sufficient rope,” said Gray, who made the test.

One of the officers remembered that, in searching the kitchen, he had found two window-cleaners’ belts, stout straps with a safety hook attached. He went in search of these while Mr. Reeder stripped his coat and vest.

“There’s a gap of four feet halfway down,” warned Brill. “The stone came away when I put my foot on it, and I nearly fell.”

Reeder, his lamp swung around his neck, peered down into the hole.

===Chinese===
“它连一个女人的分量都吃不住，绳子也不够长。”负责检验的格雷说。

一名警官记起，搜查厨房时见过两条擦窗工的腰带——结实的皮带，上面拴着安全钩。他去寻腰带，里德先生则脱下了外衣和马甲。

“半道上有四英尺宽的一截缺口，”布里尔警告说，“我一踩上去，石头就掉了，我差点儿摔下去。”

里德把灯往脖子上一挂，朝洞里窥望。

===Original===
“It’s strange I didn’t see this ladder when I saw the well before,” he said, and then remembered that he had only opened one half of the hinged trap.

Gray, who was also equipped with a belt, descended first, as he was the lighter of the two. By this time half a company of soldiers were on the scene, and by the greatest of good fortune the unit that had been turned out to assist the police was a company of the Royal Engineers. While one party went in search of ropes, the other began to extemporize a hauling gear.

The two men worked their way down without a word. The lamps were fairly useless, for they could not show them the next rung, and after a while they began to move more cautiously. Gray found the gap and called a halt while he bridged it. The next rung was none too secure, Mr. Reader thought, as he lowered his weight upon it, but they passed the danger zone with no other mishap than that which was caused by big pebbles dropping on Reeder’s head.

===Chinese===
“真奇怪，我上回看这口井的时候，竟没瞧见这架梯子。”他说，随即想起，那道双扇活板门，他先前只掀开过一半。

格雷也系上了一条腰带，两人中他身量较轻，便先下去。这时，半连士兵已经到场，而最走运的是，奉命出动协助警察的部队，恰是皇家工兵（Royal Engineers）的一个连。一队人去找绳子，另一队动手临时赶造一副吊拽装置。

两人一声不吭地往下挪。灯几乎派不上用场，照不出下一级横档在哪儿；过了一阵，他们的动作越发谨慎。格雷找到那截缺口，喊停了下行，先把它架接起来。把身子沉到再下一级横档上时，里德先生觉得它也说不上牢靠；不过他们穿过险境，除大卵石一颗颗砸在里德头上之外，倒没出别的乱子。

===Original===
It seemed as though they would never reach the bottom, and the strain was already telling upon the older man when Gray whispered:

“This is the bottom, I think,” and sent the light of his lamp downward. Immediately afterward, he dropped to the rocky floor of the passage, Mr. Reeder following.

“Margaret!” he called in a whisper.

There was no reply. He threw the light first one way and then the other, but Margaret was not in sight, and his heart sank.

===Chinese===
看样子他们永远也到不了井底；那份紧张已渐渐磨上年纪较长的这一位了，这时格雷低声道：

“我想是到底了。”说着，他把灯光朝下照去。紧接着，他落到通道的石地上，里德先生随之落下。

“玛格丽特！”他低声呼唤。

没有人应。他把灯光先照向一头，又照向另一头，可哪里也看不见玛格丽特，他的心沉了下去。

===Original===
“You go farther along the passage,” he whispered to Gray. “I’ll take the other direction.”

With the light of his lamp on the ground, he half walked, half ran along the twisting gallery. Ahead of him he heard the sound of a movement not easily identified, and he stopped to extinguish the light. Moving cautiously forward, he turned an angle of the passage and saw at the far end indication of light. Sitting down, he looked along, and after a while he thought he saw a figure moving against this artificial skyline. Mr. Reeder crept forward, and this time he was not relying upon a rubber truncheon. He thumbed down the safety catch of his Browning and drew nearer and nearer to the figure. Most unexpectedly it spoke.

“Olga, where has your father gone?”

===Chinese===
“你顺着通道往远里走，”他低声吩咐格雷，“我走另一头。”

他把灯照向地面，半走半跑地沿着弯曲的岩廊行进。前方传来一阵难以辨明的动静，他便停下，熄了灯。他小心翼翼地向前挪，转过通道的一个拐角，看见尽头远处透出一线亮光。他坐下来顺着通道望过去，过了一会儿，他觉得自己看见一个人影正映着这片人造天际线移动。里德先生匍匐向前——这一回，他倚仗的可不是一根橡皮警棍。他用拇指拨开勃朗宁手枪的保险，向那人影越逼越近。冷不防，那人影开口了。

“奥尔加，你父亲上哪儿去了？”

===Original===
It was Mrs. Burton, and Reeder showed his teeth in an unamused grin.

He did not hear the reply; it came from some recessed place, and the sound was muffled.

“Have they found that girl?”

Mr. Reeder listened breathlessly, craning his neck forward. The “No” was very distinct.

===Chinese===
是伯顿太太。里德龇了龇牙，那副笑容里没有半点笑意。

回答他听不见；那声音从某处凹进去的地方传来，闷闷的。

“他们找到那个姑娘了吗？”

里德先生屏息听着，脖子探得老长。那一声“没有”听得再清楚不过。

===Original===
Then Olga said something that he could not hear, and Mrs. Burton’s voice took on her old whine of complaint.

“What’s the use of hanging about? That’s the way you’ve always treated me. Nobody would think I was your mother. I wonder I’m not dead, the trouble I’ve had. I wouldn’t be surprised if he didn’t murder me some day, you mark my words!”

There came an impatient protest from the hidden girl.

===Chinese===
接着奥尔加说了句什么，他听不清；伯顿太太的嗓音又拿出了她那惯常的埋怨腔。

“干耗着有什么用？你们一向就是这么待我的。谁会想到我是你母亲。受了这些罪，我都奇怪自己怎么还没死。哪天他把我谋害了，我才不会奇怪呢，你记住我这话！”

藏在暗处的姑娘发出一声不耐烦的抗辩。

===Original===
“If you’re sick of it, what about me?” said Mrs. Burton shrilly. “Where’s Daver? It’s funny your father hasn’t said anything about Daver. Do you think he’s got into trouble?”

“Oh, damn Daver!”

Olga’s voice was distinct now. The passion and weariness in it would have made Mr. Reeder sorry for her in any other circumstances. He was too busy being sorry for Margaret Belman to worry about this fateful young woman.

She did not know, at any rate, that she was a widow. Mr. Reeder derived a certain amount of gruesome satisfaction from the superiority of his intelligence.

===Chinese===
“你要是烦了，那我呢？”伯顿太太尖声说，“达弗呢？你父亲对达弗的事一字不提，可真叫怪。你说，他是不是出事了？”

“哦，让达弗见鬼去吧！”

奥尔加的声音此刻听得分明了。里头的激愤与疲惫，换在别的情形下，本会让里德先生替她难过；可他正忙着为玛格丽特·贝尔曼难过，顾不上这个招灾惹祸的年轻女人。

无论如何，她还不知道自己已做了寡妇。里德先生从自己消息灵通一着上，得到几分阴惨惨的快意。

===Original===
“Where is he now? Your father, I mean?”

A pause, as she listened to a reply which was not intelligible to Mr. Reeder.

“On the boat? He’ll never get across. I hate ships, but a tiny little boat like that﻿—Why couldn’t he let us go, when we got him out? I begged and prayed him to﻿—we might have been in Venice or somewhere by now, doing the grand.”

The girl interrupted her angrily, and then Mrs. Burton apparently melted into the wall.

===Chinese===
“他眼下在哪儿？我是说你父亲？”

停了片刻，她在听一句里德先生听不真切的回答。

“在船上？他横竖过不了海。我恨透了船，可又只有那么小不点儿的一条小船——我们把他救出来的时候，他为什么就不能放我们走？我求了他，恳了他——不然这会儿我们早可以在威尼斯还是什么地方的，摆我们的阔气了。”

姑娘怒冲冲地打断了她；随后，伯顿太太竟像是化进了墙壁里。

===Original===
There was no sound of a closing door, but Mr. Reeder guessed what had happened. He came forward stealthily till he saw the bar of light on the opposite wall, and, reaching the door, listened. The voices were clear enough now; clearer because Mrs. Burton did most of the talking.

“Do you think your father knows?” She sounded rather anxious. “About Daver, I mean? You can keep that dark, can’t you? He’d kill me if he knew. He’s got such high ideas about you﻿—princes and dukes and such rubbish! If he hadn’t been mad, he’d have cleared out of this game years ago, as I told him, but he’d never take much notice of me.”

“Has anybody ever taken any notice of you?” asked the girl wearily. “I wanted the old man to let you go. I knew you would be useless in a crisis.”

===Chinese===
关门声是没有的，可里德先生猜到了是怎么回事。他悄悄上前，直到看见对面墙上那道亮光，走到门边，侧耳细听。这会儿话音已够清楚了；更清楚，是因为话多半都是伯顿太太在说。

“你说你父亲知道吗？”她听上去颇为着慌，“我是说达弗的事？这事你能瞒住不提，对不对？他要是知道了，会杀了我的。他对你的指望高着呢——亲王呀公爵呀，那一路的混账心思！他要不是疯了，多年前早该抽身离开这一行了，这话我跟他说过，可他从来不把我当回事。”

“又有谁把你当过回事？”姑娘倦倦地问，“我是要老头子放你走的。我就知道，一到紧要关头，你一点用也顶不上。”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder heard the sound of a sob. Mrs. Burton cried rather easily.

“He’s only stopping to get Reeder,” she whimpered. “What a fool trick! That silly old man! Why, I could have got him myself if I was wicked enough!”

From farther along the corridor came the sound of a quick step.

“There’s your father,” said Mrs. Burton, and Reeder pulled back the jacket of his Browning, sacrificing the cartridge that was already in the chamber in order that there should be no mistake.

===Chinese===
里德先生听见了啜泣声。伯顿太太的眼泪来得容易。

“他留下来，就为收拾里德。”她呜呜咽咽地说，“多蠢的一招！那个傻老头儿！哼，我要是心够狠，我自己也能要了他的命！”

走廊更远处传来一阵急促的脚步声。

“你父亲来了。”伯顿太太说。里德把勃朗宁的套筒往后一拉，宁可抛掉弹膛里已上着的那一发，也好万无一失。

===Original===
The footsteps stopped abruptly, and at the same time came a booming voice from the far end of the passage. It was asking a question. Evidently Flack turned back; his footsteps died away. Mr. Reeder decided that this was not his lucky day.

Lying full length on the ground, he could see John Flack clearly. A pressure of his finger, and the problem of this evil man would be settled eternally. It was a fond idea. Mr. Reeder’s finger closed around the trigger, but all his instincts were against killing in cold blood.

Somebody was coming from the other direction﻿—Gray, he guessed. He must go back and warn him. Coming to his feet, he went gingerly along the passage. The thing he feared happened. Gray must have seen him, for he called out in stentorian tones:

===Chinese===
脚步声戛然而止；与此同时，通道尽头传来一个轰雷般的嗓音，在问一句话。显然弗拉克折了回去；他的脚步声渐渐消逝。里德先生断定，今天不是他的吉利日子。

他直挺挺地伏在地上，约翰·弗拉克看得清清楚楚。手指一扣，这个恶人的问题便可一劳永逸地解决。那不过是一厢情愿的痴想。里德先生的手指扣上了扳机，可他全部的本能，都反对冷血杀人。

有个人正从另一头过来——他猜是格雷。他必须回去提醒他。他爬起身，蹑手蹑脚地沿通道走。他怕的事偏偏发生了。格雷准是瞧见了他，只见他扯开洪亮的嗓门喊道：

===Original===
“There’s nothing at the other end of the passage, Mr. Reeder﻿—”

“Hush, you fool!” snarled Reeder, but he guessed that the mischief was done.

He turned round, stooped again and looked. Old John Flack was standing at the entrance of the tunnel, his head bent. Somebody else had heard the detective’s voice. With a squeak of fear, Mrs. Burton had bolted into the passage, followed by her daughter﻿—an excursion which effectively prevented the use of the pistol, for the women completely masked the man whose destruction J. G. Reeder had privately sworn.

By the time he came to the end of the passage overlooking the great cave, the two women and Flack had disappeared.

===Chinese===
“通道那一头什么也没有，里德先生——”

“嘘，你这蠢货！”里德咬牙低喝，可他猜到，祸已经闯下了。

他转过身，重新伏下去张望。老约翰·弗拉克正立在隧道口，低着头。另有旁人也听见了侦探的嗓音。伯顿太太吓得一声尖叫，一头窜进通道，女儿紧跟在后——这一趟出洞，倒结结实实拦住了那支手枪的用处，因为两个女人把那个人遮了个严实，而 J. G. 里德私下立过誓要除掉的，正是那个人。

等他赶到俯临大洞窟的通道尽头，两个女人和弗拉克已不见了踪影。

===Original===
Mr. Reeder’s eyesight was of the keenest. He immediately located the boat, which was now floating on an even keel, and presently saw the three fugitives. They had descended to the water’s edge by a continuance of the long stairway which led to the roof and were making for the rocky platform which served as a pier for the craft.

Something smacked against the rock above his head. There was a shower of stone and dust, and the echoes of the explosion which followed were deafening.

“Firing from the boat,” said Mr. Reeder calmly. “You had better lie down, Gray﻿—I should hate to see so noisy a man as you reduced to compulsory silence.”

===Chinese===
里德先生的目力极其锐利。他一眼就找到了那条船——它此刻已平稳地浮在水上——不多时又望见了那三个逃亡者。他们是沿着通到屋顶的那道长楼梯的延伸段下到水边的，正朝那处充作码头之用的岩石平台走去。

什么东西啪的一声砸在他头顶的岩石上。石屑尘土簌簌落下，随后的爆炸回声震耳欲聋。

“是船上在开火。”里德先生平静地说，“你最好趴下，格雷——像你这么喧闹的一个人，眼看着被迫归于强制的沉默，我是很不忍的。”

===Original===
“I’m very sorry, Mr. Reeder,” said the penitent detective. “I had no idea﻿—”

“Ideas!” said Mr. Reeder accurately.

*Smack﻿—smack!*

One bullet struck to the left of him, the other passed exactly between him and Gray. He was lying down now, with a small projection of rock for cover.

===Chinese===
“真对不起，里德先生。”悔罪不已的侦探说，“我压根儿没有想到——”

“想法！”里德先生说，说得再准确不过。

*啪——啪！*

一颗子弹打在他左侧，另一颗不偏不倚，正从他跟格雷之间穿过。他此刻趴着，拿一小块凸出的岩石作掩护。

===Original===
Was Margaret on the boat? Even as the thought occurred to him, he remembered Mrs. Burton’s inquiry. As he saw another flash from the deck of the launch, he threw forward his hand. There was a double explosion which reverberated back from the arched roof, and although he could not see the effect of his shots, he was satisfied that the bullets fell on the launch.

It was pushing off from the side. The three Flacks were aboard. And now he heard the crackle and crash of her engine as her nose swung round to face the cave opening. And then into his eyes from the darkening sea outside the cave flashed a bright light that illuminated the rocky shelf on which he lay and threw the motor boat into relief.

The destroyer!

“Thank God for that!” said Mr. Reeder fervently.

===Chinese===
玛格丽特在船上吗？这念头刚一冒头，他就记起了伯顿太太的那句问话。他望见摩托艇甲板上又是一道火光，便把手往前一扬。两声连响，从拱顶上回荡下来；他虽瞧不见自己这双枪的效果，却确信子弹都落在了艇身上。

船正撑离岸边。弗拉克家三人都在艇上。这时艇头调转，对准洞口，他听得见发动机的噼啪轰鸣。紧接着，一束强光从洞外渐暗的海面上射进他的眼睛，照亮了他伏身的那道岩架，把那条摩托艇衬托得格外分明。

驱逐舰！

“为此感谢上帝！”里德先生满怀虔诚地说。

===Original===
Those on the motor launch had seen the vessel and guessed its portent. The launch swung round until its nose pointed to where the two detectives lay, and from her deck came a roar louder than ever. So terrible was the noise in that confined space that for a second Mr. Reeder was too dazed even to realize that he was lying half buried in a heap of debris, until Gray pulled him back to the passage.

“They’re using a gun﻿—a quick-firer!” he gasped.

Mr. Reeder did not reply. He was gazing fascinated, at something that was happening in the middle of the cave, where the water was leaping at irregular intervals from some mysterious cause.

Then he realized what was taking place. Great rocks, disturbed by the concussion, were falling from the roof. He saw the motor boat heel over to the right, swing round again, and head for the open. It was less than a dozen yards from the cave entrance when, with a sound that was indescribable, so terrific, so terrifying that J. G. Reeder was rooted to the spot, the entrance to the cave disappeared!

===Chinese===
摩托艇上的人望见了那艘军舰，也猜到了它意味着什么。艇身调转，艇头直指两位侦探伏身之处，甲板上轰出的声响比先前更猛。在那封闭的空间里，声浪恐怖之极，有那么一秒钟，里德先生懵得竟不知自己已半截埋在一堆碎石里，直到格雷把他拖回通道才算回过神。

“他们在用炮——速射炮！”他喘着气说。

里德先生没有作答。他正看得入迷——洞窟中央有什么事情正在发生，那里的水面不知出于何种神秘的原因，隔一阵便腾跳一次。

随后他明白了正在发生的事。大块的岩石被炮火的震荡震松，正纷纷从洞顶坠落。他看见摩托艇向右倾侧，又摆正艇身，朝洞外驶去。离洞口已不足十二码，就在这时，随着一声无法形容的巨响——骇人至极、恐怖至极，把 J. G. 里德钉在了原地——洞口不见了！

## XIX / 十九

===Original===
In an instant the air was filled with choking dust. Roar followed roar as the rocks continued to fall.

“The mouth of the cave has collapsed!” roared Reeder in the other’s ear. “And the subsidence hasn’t finished.”

His first instinct was to fly along the passage to safety, but somewhere in that awful void were two women. He switched on his light and crept gingerly back to the bench whence he had seen the catastrophe. But the rays of the lamp could not penetrate into the fog of dust for more than a few yards.

===Chinese===
刹那间，空气里充满呛人的尘土。岩石不断坠落，轰响接着轰响。

“洞口塌了！”里德冲着同伴的耳朵吼道，“塌陷还没有完！”

他的第一反应是顺着通道奔向安全地带，可那可怕的虚空里，某处还有两个女人。他拧亮手灯，小心翼翼地爬回那座石台——他正是在那里目睹了这场大难。可是灯光射不透尘雾，照不出几码远。

===Original===
Crawling forward to the edge of the platform, he strove to pierce the darkness. All about him, above, below, on either side, a terrible cracking and groaning was going on, as though the earth itself was in mortal pain. Rocks, large and small, were falling from the roof; he heard the splash of them as they struck the water. One fell on the edge of the platform with a terrific din and bounced into the pit below.

“For God’s sake, don’t stay here, Mr. Reeder. You will be killed.”

It was Gray shouting at him, but J. G. Reeder was already feeling his way toward the steps which led down to where the boat had been moored, and to which he guessed it would drift. He had to hold the lamp almost at his feet. Breathing had become a pain. His face was covered with powder; his eyes smarted excruciatingly; dust was in his mouth, his nose; but still he went on﻿—and was rewarded.

===Chinese===
他爬到平台边上，竭力想望穿黑暗。他的上下四周——上方、脚下、左右——处处都在发出可怕的爆裂与呻吟，仿佛大地本身正痛得死去活来。大大小小的岩石从洞顶坠落；他听得见石头砸进水里的溅响。一块砸在平台边上，声如巨雷，又弹进了下面的深坑。

“看在上帝分上，别待在这儿，里德先生。您会没命的。”

那是格雷在朝他喊，可 J. G. 里德已经在摸黑往台阶那边去了——台阶通往下头拴过小船的地方，他猜船会漂回那里去。他只好把灯提得几乎贴着脚。呼吸已成了苦刑。他满脸是灰，两眼刺痛难忍，嘴里鼻子里全是尘土；可他还是往前走——而且没有白走。

===Original===
Out of the dust mist came groping the ghostly figure of a woman. It was Olga Crewe.

He gripped her by the arm as she swayed, and pushed her against the rocky wall.

“Where is your mother?” he shouted.

She shook her head and said something; he lowered his ear to her mouth.

===Chinese===
尘雾里，一个女人的身影幽灵般摸索着走了出来。正是奥尔加·克鲁。

她身子一晃，他一把攥住她的胳膊，把她推靠在岩壁上。

“你母亲在哪儿？”他喊道。

她摇了摇头，说了句什么；他把耳朵凑到她嘴边。

===Original===
“… boat﻿ ﻿… great rock﻿ ﻿… killed.”

“Your mother?”

She nodded. Gripping her by the arm, he half led, half dragged her up the stairs. He found Gray waiting at the top. As easily as though she were a child, Mr. Reeder caught her up in his arms and staggered the distance that separated them from the mouth of the passage.

The pandemonium of splintering rock and crashing boulder was continuous. The air was thicker than ever. Gray’s lamp went out, and Mr. Reeder’s was almost useless. It seemed a thousand years before they pushed into the mouth of the tunnel. The air was filled with dust even here, but as they progressed it grew clearer, more breathable.

===Chinese===
“……小船……大石头……死了。”

“你母亲？”

她点了点头。他攥着她的胳膊，半搀半拽地把她弄上台阶。格雷正在上头等着。里德先生把她一抱而起，轻松得仿佛她是个孩子，踉踉跄跄地走完了他们与通道口之间的那段路。

碎石炸裂、巨砾翻滚的震耳喧嚣连绵不绝。空气比先前更浑浊了。格雷的灯灭了，里德先生的灯也差不多没了用处。他们挤进隧道口时，恍如隔了一千年。就连这里也尘土弥漫，但随着他们往前走，空气渐渐清明，也渐渐可以呼吸了。

===Original===
“Let me down﻿—I can walk,” said the husky voice of Olga Crewe, and Reeder lowered her gently to her feet.

She was very weak, but she could walk with the assistance that the two men afforded. They stopped at the entrance of the living room. Mr. Reeder wanted the lamp﻿—wanted more the water which she suggested would be found in that apartment.

A cold draught of spring water worked wonders on the girl too.

===Chinese===
“放我下来——我能走，”奥尔加·克鲁哑着嗓子说。里德轻轻把她放下，让她站住。

她虚弱极了，但靠两个男人搀扶，还能行走。他们在起居室门口停下。里德先生要灯——更要水，她说那房间里找得到水。

一口冰凉的泉水，对姑娘也发生了奇效。

===Original===
“I don’t know what happened,” she said, “but when the cave opening fell in, I think we drifted toward the stage﻿—we always called that place the stage. I was so frightened that I jumped immediately to safety, and I’d hardly reached the rock when I heard a most awful crash. I think a portion of the wall must have fallen on to the boat. I screamed, but hardly heard myself in the noise. This is punishment! This is punishment! I knew it would come! I knew it! I knew it!”

She covered her grimy face with her hands, and her shoulders shook in the excess of her sorrow and grief.

“There’s no sense in crying.” Mr. Reeder’s voice was sharp and stern. “Where is Miss Belman?”

===Chinese===
“我不知道出了什么事，”她说，“可洞口塌下来的时候，我想我们漂向了‘舞台’——我们一向管那个地方叫舞台。我吓坏了，立刻跳上了安全的地方，刚上岩石，就听见一声吓死人的巨响。我想多半是哪一段洞壁砸到了船上。我叫起来，可那么大的响动里，连我自己都几乎听不见。这是报应！这是报应！我就知道会有这一天！我就知道！我就知道！”

她双手捂住污黑的脸，肩膀因为悲伤和哀痛过了度，抖个不停。

“哭是没有用的。”里德先生的声音又尖又厉，“贝尔曼小姐在哪儿？”

===Original===
She shook her head.

“Where did she go?”

“Up the stairway﻿—Father said she escaped. Haven’t you seen her?” she asked, raising her tearful face as she began slowly to realize the drift of his question.

He shook his head, his narrowed eyes surveying her steadily.

===Chinese===
她摇了摇头。

“她去了哪里？”

“从楼梯上去了——父亲说她逃出去了。你们没有见到她吗？”她问。她慢慢咂摸出他这话的来意，抬起了那张泪脸。

他摇了摇头，眯细的眼睛定定地打量着她。

===Original===
“Tell me the truth, Olga Flack. Did Margaret Belman escape, or did your father﻿—?”

She was shaking her head before he had completed his sentence, and then, with a little moan, she drooped and would have fallen had not Gray supported her.

“We had better leave the questioning till later.”

===Chinese===
“跟我说实话，奥尔加·弗拉克。玛格丽特·贝尔曼是逃出去了，还是你父亲——？”

他这句话还没说完，她已经在摇头了；接着她低低呻吟了一声，身子一软，若不是格雷扶住，就要栽倒了。

“审问的事，最好留到以后再说。”

===Original===
Mr. Reeder seized the lamp from the table and went out into the tunnel. He had hardly passed the door before there was a crash, and the infernal noises which had come from the cave were suddenly muffled. He looked backward, but could see nothing. He guessed what had happened.

“There is a general subsidence going on in this mass of earth,” he said. “We shall be lucky if we get away.”

He ran ahead to the opening of the well, and a glad sight met his eyes. On the floor lay a coil of new rope, to which was attached a body belt. He did not see the thin wire which came down from the mouth of the well, but presently he detected a tiny telephone receiver that the engineers had lowered. This he picked up, and his hail was immediately answered.

===Chinese===
里德先生从桌上抓起灯，走出隧道。他刚过那道门，身后轰然一响，从洞里传来的那些地狱般的喧声顿时闷了下去。他回头去看，却什么也看不见。他猜到了出了什么事。

“这整团土石都在普遍沉陷，”他说，“我们能出去，就是万幸。”

他抢到井口，眼前一亮。地上放着一卷新绳，绳上系着一条安全腰带。他没看见从井口垂下来的那根细电线，但不多时便发现了工兵们吊下来的一只小电话听筒。他把它抓起，一招呼，立刻有人应声。

===Original===
“Are you all right? Up here it feels as if there’s an earthquake somewhere.”

Gray was fastening the belt about the girl’s waist, and after it was firmly buckled:

“You mustn’t faint﻿—do you understand, Miss Crewe? They will haul you up gently, but you must keep away from the side of the well.”

She nodded, and Reeder gave the signal. The rope grew taut, and presently the girl was drawn up out of sight.

===Chinese===
“你们没事吧？上头觉得像是什么地方在闹地震。”

格雷正把腰带往姑娘腰上系；系牢之后，他说道：

“你千万别晕过去——明白吗，克鲁小姐？他们会慢慢把你绞上去，但你务必躲开井壁。”

她点了点头，里德发出信号。绳子绷紧了，不多时，姑娘便被绞出了视线。

===Original===
“Up you go,” said Reeder.

Gray hesitated.

“What about you, sir?”

For answer Mr. Reeder pointed to the lowest rung, and, stooping, gripped the leg of the detective and, displaying an unsuspected strength, lifted him bodily so that he was able to grip the lower rung.

===Chinese===
“你上吧。”里德说。

格雷迟疑了。

“那您呢，长官？”

里德先生把手一指最下面那级横档，算是回答；随即俯下身去，攥住侦探的腿，使出一股无人料到的膂力，把他整个儿托了起来，好让他抓住下面那级横档。

===Original===
“Fix your belt to the rod, hold fast to the nearest rung, and I will climb up over you,” said Mr. Reeder.

Never an acrobat moved with greater nimbleness than this man who so loved to pose as an ancient. There was need for hurry. The very iron to which he was clinging trembled and vibrated in his grasp. The fall of stone down the well was continuous and constituted a very real danger. Some of the rungs, displaced by the earth tremors, came away in their grasp. They were less than halfway up when the air was filled with a sighing and a hissing that brought Reeder’s heart to his mouth.

Holding on to a rung of the ladder, he put out his hand. The opposite wall, which should have been well beyond his reach, was at less than arm’s length away!

===Chinese===
“把腰带扣在铁杆上，抓牢最近的横档，我从你身上爬过去。”里德先生说。

再灵巧的杂技演员，也不及这个最爱装成老人的角色手脚利落。事不宜迟。他攀着的那根铁件在他手里直打颤、直震颤。石头往井里落个不停，是实实在在的危险。有些横档被地颤震松了，一抓就脱。他们还没爬到一半，空气里忽然响起一片呼啸和嘶嘶声，惊得里德的心都提到了嗓子眼。

他一手攀住梯上的一级横档，伸出另一只手。对面那堵墙，本该远在他够不着的地方，此刻竟不到一臂之遥！

===Original===
The well was bulging under unexpected and tremendous stresses.

“Why have you stopped?” asked Gray anxiously.

“To scratch my head,” snarled Reeder. “Hurry!”

They climbed another forty or fifty feet, when from below came a rumble and a crash that set the whole well shivering.

===Chinese===
井筒正在出乎意料的巨大压力下鼓胀。

“您怎么停下了？”格雷焦急地问。

“挠挠我的头。”里德没好气地说，“快！”

他们又爬了四五十英尺，底下忽然传来一阵轰隆和巨响，震得整口井簌簌发抖。

===Original===
They could see starlight now, and distant objects, which might be heads, that overhung the mouth of the well.

“Hurry!” breathed J. G. Reeder, and moved as rapidly as his younger companion.

*Boom!*

The sound of a great gun, followed by a thunderous rumbling, surged up the well.

===Chinese===
他们这时望得见星光了，还望得见远处俯在井口上的一些影子，那兴许是人头。

“快！”J. G. 里德喘着气说，动作之利落，不输他那位年轻的同伴。

*轰！*

大炮般的一声巨响，夹着雷鸣似的隆隆声，从井底涌了上来。

===Original===
J. G. Reeder set his teeth. Please God, Margaret Belman had escaped from that hell﻿—or was mercifully dead!

Nearer and nearer to the mouth they climbed, and every step they took was accompanied by some new and awful noise from behind them. Gray’s breath was coming in gasps.

“I can’t go any farther!” croaked the detective. “My strength has gone!”

“Go on, you miserable﻿—” yelled Reeder, and whether it was the shock of hearing such violent language from so mild a man, or the discovery that he was within a few feet of safety, Gray took hold of himself, climbed a few more rungs, and then felt hands grip his arm and drag him to safety.

===Chinese===
J. G. 里德咬紧了牙关。求上帝保佑，玛格丽特·贝尔曼已经逃出了那座地狱——要么，就已蒙上天垂怜，死了个痛快！

他们越爬越近井口，而他们迈出的每一步，身后都有某种新的骇人的声响伴随。格雷喘得一口气接不上一口气。

“我爬不动了！”侦探哑着嗓子说，“我的力气完了！”

“接着爬，你这可恶的——”里德吼道。不知是因为从这么个温和的人嘴里听到这般狠话受了震动，还是发现自己离安全只有几英尺了，格雷定住心神，又爬了几级横档，随即觉得几只手攥住他的胳膊，把他拖到了安全之地。

===Original===
Mr. Reeder staggered out into the night air and blinked at the ring of men who stood in the light of a naphtha flare.

Was it his imagination, or was the ground swaying beneath his feet?

“Nobody else to come up, Mr. Reeder?”

The officer in charge of the engineers asked the question, and Reeder shook his head.

===Chinese===
里德先生踉跄着跨进夜气里，对着石蜡火把光下一圈的人直眨眼睛。

是他的幻觉呢，还是脚下的地面真的在摇晃？

“没有别人要上来了吧，里德先生？”

问话的是带队工兵的军官。里德摇了摇头。

===Original===
“Then all you fellows clear!” said the officer sharply. “Move toward the house and take the road to Siltbury﻿—the cliff is collapsing in sections.”

The flare was put out, and the soldiers, abandoning their apparatus, broke into a steady run toward Larmes Keep.

“Where is the girl﻿—Miss Crewe?” asked Reeder, suddenly remembering her.

===Chinese===
“那么，你们这帮人统统撤！”军官厉声说，“朝宅子那边走，取道去西尔特伯里——悬崖正一段一段地崩塌。”

火把熄灭了，士兵们丢下器械，迈开匀整的步子，朝拉尔姆宅跑去。

“那位姑娘——克鲁小姐——在哪儿？”里德问，忽然记起了她。

===Original===
“They’ve taken her to the house,” said Big Bill Gordon, who had made a mysterious appearance from nowhere. “And, Reeder, we have captured the gold convoy! The two men in charge were a fellow who calls himself Hothling and another named Dean﻿—I think you know their real names. Caught them just as the lorry was driving into the quarry cave. This means a big thing for you﻿—”

“To h﻿—﻿— with you and your big things!” stormed Reeder, in a fury. “What big things do I want, my man, but the big thing I have lost?”

Very wisely, Big Bill Gordon made no attempt to argue the matter.

===Chinese===
“他们把她送到宅子里去了，”大比尔·戈登（Big Bill Gordon）说。此人不知从哪里神出鬼没地冒了出来。“还有，里德，我们截住了那支黄金押运队！押车的两个人，一个自称霍斯林（Hothling），另一个叫迪恩（Dean）——他们的真名我想您是知道的。正赶上卡车往采石场洞里开，就把他们逮住了。这对你可是件大功——”

“叫你和你这件大功统统见——鬼去吧！”里德勃然大怒，“老兄，我丢了一件大事，除此之外，我还要什么大功？”

大比尔·戈登很知趣，没打算再争辩这件事。

===Original===
They found the banqueting hall crowded with policemen, detectives, and soldiers. The girl had been taken into Daver’s office, and here he found her in the hands of the three women servants who had been commandeered to run the establishment while the police were in occupation. The dust had been washed from her face, and she was conscious, but still in the half-stupefied condition in which Reeder had found her.

She stared at him for a long time as though she did not recognize him and was striving to recall that portion of her past in which he had figured. When she spoke, it was to ask a question.

“There is no news of﻿—Father?”

===Chinese===
他们发现宴会厅里挤满了警察、侦探和士兵。姑娘已被送进达弗的办公室，他在那里找到了她——三个女仆正围着她张罗。警察占领宅子期间，这几个女仆是被临时征来照管宅务的。她脸上的尘土已经洗去，人也清醒，却仍是里德找到她时那副半昏迷的样子。

她久久地瞪视着他，仿佛认不出他来，又仿佛在竭力追忆自己过往岁月中他曾出现的那一段。她开口时，问的是一句话。

“还没有——父亲的消息？”

===Original===
“None,” said Reeder, almost brutally. “I think it will be better for you, young lady, if he is dead.”

She nodded.

“He is dead,” she said with conviction. And then, rousing herself, she struggled to a sitting position and looked at the servants. Mr. Reeder interpreted that glance and sent the women away.

“I don’t know what you are going to do with me,” she said, “but I suppose I am to be arrested. I should be arrested, for I have known all that was happening, and I tried to lure you to your death.”

===Chinese===
“没有，”里德说，口气近乎残忍，“小姐，我想，他若是死了，对你反倒好些。”

她点了点头。

“他死了。”她口气笃定。随即她振作起来，挣扎着坐起身，朝女仆们望了一眼。里德先生会意这一眼，把女仆们都打发走了。

“我不知道你们打算怎么处置我，”她说，“不过我想，我是要被捕的。我也该被捕，因为出的事我全都知道，我还想把您诱到死地里去。”

===Original===
“In Bennett Street, of course,” said Mr. Reeder. “I recognized you the moment I saw you here﻿—you were the lady with the rouged face.”

She nodded and continued:

“Before you take me away, I wish you would let me have some papers that are in the safe,” she said. “They have no value to anybody but myself.”

He was curious enough to ask her what they were.

===Chinese===
“当然是在贝内特街，”里德先生说，“我在这里一见到你，就认出了你——你就是那位搽着胭脂的女士。”

她点了点头，接着说下去：

“在你们带我走之前，我想求您让我把保险柜里的几件东西拿去，”她说，“它们对谁都无价值，只对我自己有用。”

他好奇心切，问了问那是些什么东西。

===Original===
“They were letters﻿—in the big, flat box that is locked. Even Daver did not dare open that. You see, Mr. Reeder”﻿—her breath came more quickly﻿—“before I met my﻿—husband, I had a little romance﻿—the sort of romance that a young girl has when she is innocent enough to dream and has enough faith in God to hope. Is my husband arrested?” she asked suddenly.

Mr. Reeder was silent for a moment. Sooner or later, she must know the truth, and he had an idea that this awful truth would not cause her very much distress.

“Your husband is dead,” he said.

===Chinese===
“是信——在那只上锁的大扁匣子里。连达弗都没敢打开过它。您瞧，里德先生”——她的呼吸急促起来——“在遇见我——我丈夫之前，我有过一小段罗曼史——那种天真女孩子的罗曼史，那时她天真得还敢做梦，又对上帝怀着足够的信心，还敢盼望。我丈夫被捕了吗？”她忽然问。

里德先生沉默了片刻。她迟早得知道真相，而他有一种感觉：这个可怕的真相不会让她太过伤心。

“你的丈夫死了。”他说。

===Original===
Her eyes opened wider.

“Did my father﻿—”

“Your father killed him﻿—I suppose so. I am afraid I was the cause. Coming back to find Margaret Belman, I told Daver all that I knew about your marriage. Your father must have been hiding behind the panelling and heard.”

“I see,” she said simply. “Of course it was Father who killed him﻿—I knew that would happen as soon as he learned the truth. Would you think I was heartless if I said I am glad? I don’t think I am really glad﻿—I’m just relieved. Will you get the box for me?”

===Chinese===
她的眼睛睁大了。

“是不是我父亲——”

“是你父亲杀了他——我想是的。恐怕起因还在我。回来寻找玛格丽特·贝尔曼的时候，我把我知道的有关你们婚事的情形，都告诉了达弗。你父亲那时多半正藏在嵌板后面，听见了。”

“我明白了，”她淡淡地说，“当然是他杀的——我一知道他获知了真相，就知道会有这一步。倘若我说我心里高兴，您会不会觉得我没心肝？其实我想我也不是真高兴——我只是松了口气。您肯替我把匣子取来吗？”

===Original===
She put her hand down her blouse and pulled out a gold chain at the end of which were two keys.

“The first of these is the key of the safe. If you want to see the﻿—the letters, I will show them to you, but I would rather not.”

At that moment he heard hurrying footsteps in the passage outside; the door was pulled open and a young officer of engineers appeared.

“Excuse me, sir,” he said, “but Captain Merriman thinks we ought to abandon this house. I’ve got out all the servants, and we’re rushing them down to Siltbury.”

===Chinese===
她把手伸进衣襟，掏出一条金链，链子头上挂着两把钥匙。

“这两把里头，第一把是开保险柜的。您要是想看看——那些信——我可以拿给您看，不过我不太愿意。”

正在这时，他听见外面走廊里响起急促的脚步声；门被拉开，一名年轻的工兵军官走了进来。

“对不起，先生，”他说，“梅里曼上尉（Captain Merriman）的意思，我们应当放弃这座宅子。我已把仆人全都撤出去，正赶送他们下山去西尔特伯里。”

===Original===
Reeder stooped down and drew the girl to her feet.

“Take this lady with you,” he said, and, to Olga: “I will get your box, and I may not﻿—I am not quite sure﻿—ask you to open it for me.”

He waited till the officer had gone, and added:

“Just now I am feeling rather﻿—tender toward young lovers. That is a concession which an old lover may make to youth.”

===Chinese===
里德俯下身，把姑娘搀了起来。

“把这位小姐带上。”他说，随后又对奥尔加说：“我会去取你的匣子，我也可能——我说不准——要请你替我打开它。”

他等军官走远，又添了一句：

“眼下我对年轻的恋人们，心里正觉得格外——温软。这是一个老情人可以给青春让的一点路。”

===Original===
His voice had grown husky. There was something in his face that brought the tears to her eyes.

“Was it﻿—not Margaret Belman?” she asked in a hushed voice, and she knew before he answered that she had guessed well.

Tragedy dignified this strange-looking man, so far past youth, yet holding the germ of youth in his heart. His hand fell gently on her shoulder.

“Go, my dear,” he said. “I will do what I can for you﻿—perhaps I can save you a great deal of unhappiness.”

===Chinese===
他的嗓音已经发哑。他脸上有一种东西，惹得她的眼泪涌了上来。

“您说的——是玛格丽特·贝尔曼吧？”她压着嗓子问。不等他回答，她已知道自己猜中了。

悲剧使这个相貌古怪的人显得庄严：他的青春早已远去，心里却还揣着青春的种子。他的手轻轻落在她的肩上。

“去吧，亲爱的，”他说，“我会尽我所能帮你——或许，能让你省去许多不幸。”

===Original===
He waited until she had gone, then strolled into the deserted lounge. What an eternity had passed since he had sat there, munching his toast and drinking his cup of tea, with an illustrated newspaper on his knees!

The place in the half gloom seemed full of ancient ghosts. The House of Tears! These walls had held sorrows more poignant, more hopeless than his.

He went to the panelled wall and rubbed his finger down the little scar in the wood that a thrown knife had made and smiled at the comparative triviality of that offence.

===Chinese===
他等她走了，才踱进空无一人的休息厅。自从他坐在那里、膝上摊着一份画报、嚼着吐司、喝着那杯茶，中间竟像隔了一世！

半明半暗里，这地方仿佛挤满了古老的幽灵。眼泪之宅！这几堵墙里盛过的悲哀，比他的更深切、更无望。

他走到嵌板墙前，手指顺着木头上那道小小的疤——那是掷出的小刀留下的——一路抚下，又为那桩罪过相比之下何其轻微而微笑了。

===Original===
He had reason to remember the circumstances without the dramatic reminder which nature gave. Suddenly the floor beneath him swayed, and the two lights went out. He guessed that the earth tremors were responsible for the snapping of wires, and he hurried into the vestibule and had passed from the house when he remembered Olga Crewe’s request.

The lantern was still hanging about his neck. He switched it on and went back to the safe and inserted the key. As he did so, the house swayed backward and forward like a drunken man. The smashing of glass, the crash of overturned wardrobes startled him so that he almost fled with his mission unperformed. He even hesitated; but a promise was a promise to J. G. Reeder. He put the key in again, turned the lock, and pulled open one of the great doors﻿—and Margaret Belman fell into his arms!

===Chinese===
用不着大自然来这么一出戏剧性的提醒，他也记得当时的种种情形。突然，脚下的地板晃动起来，两盏灯一齐熄灭。他猜是地颤绷断了电线，便急匆匆走进门厅；他人已经出了宅子，才猛地想起奥尔加·克鲁的托付。

提灯还挂在他脖子上。他把它拧亮，走回保险柜前，把钥匙插进锁孔。就在这时，宅子像醉汉似的来回摇晃起来。玻璃的碎裂声、衣柜翻倒的轰响吓得他心惊胆战，险些丢下没办完的差事逃了出去。他甚至犹豫了一下；可对 J. G. 里德来说，承诺就是承诺。他又把钥匙插好，拧转锁芯，拉开一扇大柜门——玛格丽特·贝尔曼倒进了他的怀里！

## XX / 二十

===Original===
He stood, holding the half-swooning girl, peering into the face he could only see by the reflected light of his lantern, and then suddenly the safe fell back from him without warning, leaping a gaping cavern.

He lifted her in his arms, ran across the vestibule into the open air. Somebody shouted his name in the distance, and he ran blindly toward the voice. Once he stumbled over a great crack that had appeared in the earth, but managed to recover himself, though he was forced to release his grip of the girl.

She was alive﻿—breathing﻿—her breath fanned his cheek and gave him new strength.

The sound of falling walls behind him; immense, hideous roarings and groanings; thunder of sliding chalk and rock and earth﻿—he heard only the breathing of his burden, felt only the faint beating of her heart against his breast.

“Here you are!”

===Chinese===
他站在那里，搀着半昏迷的姑娘，凑近端详那张脸——那脸唯有借着灯笼反射的光才看得见。冷不防，那口保险柜毫无征兆地朝旁一荡，露出一道豁然张口的洞穴。

他将她抱进怀里，奔过门厅，冲到露天之下。远处有人喊他的名字，他便朝着那声音没头没脑地奔去。半路上他被地上一道新裂开的大缝绊了一跤，好歹稳住了身子，只是不得不松开攥着姑娘的手。

她还活着——还有呼吸——她呼出的气息拂上他的面颊，给了他新的气力。

身后是墙壁倒塌的声响，是巨大骇人的轰鸣与呻吟，是白垩、岩石和泥土倾泻而下的雷霆——他却只听得见怀中人的呼吸，只感觉得到她的心贴着自己胸口的微弱搏动。

“可找到你们了！”

===Original===
Somebody lifted Margaret Belman from his arms. A big soldier pushed him into a wagon, where he sprawled at full length, breathless, more dead than alive, by the side of the woman he loved; and then, with a whirr of wheels, the ambulance sped down the hillside toward safety. Behind him, in the darkness, the House of Tears shivered and crackled, and the work of ancient masons vanished piecemeal, tumbling over new cliffs, to be everlastingly engulfed and hidden from the sight of man.

Dawn came and showed, to an interested party that had travelled by road and train to the scene of the great landslide, one gray wall, standing starkly on the edge of a precipice. A portion of the wrecked floor still adhered to the ruins, and on that floor the bloodstained bed where Old Man Flack had laid his murdered servant.

The story that Olga Flack told the police, which appears in the official records of the place, was not exactly the same as the story she told to Mr. Reeder that afternoon when, at his invitation, she came to the flat in Bennett Street. Mr. Reeder, minus his glasses and his general air of respectability which his vanished side whiskers had so enhanced, was at some disadvantage.

===Chinese===
有人把玛格丽特·贝尔曼从他怀里接了过去。一名高大的士兵把他推进一辆救护马车，他在车里直挺挺地摊开四肢，气喘吁吁，半死不活，身边躺着他心爱的女人；随后车轮呼呼一转，车子顺着山坡朝安全地带疾驰而下。在他身后的黑暗里，“眼泪之宅”簌簌发抖、噼啪崩裂，古代石匠的手笔一块块地湮灭，翻滚下新生的悬崖，被永远吞没，从此不见天日。

天亮了，向那批由公路和铁路赶来看大塌方的兴致勃勃的看客们展示出来的，是赫然矗立在悬崖边上的一堵灰墙。一段坍毁的地板还附着在废墟上，地板上放着那张血迹斑斑的床——老弗拉克曾把他那被害的仆人停放在那上面。

奥尔加·弗拉克对警察讲的那番话——如今已载入当地的官方记录——与那天下午她应里德先生之邀来到贝内特街的公寓时对他讲的那番，并不尽相同。里德先生这回没了眼镜，颊须也剃了个精光，那副曾靠颊须大大增色的正派体面的气度随之而去，多少有些吃亏。

===Original===
“Yes, I think Ravini was killed,” she said, “but you are wrong in supposing that I brought him to my room at the request of my father. Ravini was a very quick-witted man and recognized me. He came to Larmes Keep because he”﻿—she hesitated﻿—“well, he was rather fond of Miss Belman. He told me this, and I was rather amused. At that time I did not know his name, although my husband did, and I certainly did not connect him with my father’s arrest. He revealed his identity, and I suppose there was something in my attitude, or something I said, which recalled the schoolgirl he had met years before. The moment he recognized me as John Flack’s daughter, he also recognized Larmes Keep as my father’s headquarters.

“He began to ask me questions: whether I knew where the Flack million, as he called it, was hidden. And of course I was horrified, for I knew why Daver had allowed him to come.

“My father had recently escaped from Broadmoor, and I was worried sick for fear he knew the trick that Daver had played. I wasn’t normal, I suppose, and I came near to betraying my father, for I told Ravini of his escape. Ravini did not take this as I had expected; he rather overrated his own power, and was very confident. Of course, he did not know that Father was practically in the house, that he came up from the cave every night﻿—”

“The real entrance to the cave was through the safe in the vestibule?” said Mr. Reeder. “That was an ingenious idea. I must confess that the safe was the last place in the world I should have considered.”

“My father had it put there twenty years ago,” she said. “There always was an entrance from the centre of the Keep to the caves below, many of which were used as prisons or as burying places by the ancient owners of Larmes.”

===Chinese===
“是的，我想拉维尼是被杀了，”她说，“不过你要是以为，是我奉父亲之命把他带进我房里的，那你就想岔了。拉维尼是个脑筋极快的人，认出了我。他到拉尔姆宅（“拉尔姆”是法语“眼泪”的意思）来，是因为他”——她迟疑了一下——“嗯，他相当倾心于贝尔曼小姐。这是他亲口对我说的，我当时还觉得挺有趣。那时我并不知道他的姓名，虽然我丈夫知道；我也绝没有把他同我父亲的被捕扯到一起。是他自己亮明了身份，而我猜想，必是我的某种神色，或者我说过的什么话，唤起了他多年前遇见的那个女学生的记忆。他一认出我是约翰·弗拉克的女儿，也就认出了拉尔姆宅是我父亲的总部。”

“他开始向我发问：问我是否知道他所谓的‘弗拉克百万’藏在什么地方。我自然吓坏了，因为我明白达弗为什么放他进来。”

“我父亲那时刚从布罗德莫尔逃出来不久，我愁得要死，就怕他已经识破达弗耍的那套把戏。我想我那阵子不太正常，差一点儿就出卖了父亲，因为我把越狱的事告诉了拉维尼。拉维尼的反应全不像我预料的那样；他未免把自己估量得太高，又信心十足。当然，他不知道父亲其实差不多就在宅子里，不知道他每天夜里都从洞里上来——”

“那洞真正的入口，就是门厅里那口保险柜？”里德先生说，“这主意真巧。我必须承认，保险柜是全世界我最不可能疑心的地方。”

“那是我父亲二十年前叫人安在那儿的，”她说，“主堡中心通到下面洞穴的入口向来就有，那些洞穴有不少曾被拉尔姆古代的主人们用作牢房或葬身之地。”

===Original===
